**Evidence.** verify-orca-overlay.mjs:259-261: `if (!isGitTop(orcaDir)) { notes.push(`${NAME}: SKIP -- orca/ is not a fetched git checkout, ...`); } else { ...blob / replay ... }` and render() returns code 0 when fails is empty. verify-orca-pane-routes.mjs:548: `if (!existsSync(join(REPO_ROOT, REL.tabTypes))) { console.log(`...SKIP -- orca/ is not fetched`); process.exit(0); }` placed before the --self-test branch. verify-orca-pin.mjs:191-193: `if (!io.exists('orca')) { notes.push('orca/ absent, checkout step skipped'); }`. verify.yml steps: generate, fetch-upstream, yarn build, verify-platform.sh --quick, with no fetch-orca.

**Suggested fix.** Add `bash scripts/fetch-orca.sh` (clone at the pin, a verified shallow clone) to verify.yml before the --quick step. Alternatively make these rows FAIL, not SKIP, when CI=true and orca/ is absent. Correct the "none is silently skipped" comment in verify.yml.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** verify.yml runs generate, fetch-upstream.sh, yarn build and verify-platform.sh --quick; no fetch-orca.sh anywhere in it (fetch-orca.sh appears only in release.yml and release-train.yml). The quick rows orca-overlay, orca-pin and orca-pane-routes are registered in verify-platform.sh. In the pane-routes script (line 548) the SKIP exits 0 before the self-test dispatch. verify-orca-overlay.mjs:259-260 turns a missing orca/ into a note, so blob, replay and shadowing steps do not run. verify-orca-pin.mjs:191-193 skips the checkout step when orca/ is absent. The workflow comment 'none is silently skipped' is wrong for these rows, though the SKIP is printed. The hermetic rebase-orca-self-test still runs but does not check the real patches.

*Checked at:* No guard in .github/workflows/verify.yml; scripts/verify-orca-overlay.mjs:259; scripts/verify-orca-pane-routes.mjs:547-548; scripts/verify-orca-pin.mjs:191

### harness-static-structure-02: verify-platform.sh counts every exit-0 SKIP as PASS, so a check that did not run is reported as green

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-platform.sh:6986`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** The driver derives a row's result only from the exit status of the check, and SUMMARY records "$label: PASS". No row output is inspected for SKIP. Every named SKIP in the assigned shard therefore reads as PASS in the summary and in --gate.
SKIP exits exist in: orca-build-shape (no built tree), orca-slotless-start (no built orca/ or PowerBrowser, or an installed Orca), orca-pane-entry (no pb-panes bundle), orca-pane-css, orca-overlay-built, orca-pane-groups, orca-pane-routes, orca-editor-routes, orca-no-pb-add-project, orca-terminal-keys (typescript absent) and orca-pin (both pin keys absent).
The known orca-agent-status-live instance is the same defect.

**Scenario.** On a machine or CI job without a built orca/ or without theia/node_modules/typescript, every one of these rows prints SKIP and exits 0. The summary shows PASS, and --gate reports "every registered check is green".

**Preconditions.** Any run where an input of a SKIP-capable row is absent.

**Evidence.** verify-platform.sh ~6972-6990: `wait "$CURRENT_CHECK_PID" || row_ok=0` ... `if [ "$row_ok" -eq 1 ]; then SUMMARY+=("$label: PASS")`. verify-orca-build-shape.mjs:157 `if (r.skip) return { code: 0, lines: [`SKIP -- ...`] }`.

**Suggested fix.** Let the driver capture each row's output and report SKIP when it contains a line matching `^<label>: SKIP`. Have --gate refuse any SKIP not on the WINDOWS.md known-open list. Alternatively give SKIP-capable checks a distinct exit code (for example 77) that the driver maps to SKIP.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** verify-platform.sh ~6972-6990 sets row_ok only from the wait exit status (and the orcad leak check) and records '$label: PASS'. Output is not inspected for SKIP. SKIP paths exit 0 (e.g. verify-orca-build-shape.mjs:157, pane-routes:547-548, no-pb-add-project:339). So a not-run row reads PASS in the summary and in --gate.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-platform.sh:6972-6990 has no SKIP handling; scripts/verify-orca-build-shape.mjs:157

### harness-live-security-02: orca-browser-agent-live and orca-agent-mark-live exit 0 (counted PASS) when tree markers or build outputs are missing, and when the decoy clause skips

- **Severity:** medium (reported as high)
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs:56`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** The driver counts a row as PASS on exit status 0 alone (verify-platform.sh:6978-6986). prerequisites() returns a SKIP, and both rows then exit 0, when any of these hold: a regex `const AGENT_METHODS =` does not match PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs; theia/extensions/orca-browser does not exist; the built host bundle orca/out/web/assets/pb-panes/pb-browser-host.js is missing; or any tabsPrerequisites condition holds (no orca-panes dir, no Theia build, no orcad.js, no panes bundle). These are no longer lane-merge states. A rename of the constant, or an Orca build that fails to emit the host bundle, turns the agent-ability rows green. Clause (f) has a separate skip: when an installed Orca exists at /usr/bin, /usr/local/bin or /opt/Orca, the row prints 'PASS -- ... (f) SKIP' and exits 0. The self-test even asserts that a skipped (f) yields no failures.

**Scenario.** A refactor renames AGENT_METHODS to AGENT_COMMANDS in PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs, or scripts/build-orca.sh stops copying pb-browser-host.js. The full run or --gate runs orca-browser-agent-live and orca-agent-mark-live. Both print 'SKIP -- lane p66-03 not merged' or 'lane p66-01 not merged or Orca not built' and exit 0. The summary shows PASS. The checks covering tab scope, container isolation, downloads, protocols, clipboard and the agent mark do not run.

**Preconditions.** A rename of AGENT_METHODS or of the extension folder, a missing host bundle (see the failure-page finding), or a system-wide Orca install for clause (f).

**Evidence.** scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs:56 `if (!/\bconst AGENT_METHODS\s*=/.test(api)) return { skip: ... }`
:57 `if (!existsSync(join(root, ORCA_BROWSER_REL))) return { skip: ...`
:60 `if (!existsSync(join(root, HOST_BUNDLE_REL))) return { skip: ...`
scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:1001 `if (pre.skip) { console.log(`${NAME}: SKIP -- ${pre.skip}`); return 0; }`
scripts/verify-orca-agent-mark-live.mjs:115 same
scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:911 `if (stock.skip) obs.f = { skip: stock.skip };` and 1012-1016 exit 0 with '(f) SKIP'
scripts/verify-orca-hidden-electron.mjs:225 skip when an installed Orca exists
scripts/verify-platform.sh:6978 `wait "$CURRENT_CHECK_PID" || row_ok=0`

**Suggested fix.** Remove the lane-merge skips, since the lanes are merged. When a merged-tree marker or build output is missing, return FAIL with a next step. Give a skip a distinct exit status (for example 77) and make the driver record it as SKIP, not PASS, so --gate requires an exclusion entry. When the decoy stock half skips, report clause (f) as FAIL or as a distinct non-PASS state.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** I traced each step myself.

1. prerequisites() in scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs:53-64 returns a skip in four cases: the regex /\bconst AGENT_METHODS\s*=/ does not match, theia/extensions/orca-browser is absent, the Firefox binary is absent, or orca/out/web/assets/pb-panes/pb-browser-host.js is absent. It then defers to orca-tabs-live.mjs's prerequisites(), which also skips when orca-panes, the Theia build, orcad.js or the panes bundle is missing.
2. verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:1001 and verify-orca-agent-mark-live.mjs:115 print SKIP and return 0 when that happens.
3. verify-platform.sh:6976-6986 decides a row only by its wait status, plus the leak leg. Nothing after line 7000 treats SKIP output differently, and --gate adds only the WINDOWS.md exclusions. So a skipped row shows as "PASS" in the summary. Both rows are registered at verify-platform.sh:6894-6895.
4. Clause (f): at line 911, when the stock half skipped (verify-orca-hidden-electron.mjs:225 skips if an installed Orca exists at the derived system paths), obs.f is set to a skip. The row then prints the SKIP line and still exits 0 with "(f) SKIP" in its PASS line. The self-test at line 1177 checks that a skipped (f) gives zero failures and exactly one named skip.

All of this matches the finding.

Two points lower the severity from high.

First, the AGENT_METHODS rename scenario does not turn the whole run green. Two rows in the --quick set (which starts at verify-platform.sh:3659) fail on it:
- orca-browser-abilities (registered at 4060) fails at verify-orca-browser-abilities.mjs:252 when AGENT_METHODS is missing while the tab-scope file exists.
- orca-browser-tab-scope (registered at 4079) reports a broken instrument at verify-orca-browser-tab-scope.mjs:387.
The two live rows still show PASS, but the run as a whole goes red.

Second, a host bundle that fails to build does not produce this state. build-orca.sh and build-orca-browser-host.sh both run under set -euo pipefail (build-orca.sh:39, build-orca-browser-host.sh:22), so a failed build stops the script. The state appears only if step 10 is removed from build-orca.sh or its output stops being written. In that case orca-browser-host-bundle also skips with exit 0 (header lines 35-36), so the path to an all-green run is real.

The checks can go red when their prerequisites hold. That rules out "a security check that cannot go red". This is a harness gap: a named SKIP counts as PASS in full and --gate runs, and nothing requires these rows to actually run on a tree that is merged and built. That is a gap with a concrete path (removing build step 10), which puts it at medium. The installed-Orca skip of (f) is deliberate and printed by name, but it still counts as a pass on hosts with a system-wide Orca install. No such install exists on this host.

*Checked at:* scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs:56-63 (skip conditions); scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:1001 and :911/:1015 (exit 0, (f) SKIP inside PASS); scripts/verify-orca-agent-mark-live.mjs:115; scripts/verify-platform.sh:6978-6986 (exit status alone decides PASS). Partial mitigation of the rename case: scripts/verify-orca-browser-abilities.mjs:252 and scripts/verify-orca-browser-tab-scope.mjs:387 (quick rows at verify-platform.sh:4060/4079). The host bundle is not otherwise guarded: scripts/verify-orca-browser-host-bundle.mjs:35 also skips.

### harness-live-security-03: Other live rows exit 0 on SKIP and the driver counts them PASS (credential store, no remote port, service, failure page, snapshot, agent status)

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-credential-store-live.mjs:120`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** Apart from the known agent-status sign-in case, these exit-0 SKIP paths exist in the assigned rows. verify-orca-credential-store-live skips with no binary, no orcad.js, or no theia/node_modules/typescript; the last is plausible when PB_FIREFOX_BIN points at another checkout. verify-no-remote-agent-port-live skips the live row, and its --self-test skips the live half but prints PASS, both with no binary or orcad. verify-orca-service-live and verify-orca-failure-page-live skip with no binary or Orca build; failure-page also skips when the orca-failure-copy.ts path moves. verify-orca-browser-snapshot-live skips with no binary, orcad or fixture manifest. The quick row orca-snapshot-fixtures skips without orca/package.json, which is every CI job that does not fetch orca/. verify-orca-agent-status-live skips with no claude on PATH, and with any seedClaudeLogin refusal: no sign-in, an API-key setup, or under 60 min to expiry. All of these print SKIP and exit 0, and verify-platform.sh records PASS. Three of these rows guard security requirements: the credential store (RUN-09), no remote agent port (D-11) and the failure classes.

**Scenario.** A developer runs `scripts/verify-platform.sh --gate` from a worktree whose theia/node_modules is not installed, with PB_FIREFOX_BIN pointing at the main checkout's binary. orca-credential-store-live prints 'SKIP -- theia/node_modules/typescript is not installed' and the summary shows 'orca-credential-store-live: PASS'. The plaintext-override and temporary-profile refusals are never exercised. In CI without orca/, orca-snapshot-fixtures is always PASS.

**Preconditions.** The named precondition holds at run time: no typescript, no claude on PATH, a sign-in near expiry, no orca/ in CI, and so on.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-orca-credential-store-live.mjs:116-120 three `console.log(...SKIP...); return 0;`
scripts/verify-no-remote-agent-port-live.mjs:286 `if (skip) { console.log(`${NAME}: SKIP -- ${skip}`); return 0; }` and 349-352 self-test 'PASS -- ... live half skipped' exit 0
scripts/verify-orca-service-live.mjs:208-209
scripts/verify-orca-failure-page-live.mjs:522,535-537
scripts/verify-orca-browser-snapshot-live.mjs:238,318-321,487 (skip leaves code 0)
scripts/verify-orca-agent-status-live.mjs:198-200,395 (skip leaves code 0)
scripts/verify-platform.sh:6985 `SUMMARY+=("$label: PASS")` on exit 0

**Suggested fix.** Use one skip protocol everywhere: a distinct exit code that the driver records as SKIP, and that --gate refuses unless a ledger exclusion names it. In the full tier, which runs after the build, turn 'not built' into FAIL. The orca-snapshot-fixtures quick row should FAIL, not SKIP, wherever orca/ is expected.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** Every cited SKIP path exists and exits 0. credential-store-live returns 0 at lines 116, 117 and 120 (loadTypescript at verify-orca-credential-store.mjs:77-78 returns undefined when require fails). no-remote-agent-port-live returns 0 at 286, and its self-test prints 'PASS ... live half skipped' and returns 0 at 349-352. service-live returns 0 at 208-209. failure-page-live exits 0 at 534-537, and skipReason (518-523) also skips when COPY_REL is missing. browser-snapshot-live's live() returns {skip} at 304-307 and the entry point at 487 leaves code 0. The --fixtures mode skips without orca/package.json (runFixtures). CI's verify.yml runs --quick with no fetch-orca.sh step (only fetch-upstream and the Theia build at lines 79-93), so orca-snapshot-fixtures always passes there. agent-status-live skips with no claude on PATH and on any seedClaudeLogin refusal (firefox-bidi.mjs:228, 231, 233, 238), and 395 leaves code 0. In verify-platform.sh, a node row that exits 0 is recorded as '$label: PASS' unless the leak leg fires. Gate mode only reads ': FAIL' summary lines (extract_failed_labels), so a SKIP is never seen. No guard turns a SKIP into a red.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-platform.sh run loop: `wait "$CURRENT_CHECK_PID" || row_ok=0` ... `SUMMARY+=("$label: PASS")`; .github/workflows/verify.yml:79-93 has no fetch-orca step before `verify-platform.sh --quick`; scripts/verify-orca-browser-snapshot-live.mjs runFixtures `if (!existsSync(join(REPO_ROOT, 'orca', 'package.json'))) return { skip: ... }`

### harness-live-security-04: failure-page-live moves the built pb-browser-host.js aside and does not restore it when ended by SIGTERM, which makes the agent-browser rows SKIP and PASS

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-failure-page-live.mjs:486`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** Induction 5 renames the shared build output orca/out/web/assets/pb-panes/pb-browser-host.js to *.pb-aside and restores it in a finally block. The script calls liveEnv(), which installs liveHome()'s SIGTERM and SIGHUP handlers. Those handlers dispose the scratch HOME and re-raise the signal with the default disposition, so the finally block never runs. verify-platform.sh stops a running check with SIGTERM to its process group (setsid row, cleanup). The host bundle stays moved aside. The next orca-browser-agent-live and orca-agent-mark-live runs then hit prerequisites()' `HOST_BUNDLE_REL is absent` SKIP and pass. They run before failure-page in registry order (lines 6894-6895 against 6907). The next failure-page run then fails on ENOENT and only after that restores the file. A concurrent unlocked run (see the lock finding) also sees the file missing during induction 5.

**Scenario.** The owner interrupts a --gate run (Ctrl-C, or the driver's group SIGTERM) during failure-page launch 3. pb-browser-host.js is left as pb-browser-host.js.pb-aside. On the re-run, orca-browser-agent-live prints 'SKIP -- lane p66-01 not merged or Orca not built (...pb-browser-host.js is absent)' and exits 0, so it counts as PASS. The live browser host is also broken for manual use until the next failure-page run restores the file.

**Preconditions.** The row is ended by a signal during induction 5, or another live row runs at the same time.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-orca-failure-page-live.mjs:486 `renameSync(join(REPO_ROOT, HOST_BUNDLE_REL), hostAside);`
:509 `if (existsSync(hostAside)) renameSync(hostAside, join(REPO_ROOT, HOST_BUNDLE_REL));` (finally only)
scripts/lib/firefox-bidi.mjs:155-163 signal handler: `disposeHome(home); process.off(sig, onSignal); process.kill(process.pid, sig);`
scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs:60 skip on missing host bundle

**Suggested fix.** Do not mutate the shared build tree. Induce browser-not-attached by pointing a pref or the orca root at a scratch copy of out/web without the host bundle. Otherwise register a synchronous restore in the SIGTERM, SIGINT and SIGHUP path, and restore any stale *.pb-aside at the start of every row that reads HOST_BUNDLE_REL. In any case, make a missing host bundle FAIL in prerequisites().

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** Line 486 renames orca/out/web/assets/pb-panes/pb-browser-host.js to *.pb-aside. It is restored only at 495 (inside the session) or in the finally at 509. failure-page calls liveEnv (lines 306, 322, and through withShell/withFirefoxPage), so liveHome() installs handlers for SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP (firefox-bidi.mjs:155-163). No other SIGTERM listener exists in pb-relaunch or firefox-bidi: the only other process.on calls are SIGINT at firefox-bidi.mjs:585 and 764, and that handler also ends with process.exit(130), which skips finally blocks. On SIGTERM the liveHome handler disposes the HOME, removes itself and re-raises with the default disposition, so the process dies without running the finally. verify-platform.sh cleanup() sends `kill -- -$CURRENT_CHECK_PID` to the setsid group, and that group includes the flock child and the re-exec'd node (spawnSync children share the group). prerequisites() in orca-browser-live.mjs:60 then skips on the same HOST_BUNDLE_REL, and browser-agent-live:1001 and agent-mark-live:115 return 0 for that skip. Registry order 6894-6895 runs before 6907. On the next run, failure-page's skipReason checks only that the pb-panes directory exists, so the run proceeds, fails at the rename, and the finally restores the file.

*Checked at:* scripts/lib/firefox-bidi.mjs:156-162 `if (process.listenerCount(sig) > 1) return; disposeHome(home); process.off(sig, onSignal); process.kill(process.pid, sig);`; scripts/verify-platform.sh:139-143 group SIGTERM; scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs:60 skip on missing host bundle

### harness-live-security-05: Seven live rows claim to run 'under flock pb-live.lock' but neither they nor the driver take the lock

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:1184`
- **Category:** race

**What is wrong.** verify-orca-credential-store-live, verify-sidecar-origin-gate-live and verify-orca-failure-page-live re-exec themselves under flock <git common dir>/pb-live.lock. Seven assigned rows do not, although their headers say they run under it: browser-agent-live, agent-mark-live, browser-snapshot-live, agent-status-live, no-remote-agent-port-live, service-live and packaged-live. The registry invokes them as plain `node ...`, and verify-platform.sh contains no flock call. Concurrent sessions on one checkout are a documented condition of this project. Without the lock these rows interleave with the locking rows. Two cases: failure-page's host-bundle rename (the finding above) and its SIGSTOP of an orcad. The rows also wipe fixed scratch paths: snapshot-live runs `rmSync(work)` on .mozbuild/orca-browser-snapshot-live, status-live removes the fixed .mozbuild/orca-agent-status-live/repo, and service-live removes .mozbuild/orca-service-live/repo. A second run of the same row deletes the first run's repository while orcad is using it.

**Scenario.** Session A runs `--only orca-failure-page-live` (locked). Session B runs `--only orca-browser-agent-live` at the same time. B is not blocked. If B starts during A's induction 5, B SKIPs and passes. Or two sessions run orca-agent-status-live together: the second removes the shared repo folder under the first's live claude, and the first run fails or measures the wrong session records.

**Preconditions.** Two live runs on the same checkout at the same time.

**Evidence.** grep: no spawnSync('flock'/PB_LIVE_LOCK_HELD in verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs, verify-orca-agent-mark-live.mjs, verify-orca-browser-snapshot-live.mjs, verify-orca-agent-status-live.mjs, verify-no-remote-agent-port-live.mjs, verify-orca-service-live.mjs, verify-orca-packaged-live.mjs; none in verify-platform.sh or scripts/lib.
scripts/verify-orca-agent-status-live.mjs:45-46 'Run under the live lock'
scripts/verify-orca-browser-snapshot-live.mjs:335 `rmSync(work, { recursive: true, force: true });`
scripts/verify-orca-agent-status-live.mjs:215 fixed `repo` path, 317 `rmSync(repo, ...)`
scripts/verify-platform.sh:6894-6895 `"orca-browser-agent-live|node $REPO_ROOT/scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs"`

**Suggested fix.** Take the lock in one place: a shared helper in scripts/lib that every live row calls at entry (re-exec under flock unless PB_LIVE_LOCK_HELD=1), or have verify-platform.sh wrap every full-tier live row in flock. Use mkdtemp scratch repositories instead of fixed paths.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** A grep for flock and PB_LIVE_LOCK_HELD finds the re-exec only in credential-store-live (293-297), failure-page-live (539-543), sidecar-origin-gate-live (36-39) and landing-logo-live. browser-agent-live, agent-mark-live, browser-snapshot-live, agent-status-live, no-remote-agent-port-live, service-live and packaged-live mention flock only in header comments. Every flock occurrence in verify-platform.sh is a comment, and the registry rows are plain `node ...` (6766, 6777, 6791, 6877, 6884, 6894, 6895). Fixed scratch paths are confirmed: browser-snapshot-live runs `rmSync(work, recursive)` on .mozbuild/orca-browser-snapshot-live. agent-status-live uses the fixed repo path `join(work,'repo')` and removes it in its finally. service-live removes .mozbuild/orca-service-live/repo at line 296. Two concurrent unlocked runs therefore interfere. The only bar is the stated precondition of two concurrent sessions, which project memory documents as a real condition.

*Checked at:* No guard: `grep -n flock scripts/verify-platform.sh | grep -v '#'` is empty; scripts/verify-orca-agent-status-live.mjs finally `rmSync(repo, { recursive: true, force: true })`; scripts/verify-orca-service-live.mjs:295-296

### harness-live-security-06: Clauses (h), (i) and (e) of orca-browser-agent-live turn a failed CLI read into 'not listed' or 'not visible', which passes

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:395`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** listIds() and cookieNames() return [] when the CLI call fails (r.ok false). Clause (h), user close (A7): listedAfter becomes false on the first failed `tab list`, and the clause passes. Clause (i), reload: oldPages is read through listIds before the reload. If that call fails, oldPages is [] and oldListed stays [], so the 'old pages still listed' assertion checks nothing. Clause (e), container: userVisible comes from a second `cookie get` on the agent page. If that call fails, the user cookie counts as not visible. The earlier ownRead positive read used a different call. These clauses guard tab scope and container isolation: a closed or reloaded tab must not remain addressable, and an agent must not read the user's cookies.

**Scenario.** After the reload in (i), orcad answers `tab list --worktree W` with runtime_error, for example because the host session is mid re-attach. oldPages=[] (or now=[] on every poll), oldListed=[], and verdictReload reports no old pages. In (h), a stale page that is still listed passes whenever `tab list` fails at that moment. In (e), if the agent's second cookie get is refused with an error, userVisible=false and the clause passes.

**Preconditions.** An orcad or CLI error at the moment of the read. A regression that fails the read and leaves the page listed passes.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:395 `const listIds = (r) => (r.ok ? (r.result?.tabs ?? []).map((t) => t.browserPageId) : []);`
:396 `const cookieNames = (r) => (r.ok ? ... : []);`
:938 `for (let i = 0; i < 20 && listedAfter; i++) { ... listedAfter = listIds(await ctx.cli('tab', 'list', '--worktree', W)).includes(page); }`
:969 `const oldPages = listIds(await ctx.cli('tab', 'list', '--worktree', W));`
:975 `oldListed = oldPages.filter((p) => now.includes(p));`
:882 `userVisible: cookieNames(await ctx.cli('cookie', 'get', '--page', page)).includes('pbuser')`

**Suggested fix.** Record the CLI outcome alongside the list. Make a failed `tab list` or `cookie get` red ('instrument failed'). Require oldPages to be non-empty and to contain the (a)/(j) page ids before the reload. Require the user-close poll to have seen the page listed once before the close. Add self-test plants for each case.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** At line 395, listIds maps a failed CLI result (r.ok false) to [], and cookieNames at 396 does the same. Clause (h), line 937-938: the loop stops at the first failed `tab list`, listedAfter becomes false, and verdictUserClose (248-251) passes. Clause (i): oldPages (969) comes from listIds. If that call fails, oldListed starts as [] and the loop never runs. If any later poll returns [] from an error, oldListed becomes []. verdictReload (253-259) checks only oldListed.length and has no 'oldPages was read' positive control. Clause (e): userVisible (882) is a separate cookie get. A failure counts as not visible, and verdictContainer (182-186) passes, because ownRead is an earlier, independent call. No caller validates r.ok for these reads. The only runtime element is the CLI error itself, which is the stated precondition, not an unresolved step in the code path.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:395-396, 882, 937-938, 969-975; verdictReload/verdictUserClose/verdictContainer have no read-succeeded control

### harness-live-tabs-01: A SKIP (exit 0) from a live row is counted PASS by the driver and by --gate

- **Severity:** medium (reported as high)
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:943`
- **Category:** false-green
- **Owner decision cited:** D-60 (only for orca-agent-status-live's SKIP inside the gate; it does not cover these three rows)

**What is wrong.** orca-tabs-live, orca-terminal-keys-live and orca-relaunch-restore-live print 'SKIP -- <reason>' and exit 0 when the built PowerBrowser, the Theia app, orca/ or a lane is absent. verify-platform.sh decides PASS or FAIL from the exit status alone and never inspects output for SKIP, so the row is recorded 'PASS' and the gate prints 'every registered check is green'. Stages l (route containment) and m (pairing-offer containment) of orca-tabs-live guard security requirements and are never exercised on such a run.

**Scenario.** A CI runner or a developer checkout with no objdir/ and no orca/ build runs verify-platform.sh --gate. All three live rows exit 0 with a SKIP line; the summary lists them as PASS; the gate reports PASS. A regression in the pb-panes route or in offer containment is never seen. This is the same mechanism as the known agent-status-live SKIP counted PASS in gate run 4.

**Preconditions.** Gate or full run on a tree where any prerequisite (objdir binary, theia lib/backend/main.js, orca/out/orcad, pb-panes bundle) is absent.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:943 `if (pre.skip) { console.log(`${NAME}: SKIP -- ${pre.skip}`); return 0; }`; scripts/verify-orca-terminal-keys-live.mjs:939 `if (r.skip) { say(`SKIP -- ${r.skip}`); return 0; }`; scripts/verify-orca-relaunch-restore-live.mjs:536 `if (pre.skip) {... return 0; }`; scripts/verify-platform.sh:6975-6985 `setsid $cmd & ... wait "$CURRENT_CHECK_PID" || row_ok=0 ... SUMMARY+=("$label: PASS")` with no output inspection.

**Suggested fix.** Have the driver treat a row whose output contains a line matching '^<label>: SKIP' as SKIP, not PASS, and make --gate refuse (or list as NOT RUN) any SKIP not named in GATE_KNOWN_OPEN_EXCLUSIONS; or give the live rows a distinct exit code (e.g. 77) that the driver maps to a gate failure.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** I traced the whole path myself. (1) The cited code exists and does what the finding says. verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:943 prints "SKIP --" and returns 0 when lib.prerequisites() returns a skip. That function returns a skip, never a fail, in these cases: orca-panes is absent, the objdir firefox binary is absent, the Theia backend main.js is absent, orca/out/orcad is absent, or the panes bundle is absent (scripts/lib/orca-tabs-live.mjs:91-104). verify-orca-relaunch-restore-live.mjs:536 does the same through livePrerequisites() (lines 213-221), which also skips while lane p65-01 is unmerged. verify-orca-terminal-keys-live.mjs:939 returns 0 on r.skip, which run() sets from base.skip (line 810). (2) The driver's run loop (verify-platform.sh, about lines 6973-6990) runs `setsid $cmd &`, waits, and records "$label: PASS" whenever the exit status is 0 and the orcad leak check finds nothing. It never reads the row's output. (3) run_gate_mode (verify-platform.sh:7129-7206) only acts when rc is non-zero, and then only on labels that extract_failed_labels (7066-7067) finds as "  label: FAIL" lines. A SKIP row is recorded as PASS, so with no other red the gate prints "--gate PASS -- ... every registered check is green at this commit" (7200). The only SKIP-is-red logic in the tree is inside rebase-orca.sh's self-test (comment at 3962), not in the driver. (4) The precondition is real and cheap: any checkout or runner with no objdir build or no built orca/. I found no guard that turns SKIP into a failure under --gate. Why medium and not high: the check is not one that "cannot go red". On any tree where the build exists, stages l (route) and m (offer) run and can fail. The gap is a false-green gate summary on unbuilt trees, and the SKIP line still appears in the printed log. That is a concrete defence-in-depth and process gap. The tree accepts the same SKIP-passes pattern elsewhere and comments on it ("ON A TREE WITH NO generated/, THIS ROW SKIPS AND PASSES", around line 4754). I found nothing in DECISIONS.md that accepts this risk for the live rows. Even if an owner decision did accept it, the finding would still stand.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:943 (SKIP returns 0); scripts/lib/orca-tabs-live.mjs:91-104 (missing prerequisites are a skip, not a fail); scripts/verify-platform.sh about 6973-6990 (PASS decided by exit status alone); scripts/verify-platform.sh:7066-7067 and 7199-7200 (gate only matches lines ending ": FAIL", then prints "every registered check is green")

### harness-live-tabs-02: no-tab-chrome and offer-storage absences go green when the page probe returns an error object

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:672`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** ctx.probe returns `{ error: 'probe threw: ...' }` (pb-relaunch.probe) instead of throwing when the page body throws. Two absence judgments consume probe results without checking for that shape. theiaMatches = {error:'...'} yields entries [['error','probe threw...']]; the string is never > 0, so chromeVerdict reports no match and the stage is PASS. The storage probe feeding the offer places becomes `storage?.local ?? ''`, so the offer is judged absent from localStorage and sessionStorage.

**Scenario.** The Theia-side querySelectorAll probe (or the storage probe) throws (frontend mid-reload, a selector derived from Orca source that is not a valid CSS selector, page error). Stage i and the Theia-storage half of stage m report PASS having inspected nothing.

**Preconditions.** A page-side exception in those two probes.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:672 `const theiaMatches = await ctx.probe(`const out = {}; for (const s of ... out[s] = document.querySelectorAll(s).length ...`);` then chromeVerdict: `Object.entries(obs.theiaMatches ?? {}).filter(([, n]) => n > 0)`; line 743-744 `const storage = await ctx.probe(...)`, `'the Theia document\'s localStorage': storage?.local ?? ''`. scripts/lib/pb-relaunch.mjs:85-89 `result = { error: 'probe threw: ' + e }`.

**Suggested fix.** In chromeVerdict require every selector key to be a finite number in theiaMatches and fail otherwise; in stage m fail when storage is null or has .error; add self-test plants for both.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** probeExpression turns a page throw into {error:'probe threw: ...'} and probe() returns it parsed. For the no-tab-chrome probe, theiaMatches={error:string}. chromeVerdict filters entries with n>0; a string compared to 0 is false, so no failure is reported. For the storage probe, storage?.local ?? '' yields '', so those two places are searched as empty. Neither consumer checks for .error. Mitigation: the detector count catches an invalid selector (count().catch(()=>0)), which makes chromeVerdict go red. Other page throws (reload in flight, etc.) still pass silently.

*Checked at:* scripts/lib/pb-relaunch.mjs:85-89; scripts/verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:342-346, 672, 743-744

### harness-live-tabs-03: Offer-containment check has a trivial detector, covers only the full offer string, and no control per searched place

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:745`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** Stage m's detector is bridgeFile.includes(offer) where offer was itself read from that same file (ctx.offerFromFile), so it always holds and proves nothing about whether the log, state, diagnostics and storage dumps can display an offer. Each of those places is searched with text.includes(offer) for the full offer URL only. The pairing input actually used (pairingInputFromFile: the fragment or token) is a different string and is never searched, so a leak of just the token passes. orcad's own stdout and log files, the profile and bridge folder other files are not searched, although the header claims 'none of the services' logs'. Empty or capped ring buffers (getRecentLog returning []) also pass.

**Scenario.** OrcaService logs `pairing=<token>` or the URL fragment without the origin, or orcad logs the pairing code to its own log. includes(offer) is false in every place; stage m is PASS.

**Preconditions.** A secret-bearing log line that is not byte-identical to the full offer URL.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:745 `fail('offer', offerVerdict({ detector: !!offer && !!bridgeFile && bridgeFile.includes(offer), foundIn: offer ? Object.entries(places).filter(([, text]) => text.includes(offer)).map(([p]) => p) : [] }));` with `offer = await ctx.offerFromFile()` (lib line 276-282 reads the same web-pairing file).

**Suggested fix.** Search for the pairing input (ctx.pairingInputFromFile) and its parts as well as the offer; plant a known-secret control into each place (or prove each dump non-empty and able to carry a planted string) before judging absence; include orcad's log files and the profile scan.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** offer comes from the web-pairing file and the detector tests bridgeFile.includes(offer) on that same file, so it is true whenever the file is non-empty and cannot show that the search would notice a leak. Each place is searched only with the full offer string, so a leaked token or fragment alone is not matched. Only the ring-buffer logs, state, diagnostics and the two storages are searched, not orcad's own stdout or log files, despite the claim in the header.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:397-400, 741-745; scripts/lib/orca-tabs-live.mjs:276-282

### harness-live-tabs-05: relaunch-restore ignores the result of the Setup restore and mode-switch commands

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-relaunch-restore-live.mjs:462`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** s.command swallows every error and returns false; restoreSetup(), mode() and the command ids (SAVE, RESTORE, ACTIVATE read by regex, null if the source moved) are never checked after launch 1's save. Launch 2 asserts dock, modes, identity and terminals against saved state, but Theia's own layout restore after a normal relaunch (the observer's restore hold exists for that) can reproduce the same state with Setups' restore command failing. If ACTIVATE is null every s.mode() is a no-op, so 'modes' compares the same main-area tab list under both mode names.

**Scenario.** setups-commands.ts renames its restore command id or the restore throws; restoreSetup returns false, nothing reads it, the dock, modes and identity clauses compare against Theia's own restored layout and PASS; criterion 4 (a saved Setup restores) is unproven.

**Preconditions.** The restore or activate command fails or its id constant moves.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-orca-relaunch-restore-live.mjs:462 `await s.restoreSetup(SETUP_NAME);` (value dropped); 335-337 `async command(id, ...args) { return probe(`try { await ...executeCommand(...); return true; } catch (e) { return false; } `);}`; 338 `async mode(id) { await s.command(ACTIVATE, id); await sleep(800); }`; 275-277 `sourceConst(...)` may return null.

**Suggested fix.** Throw when sourceConst returns null, and require restoreSetup() and each mode() to return true; add a positive control that the restore changed something (e.g. close a tab before restoring and require it back only via the Setup).

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** launch 1's saveSetup result is checked and throws on failure (line 451). Launch 2's restoreSetup result is dropped at line 462. s.command returns false on any error. mode() ignores the result as well, and the ACTIVATE and RESTORE constants can be null if the source moves. The relaunch comparisons could then pass on Theia's own layout restore. Whether that restore reproduces the saved dock exactly is runtime-dependent, so the consequence is credible but not proven.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-orca-relaunch-restore-live.mjs:335-337, 338, 359, 451, 462

### harness-live-panels-01: Every live row in this shard prints SKIP and exits 0, which the driver counts as PASS

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-pane-css-live.mjs:421`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** Each live script returns 0 after printing a SKIP line when an artifact is absent. verify-platform.sh does not parse output for SKIP; exit 0 is PASS. The same pattern is in all six live rows. This is the known agent-status-live defect, repeated here. Several of these rows guard security-relevant requirements: landing-logo (origin gate for the injected mark, INJ-01) and pane-standalone (session-only pairing, API clauses).

**Scenario.** A --gate run on a tree where orca/out/web/assets/pb-panes, the Orca CLI, or theia/node_modules/typescript is missing or stale. skipReasons() or prerequisites() returns a reason, the script prints 'SKIP -- ...' and exits 0, and the registry row is recorded PASS. Nothing was measured.

**Evidence.** css-live:421 `if (skips.length) { console.log(`${NAME}: SKIP -- ...`); return 0; }`; standalone-live:439 same; add-project-live:210-212 (three SKIPs, return 0); workspace-live:254 `if (ready.skip) {... return 0; }`; side-panel-live:493,495; landing-logo-live:152. grep of scripts/verify-platform.sh finds no SKIP handling beyond comments.

**Suggested fix.** Make the driver treat a row whose output contains a SKIP line as not-green under --gate (a named exclusion list like the WINDOWS.md one), or exit with a distinct code that the driver maps to 'skipped, not passed'. Under --gate every live row must have measured.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** css-live:421 prints SKIP and returns 0 when harness.skipReasons() is non-empty. verify-platform.sh has no SKIP parsing; its SKIP mentions are only comments. Registry rows are plain 'node script' commands (e.g. :6811, :6828), so exit 0 is recorded as PASS. A --gate run on a tree missing the built bundle or the CLI records PASS having measured nothing. Severity is medium rather than high because the prerequisites are build artifacts that a gate run normally has, and the rows do go red on real failures once they run.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-orca-pane-css-live.mjs:421; scripts/verify-platform.sh:6811 (no SKIP handling)

### harness-live-panels-03: Side-panel egress clause judges nearly the whole run under the lenient user-action verdict

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-side-panel-live.mjs:820`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** The user-action marker is fetched immediately before activateMode('orca'), and every clause (a to m) runs after it. hostsAfterMarker therefore returns almost every host first resolved during the run, and egressVerdict judges those with userAction:true. A host whose allowlist disposition is user-initiated passes even if the Orca panels resolved it unattended. The unattended verdict applies only to hosts first resolved before the marker, i.e. boot-time hosts.

**Scenario.** Orca's sidebar or Landing starts an automatic request to github.com or a .onorca.dev host while rendering. Both are user-initiated in endpoint-allowlist.json. The host first resolves after the marker, so violations(..., {userAction:true}) skips it and clause i passes, although the requirement (P65-24) is that rendering the panels makes no unattended call.

**Evidence.** side-panel:518-519 marker fetch then `activateMode('orca')`; :820 `const afterAction = hostsAfterMarker(logBase, ACTION_HOST);` :351-352 `violations(allowlist, observed.filter(h => after.has(h)), { userAction: true })`; endpoint-verdict.mjs:21-27 `d === 'user-initiated' && userAction` continues.

**Suggested fix.** Place the user-action marker only around a real user action (the mode switch click) and take a second marker after it; judge everything resolved after the action settles with the unattended verdict.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** The marker fetch happens at side-panel:517-518, immediately before activateMode('orca'). Every Orca panel render therefore falls after the marker. egressVerdict (:340-352) applies the user-action verdict to all hosts first resolved after the marker, so a user-initiated host such as github.com passes even if the panels resolved it unattended. The unattended verdict covers only boot-time hosts. The comment shows this is intended, but it weakens the P65-24 unattended-call check. An unlisted host still goes red.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-orca-side-panel-live.mjs:517-518,820; egressVerdict :340-352

### harness-live-panels-04: Width clause m can pass with zero web tabs added

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-side-panel-live.mjs:772`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** The loop that adds WEB_TABS web tabs with the '+' button does `if (!at) break;` when the button is not found and ignores the result of `until(...)` after each click. widthVerdict only compares before and after widths. If no tab was added, before equals after and the 'after 3 web tabs' pass is vacuous. This is exactly the owner's reported regression path.

**Scenario.** The New Tab button's aria-label changes or the button is hidden in the Orca mode. `at` is null on the first iteration, the loop breaks, widths are unchanged, and keep[0] is green. Any tab-driven width regression is invisible.

**Evidence.** side-panel:772-779 `if (!at) break; ... await until(() => ...getWidgets('main').length > window.__pbMainCount, 5000)` with no check of the count; :779 `mObs.keep.push({ when: `after ${WEB_TABS} web tabs...` ...})`; widthVerdict :304-311 has no tabs-added field.

**Suggested fix.** Record the main-area widget count before and after, put tabsAdded in the observation, and make widthVerdict red (blind) when it is less than WEB_TABS.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** The loop at :765-775 breaks when the New Tab button is not found. It does not check that the main widget count increased after the click. widthVerdict (:304-311) compares only before and after widths, with no tabs-added field. If no tab is added, before equals after and keep[0] passes without testing a tab-driven width change. WEB_TABS=3 (:148).

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-orca-side-panel-live.mjs:765-779, widthVerdict :304-311

### harness-live-panels-10: workspace-live leaks orcad processes and real-home worktree directories when the run throws

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-orca-workspace-live.mjs:456`
- **Category:** leak

**What is wrong.** The SIGKILL of the recorded orcad/daemon pids runs after withProbeShell returns normally, inside the try but not in the finally. Any throw (a failed pollFor, orca CLI error, bundle boot failure) skips it. Separately the 'ws-live' and 'ws-live-2' worktrees are created by Orca under its workspaceDir, which the script itself notes is the host's ~/orca/workspaces/<repo>/; cleanup runs only at the end of the success path and rmdirSync removes only empty directories.

**Scenario.** orca('worktree','create') succeeds, then (b) throws. The harness error is recorded, the scratch repo is deleted, the orcad and daemon processes stay running and bound to their ports, and ~/orca/workspaces/repo/ws-live remains on the user's disk with a dangling git worktree.

**Evidence.** workspace-live:439 comment 'Orca creates worktrees under its workspaceDir (the host's ~/orca/workspaces/<repo>/)'; :456 `for (const [pid, ticks] of recorded) signalIdentity(pid, ticks, 'SIGKILL');` placed after `await live.withProbeShell(...)` and before `} catch (e)`; :459-465 finally has no kill and removes only empty parents.

**Suggested fix.** Move the signalIdentity loop into the finally block; give orcad a scratch workspaceDir so no worktree lands under the real HOME; remove created worktrees through Orca in the finally.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** The SIGKILL loop at workspace-live:456 sits after await withProbeShell(...) in the try body. The finally at 459-465 has no kill, so any throw skips it. withProbeShell's own finally only disposes the profile and does not signal orcad. Worktree cleanup (orca worktree rm and the rmdirSync of empty parents) runs only on the success path inside the callback, and the script's own comment says worktrees are created under the host's ~/orca/workspaces/<repo>/. A throw after worktree create leaves the worktree directory and the orcad/daemon processes behind. Whether withShell's browser kill ends orcad on its own is the only runtime-dependent point, and the script's explicit SIGKILL suggests it does not.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-orca-workspace-live.mjs:439,456,459-465; scripts/lib/pb-live-probe.mjs:57-67

### harness-other-03: orca-repin workflow rule asserts nothing when bump-pr-orca or detect-orca is renamed or removed

- **Severity:** medium
- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Location:** `scripts/verify-workflows.mjs:1087`
- **Category:** false-green

**What is wrong.** The orca-repin rule enforces several App-token ordering invariants in release-train.yml: the token is minted only after the --replay-only replay and the explicit-path git add, no Orca code (pnpm, corepack, tsc, build-orca.sh) runs in the job that holds the token, the job only arms auto-merge, and TAG and COMMIT are re-checked before use. Every clause sits under `if (bump !== undefined && bump !== null)`, keyed on the literal job id. Rename the job (for example to bump-orca or orca-bump-pr), or move these steps into another job, and the rule is silent: no clause fails and nothing asserts that the bump job exists. The ORCA_CODE scan also covers bump-pr-orca alone, so a new job that mints the bot App token and runs pnpm on the fetched Orca tree is not checked by this rule. detect-orca has the same existence guard.

**Scenario.** A refactor renames bump-pr-orca to bump-orca and moves the 'Mint the bot App token' step above the 'Replay the pin move' step, which clones the tagged upstream Orca tree. That is the P68-35 exposure: the token exists while code from a possibly compromised upstream tag (actor f) is present in the job. scripts/verify-workflows.mjs passes and the workflows row stays green in --quick.

**Preconditions.** A workflow refactor renames or splits the Orca bump job.

**Evidence.** scripts/verify-workflows.mjs:1086-1087 `const bump = jobs['bump-pr-orca']; if (bump !== undefined && bump !== null) { ... mint < replay ... ORCA_CODE ... }` and :1112-1113 `const det = jobs['detect-orca']; if (det !== undefined && det !== null) {`. No else branch and no existence assertion. The orca-repin self-test plants edit only the existing job and never remove or rename it.

**Suggested fix.** Fail when release-train.yml has no bump-pr-orca or detect-orca job while scripts/fetch-orca.sh is tracked (derive the expectation from the tree). Better, identify the bump job structurally: any job that both mints the App token and runs rebase-orca.sh or touches orca/. Apply the ORCA_CODE scan to every job that mints an App token. Add plants that rename the job and require red.

**Verifier (CONFIRMED, medium).** In scripts/verify-workflows.mjs:1086-1087 every orca-repin clause (mint-after-replay/add ordering, ORCA_CODE scan, --auto, TAG/COMMIT re-check) sits inside `if (bump !== undefined && bump !== null)` keyed on the literal 'bump-pr-orca'. detect-orca has the same existence guard at :1112-1113. There is no else branch, and I found no job-set pinning for release-train.yml in the script. The header comment at :141 states the rule applies 'when bump-pr-orca exists', so skipping a missing job is deliberate. A rename or split therefore removes these assertions without a red. Generic rules (issues-write, app-token-inputs, dry-run-gate) still apply to a renamed job. None of them checks mint ordering against the Orca replay or Orca code execution in the token-holding job, so the P68-35 ordering is no longer checked. It needs a maintainer workflow edit, which goes through review.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-workflows.mjs:1086-1111 (bump-pr-orca guard, no else), :1112-1118 (detect-orca guard); release-train.yml:1060 job id; no existence assertion found

## Low findings (97)

Each entry gives the defect and the suggested fix. The scenario, evidence and verifier reasoning are in the findings JSON next to this report.

### orcad-hosting

**orcad-hosting-03: Fixed loopback port 26768 lets any local user deny Orca to PowerBrowser by binding it first**
`powerbrowser/shell/powerbrowser-sidecar.js:69` · CONFIRMED

The orcad port is a fixed, published constant, and a busy port is deliberately non-recoverable: no retry, no other port. TCP ports on 127.0.0.1 are not per-user, so another local user or any same-user process can bind 127.0.0.1:26768 before PowerBrowser starts, and Orca then never starts for that session. This is fail-closed: PowerBrowser does not adopt the squatter (T-62-09), and the client's E2EE pinned key means a stale offer sent to a squatter does not reveal the device token. Availability is still lost, and the only recovery is editing a pref by hand. The squat also works in the gap between an orcad crash and its respawn, which sends a running session to the failed state.

*Fix:* When the pinned port is busy at a cold start, fall back to a port PowerBrowser picks from its own range and records in the state file and the hand-off. Keep refusing adoption. At minimum, show the holder's uid (from /proc/net/tcp) in the diagnostics rows so the user can tell a squatter from a leftover.

**orcad-hosting-05: A quit that lands just after orcad's ready line can leave the pairing credential file behind after exit**
`powerbrowser/shell/OrcaService.sys.mjs:529` · CONFIRMED

_stopTree removes the three bridge files first (lines 630-636) and then ends orcad. _spawnAndGate does not check _shuttingDown again after outcome.ready resolves. It goes on to await readProcessStartTicks, writeStateFile and writePrivateFile(orcad-data-root), then _writeHandOff writes web-pairing and orca-panes-dir, and finally sets _state = 'ready'. If stop() starts during those awaits, the removal can run before the writes, so the web-pairing file (a reusable credential) and the other hand-off files remain in the bridge folder after the browser exits. The state can also report 'ready' after 'stopped'. The file is 0600 in a 0700 folder, so exposure is limited to the same user. The next session then consumes it as a stale offer (see the stale hand-off finding).

*Fix:* Re-check this._shuttingDown after each await between ready and _writeHandOff, and skip the writes (and endOwn) when set. Alternatively, have _stopTree remove the bridge files again after the process is gone.

**orcad-hosting-06: Hand-off files from a crashed session are not cleared at start and are served to Theia before OrcaService runs**
`powerbrowser/shell/OrcaService.sys.mjs:106` · PLAUSIBLE

Only _stopTree removes the bridge files web-pairing, orca-panes-dir and orcad-data-root. Nothing in _startSequence or _resolve clears them, and a crash skips the stop. powerbrowser.js starts OrcaService only after TheiaService.start settles, so the Theia frontend boots first. orca-pane-bundle.ts waitForPairing() takes the first offer handOff() returns, and handOff() does not check orcad's state. The frontend therefore boots its memoized pane bundle with the previous session's offer and bundle path. Per 67-03 (P65-17, A-7) the device row is session-only, so that offer is dead once the new orcad replaces the leftover. Before that happens, it authenticates to the leftover orcad that survived the crash, which OrcaService then kills. Expected result: Orca tabs come up in a pairing-lost state after every crash until the user presses Reconnect.

*Fix:* Remove the three bridge files at the start of _resolve, once _bridgeDir is known and before any spawn. Better, have handOff() refuse unless the offer was written after the current orcad's ready (for example, by a start nonce written beside it).

**orcad-hosting-07: orcad crashes after ready are restarted without limit or backoff**
`powerbrowser/shell/OrcaService.sys.mjs:583` · CONFIRMED

giveUpAttempts and the backoff apply only to consecutive failures inside one _launch call. Every successful ready ends that loop, so an orcad that reaches ready and then exits, from a crash bug, an agent-triggered fault or a bad restored session, is restarted by _supervise right away (attempt 1 has no sleep), indefinitely. _restartCount only grows. The state alternates between starting/restarting and ready and never reaches failed, so the failure page and Restart button never appear, and each cycle also kills and respawns orcad's subsystems.

*Fix:* Track exits within a time window (for example, N post-ready exits in M minutes) and switch to the failed state with a 'crashing repeatedly' row. Carry the backoff across supervise cycles instead of resetting it on ready.

**orcad-hosting-08: A start rejected after orcad has started its detached daemon leaves that daemon untracked through quit**
`powerbrowser/shell/OrcaService.sys.mjs:493` · CONFIRMED

orcad starts the detached terminal daemon early (orcad-entry.ts:75), well before it binds the WebSocket port and prints ready, and its own shutdown only disconnects from the daemon. Three OrcaService paths kill orcad through its process handle alone: the startup timeout (line 474), the loopback check (493) and the Node-version check (497). In none of them is the daemon recorded in _daemonPid or the state file. A port-in-use exit after daemon start does the same. At quit, _stopTree sees no live proc and _daemonPid is null, so the daemon is never ended. It lingers until its own idle shutdown, or indefinitely if it adopted or restored sessions. This breaks the RUN-05 goal that quit leaves nothing behind.

*Fix:* Before endOwn on any failed gate, snapshot listProcessTree([proc.pid]) and kill the daemon rows the same way _endDaemon does. On the loopback and Node-version paths, also record ready.daemonPid so quit can reap it.

### pairing-credentials

**pairing-credentials-03: Stale web-pairing and orca-panes-dir files are never cleared at start, on an unexpected orcad exit, on a failed launch, or when the new ready line has no offer, and handOff serves them as current**
`powerbrowser/shell/OrcaService.sys.mjs:546` · CONFIRMED

The bridge files are removed only in _stopTree. A browser crash or kill leaves web-pairing behind. On Linux the bridge folder is under XDG_RUNTIME_DIR; on hosts without it, it falls back to HOME/.cache and survives reboots. Nothing removes the file at the next start or in _supervise. When a new ready line reports pairing.available false, _writeHandOff logs a line and leaves the previous start's offer in place. The backend has no generation or orcad-identity check, so it hands that stale credential to the frontend as this start's offer. Because orcad's token rows persist (see the next finding), the stale file is still a working runtime-scope credential, not dead data. This contradicts the OrcaEnv contract 'removed on stop' as a statement of the offer's lifetime.

*Fix:* Remove web-pairing and orca-panes-dir in _startSequence before the spawn, at the start of _supervise's relaunch, and on the no-offer branch of _writeHandOff. Optionally have the backend refuse an offer file whose mtime predates a chrome-written orcad-start marker.

**pairing-credentials-04: Every orcad start mints a new runtime-scope token row that is never revoked, so each offer PowerBrowser writes stays valid indefinitely**
`theia/extensions/orca-panes/src/browser/failure/orca-hosting-status.ts:20` · CONFIRMED

PowerBrowser treats the offer as tied to one orcad start ('the session-only device row died with the old orcad'; D-36 cites P65-17). At the pin, the row that dies is only the ephemeral session-key fork. The legacy token row that the offer's deviceToken names survives. It is marked seen on first authentication, so it is no longer pending, and the next orcad start calls getOrCreatePendingDevice and creates another row with reach 'network'. Upstream removes token rows only through explicit user removal, and pruneIdleEphemeralDevices touches only ephemeral rows. Over months the profile's orca-devices.json accumulates one live full-runtime token per launch or orcad restart. Every web-pairing file, copy, or backup ever written stays a working credential against any later orcad on that profile. The pairing-containment check lists the registry as holder 5, but neither it nor any decision addresses the unbounded count or the lifetime.

*Fix:* At each orcad start (or stop), have PowerBrowser revoke the token row its previous offer named. pairedDeviceId is in the decoded offer, so chrome can record it in memory and remove it through orcad's device RPC or CLI after the new offer is written. Alternatively, request the offer with rotate so that pending rows are replaced, and remove seen PowerBrowser rows by name. Record the token's real lifetime in INTERNAL-APIS or DECISIONS.

### sidecar-gate-backends

**sidecar-gate-backends-01: Third-party Orca renderer bundle runs in the sidecar's own origin with no CSP, so any Orca UI XSS reaches Theia's full RPC**
`theia/extensions/orca-panes/src/browser/orca-pane-bundle.ts:135` · CONFIRMED · reported as medium · cites D-15

The Orca pane bundle (upstream Orca renderer code, re-pinned weekly under D-26) is loaded as a module script into the Theia document at http://127.0.0.1:<sidecar port>. The 67-02 gate admits its requests as same-origin by construction. Code in that document can call every authenticated Theia JSON-RPC service: filesystem read and write, terminal and process spawn, plugin install, and pb-orca-git. In Orca's own web build the renderer can only reach orcad. Here it renders untrusted remote text (GitHub/GitLab/Jira/Linear comments through CommentMarkdown with rehype-raw plus rehype-sanitize, markdown preview, Mermaid, ipynb), and it runs with Theia's privileges. No Content-Security-Policy is set on the Theia document (no CSP header or meta anywhere in theia/extensions or the browser app). Local code-execution safety therefore depends on every Orca sanitizer holding.

*Fix:* Set a strict CSP on the Theia document (script-src 'self' with the inline boot replaced by a hashed or nonce'd script, no unsafe-eval where Monaco allows it, object-src 'none'). Consider hosting the Orca pane bundle in a separate origin or frame that reaches only orcad and a narrow postMessage bridge, so an Orca-side XSS does not get the Theia RPC. At minimum, add a re-pin check that diffs Orca's sanitize schemas and raw-HTML sinks.

**sidecar-gate-backends-02: pb-orca-git git reads do not disable lazy fetch or prompts, and a timeout kills only git and leaves its fetch children running**
`theia/extensions/orca-workspace/src/node/orca-workspace-backend-module.ts:27` · PLAUSIBLE

runGit removes GIT_* from the environment and passes only `-c core.fsmonitor=false`. It does not set GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1, GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0 or protocol.allow restrictions, and it does not drop SSH_ASKPASS. In a partial-clone (promisor) worktree, `git cat-file blob -- <oid>:<path>` for a missing tree or blob starts a lazy `git fetch` against the repository's configured remote. That fetch uses repository-level `core.sshCommand`, `remote.<n>.uploadpack` (which runs locally for a file/local remote), credential helpers and askpass. execFile's 10 s timeout sends SIGTERM to the git process only, so the spawned fetch, ssh or remote-helper children keep running. The header comment says the read has 'no textconv or filter, fsmonitor off' and every GIT_* name removed, but lazy fetch is not covered.

*Fix:* Set GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1, GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0 and GIT_ASKPASS/SSH_ASKPASS to empty or `true` in the child env. Add `-c protocol.allow=never` (as Orca's own plugin-git-arguments.ts does). Spawn git detached in its own process group and kill the group on timeout.

**sidecar-gate-backends-03: A planted POWERBROWSER_TOKEN cookie with a longer path shadows the real token, and the gate reads only the first match**
`theia/extensions/token-gate/src/node/token-gate-backend-contribution.ts:214` · PLAUSIBLE

Cookies are not port-isolated. Any script on any http://127.0.0.1:<port> origin, including orcad's own web client (which v1.7 loads in stock windows and web tabs), can set a non-HttpOnly cookie named POWERBROWSER_TOKEN with a more specific path such as /socket.io/ or /orca-panes/. HttpOnly on the real cookie prevents overwriting it but not adding a distinct-path cookie. Browsers list longer-path cookies first. getTokenFromCookie returns the first POWERBROWSER_TOKEN entry, so the gate checks the planted value and returns 403 for every request under that path. The chrome's strip observer removes POWERBROWSER_TOKEN entries only on requests it does not allow; it leaves sidecar-origin requests unchanged. The impact is availability only: a valid token cannot be fixed or learned this way.

*Fix:* Accept the request if any POWERBROWSER_TOKEN value in the header validates, comparing each value in constant time. Alternatively, in the http-on-modify-request observer, rewrite the Cookie header on allowed sidecar requests to carry only the chrome-set token.

### chrome-actor

**chrome-actor-04: All agents share one public container; browser.cookie.get with no URL returns every agent-container cookie, httpOnly included**
`powerbrowser/shell/PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11488` · CONFIRMED · cites D-41

agentContainerId creates one public ContextualIdentity, 'PowerBrowser Orca agents', that every agent tab of every worktree and agent session uses. agentCookieGet with no url returns getCookiesWithOriginAttributes({userContextId}): the whole container's jar, httpOnly cookies included. A prompt-injected agent in one worktree can read session cookies another agent's tab obtained, or that the user created by signing an agent tab into a site so the agent could work. The identity is public, so the user (stock container menus) and WebExtensions with the contextualIdentities or cookies permission can also use this jar.

*Fix:* Use one container per Orca worktree or agent session (or per browserProfileId Orca already sends), and use a private or hidden identity (ContextualIdentityService private identities) so the jar is not offered in user container menus. Consider limiting cookie.get with no URL to the tab's current site.

**chrome-actor-05: Agent browsing is written into the user's global history and tab store, so an agent can seed the user's address-bar suggestions**
`powerbrowser/shell/PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:3620` · CONFIRMED

The agent branch of webTabOpen changes usercontextid, messagemanagergroup and autocompletepopup. It does not set disableglobalhistory, and webTabAttach registers the overlay's tab-store row as for any user tab. Places is not partitioned by container, so every page an agent visits, with its page-controlled title, enters the history that the suggestion rows (NG-023) and Panorama read. A prompt-injected agent can visit an attacker URL repeatedly under a title like 'GitHub - Sign in', raising its frecency until it is a top suggestion when the user types 'github' in a user tab.

*Fix:* Set disableglobalhistory on agent overlays (and skip or mark their tab-store rows), or filter agent-container visits out of suggestions.

**chrome-actor-06: Page-menu edit rows on a text field in an agent tab always fail: PowerBrowserEditState is registered for the 'browsers' group only**
`powerbrowser/shell/PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:9062` · CONFIRMED

WINDOWS 43 routes a text-field edit row to `global.getActor(EDIT_STATE_ACTOR_NAME)`. That actor is registered with messageManagerGroups ['browsers'], and agent overlays are in 'powerbrowser-orca-agent', so getActor throws NotSupportedError. handlePageMenuAction catches it and answers 'the page menu row failed'. Undo, Redo, Delete and Select All on an input in an agent tab never run. Cut, Copy and Paste are refused by D-38 anyway. pageEditTargetCommands falls back to cached state, so the rows appear enabled.

*Fix:* Add AGENT_MESSAGE_MANAGER_GROUP to the edit-state pair's messageManagerGroups, or fall back to the Controllers actor when getActor throws.

### agent-browser

**agent-browser-06: The agent navigation wall checks the scheme only: loopback and LAN http services are reachable**
`powerbrowser/shell/PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:4147` · CONFIRMED

browser.goto (agentUrl -> webTabNavigate) and the navigate page command admit any http(s) URL. Each request is checked once, and redirects are not re-checked. The scheme wall correctly blocks file:, chrome:, about:, resource:, moz-extension:, view-source:, data: and javascript:. Hosts are not checked, so an agent can load 127.0.0.1 services (orcad's port, local dev servers, other users' loopback services) and LAN admin pages, and read them with snapshot or eval. The Theia sidecar is protected only because its token cookie is in the user's container, not the agent's.

*Fix:* Refuse the sidecar's and orcad's own loopback ports for agent overlays (from OrcaService and TheiaService state), and consider a user-confirmed allow list for private-address targets, checked again on redirects (http-on-modify-request already sees agent channels).

### overlay-renderer

**overlay-renderer-03: A failed bootstrap is cached for the life of the document and Reconnect cannot recover it**
`orca-overlay/files/src/renderer/src/pb/pb-pane-mount.tsx:669` · CONFIRMED

bootstrap stores its first promise in `booting ??=`. A rejection at any step (pairing, the session-only assertion, installWebPreloadApi, or the services root) is returned unchanged to every later call. The Theia loader caches the same rejection (`this.booting ??= this.boot()`), and OrcaHostingStatus sets its repair API only after ready() resolves. After a failed boot, Reconnect therefore never calls repair, and nothing re-runs bootstrap. Side effects from the failed attempt (presetSessionOnly, closeActiveRuntimeClients, the wrapped runtimeEnvironments.subscribe and settings.set) also stay installed, so a retry would wrap them a second time.

*Fix:* On rejection, reset `booting` to null so that a later call can retry, and make the wrappers idempotent (install them once behind a flag). On the Theia side, let Reconnect re-run boot() when `failed` is set.

**overlay-renderer-04: mountErrors grows without bound**
`orca-overlay/files/src/renderer/src/pb/pb-pane-mount.tsx:196` · CONFIRMED

Every caught, uncaught and recoverable React error from every pane root and the services root is appended to a module-level array, and nothing removes entries. state() copies the whole array on each call. A surface whose error boundary catches on every resetKey change, for example a page that throws on each store update, adds an entry each time for the life of the document.

*Fix:* Keep a fixed-size ring buffer, for example the last 50 entries plus a total count.

### theia-pane-hosting

**theia-pane-hosting-03: The launch prune treats an unreadable or corrupt setups.json as naming nothing, so tombstoned pb-orca names a Setup still uses are released and minted again**
`theia/extensions/modes/src/browser/setups-service.ts:1419` · CONFIRMED

referencedTabUris() catches any read error of setups.json and returns an empty set for it, and uses parseSetupStore(...).setups even when parsed.ok is false. OrcaPaneNameMap.prune then drops every tombstone that the session snapshot does not name. SetupsService's own loader says read failures happen in a mid-save race. Once a tombstone is pruned, mintName can issue that name to a different Orca pane, and a saved Setup that names pb-orca:<name> then opens the wrong pane in its slot. This is the case T-65-13 says is mitigated: "never reissued while a Setup or the session names them".

*Fix:* Return a tri-state from referencedTabUris: distinguish 'file absent' (empty is correct) from 'read failed' or 'parse not ok', and skip the prune when the reference set is unknown. A tombstone left in place an extra launch costs nothing.

**theia-pane-hosting-04: A user's existing custom mode named "Orca" is silently dropped on upgrade and erased on the next mode save**
`theia/extensions/modes/src/browser/mode-service.ts:216` · CONFIRMED

v1.7 adds a shipped mode labelled 'Orca'. parseModeStore skips any stored custom row whose name matches a shipped label (`continue`), without adding it to badNames, so no notice is shown. The next saveCustom writes `[...this.lastGoodCustoms, row]`, which removes the user's row from storage permanently. A persisted active id of custom-orca then resolves to Browsing.

*Fix:* Migrate a colliding legacy row instead of dropping it: rename it (for example 'Orca (custom)'), or keep it under its custom-<slug> id with a distinct label, and report it through the existing badNames notice path.

**theia-pane-hosting-05: pb-orca:creating opens the worktree creation surface when no creation is pending**
`theia/extensions/orca-panes/src/browser/restore/orca-pane-name-map.ts:191` · CONFIRMED

ORCA_PAGE_ROUTES marks the creating page whilePendingCreation, and docs/URI-SCHEMES.md says it is "open only while an Orca worktree creation is pending". The restore opener nevertheless creates a page:creation widget for the name 'creating' whenever the URI is opened: typed in the address bar, opened from a Setup or session restore, or recreated by Theia's layout restorer, since page widgets are never disposed from a list. Nothing reads whilePendingCreation. Only OrcaSidePanelSync closes the tab, and only on a pending-to-not-pending transition.

*Fix:* In widgetFor, refuse the whilePendingCreation route unless the side-panel sync reports pendingCreation. Close a layout-restored page:creation widget at start when the store reports no pending creation.

### agents-extensions

**agents-extensions-01: Wrapper leaks an Orca pane when the pane-key lookup fails after the pane was created, or when a signal arrives during the synchronous CLI phase**
`theia/extensions/orca-agents/bin/claude-wrapper.mjs:152` · CONFIRMED

createPane creates the terminal first, then may call `terminal show` (errors swallowed) and `terminal list`. The `list` call is not inside a try block, so a timeout, non-zero exit or non-JSON output throws a raw execFileSync/JSON error that has no `.pane` property. main() closes a half-created pane only when `e?.pane?.handle` is set (line 192), so in this path the pane is never closed. The same happens when `terminal create` times out after orcad has already created the terminal, or when its output does not parse. Separately, the SIGINT/SIGTERM/SIGHUP handlers are installed only after spawn (line 205). A SIGTERM during the up-to-60 s synchronous CLI phase therefore kills the wrapper with the default action, and a pane that was already created is left behind. Each leaked pane is a 'Claude Code' Orca terminal tab running `cat` with no agent in it, and it stays until orcad restarts.

*Fix:* Inside createPane, wrap everything after a handle is known in try/catch and rethrow with `{ pane: { handle } }` attached, so main() always closes it. Install the signal handlers before the CLI phase: on a signal before spawn, close the pane if one exists and exit 128+n.

**agents-extensions-02: Claude start-up blocks on up to three synchronous 20 s Orca CLI calls before claude is spawned**
`theia/extensions/orca-agents/bin/claude-wrapper.mjs:119` · CONFIRMED

runCli uses execFileSync with CLI_TIMEOUT_MS = 20000. createPane can make three sequential calls (create, show, list) before claude is spawned, so a wedged but reachable orcad delays every Claude Code session by up to 60 s. During that time the event loop is blocked and nothing is forwarded. The orcad-data-root file is removed only when OrcaService stops orcad cleanly, so after a crash or hang the wrapper still trusts the stale file and pays the full timeouts on every session.

*Fix:* Use one overall deadline for the pane phase (for example 3-5 s in total) instead of 20 s per call, or spawn claude at once and attach the pane identity only when it is ready within a short budget.

**agents-extensions-03: On Windows the backend still registers a POSIX sh launcher as the Claude Code process wrapper default**
`theia/extensions/orca-agents/src/node/orca-agents-backend-module.ts:48` · PLAUSIBLE · cites D-40

TheiaService passes POWERBROWSER_ORCA_BRIDGE_DIR on win32 (bridgeDirFor returns a LOCALAPPDATA path), and onStart skips the POSIX mode checks there. It then writes the `#!/bin/sh` launcher with no extension and the frontend registers that path as the claudeCode.claudeProcessWrapper default. Windows cannot execute an extensionless sh script, so if the extension honours the default, Claude Code fails to start rather than running unwrapped. The wrapper is also inert on Windows even if it were reached, because readDataRoot requires `cli/bin/orca` while orcad writes `cli/bin/orca.exe` on win32. WINDOWS entry 53 tracks the missing Windows launcher, but the backend is not made inert on win32 meanwhile.

*Fix:* Return undefined from getClaudeWrapperPath on win32 until a Windows launcher exists (for example an .exe shim), so the extension's own spawn stays untouched there.

### ci-supply-chain

**ci-supply-chain-03: The green re-pin replay that auto-merge relies on is reported by the upstream code it is meant to check**
`.github/workflows/release-train.yml:1176` · CONFIRMED · reported as medium · cites D-26

bump-pr-orca arms `gh pr merge --auto` when rebase-orca reports stage `replayed`. That stage is decided in the job that installs and runs the new Orca tag. The typecheck:web and typecheck:node commands are scripts from Orca's own package.json. tsc, Vite and every build tool come from Orca's lockfile at the new tag. During the replay step, Orca code can also write $GITHUB_ENV (for example BASH_ENV) or edit the workspace to steer the later 'Name the stage' and 'notices' steps. The PR's required `verify` check builds nothing (verify.yml), and no other workflow builds Orca, so nothing outside the job under test runs the new code. The only remaining gate is the owner's approving review (main-review), and the diff it shows is a pin hash and regenerated seam context. A review of that diff cannot see what changed upstream. T-68-12 ('vacuous green replay') covers SKIP lines but not a green result forged by hostile upstream code.

*Fix:* Treat `replayed` as a compatibility signal, not a security signal, and say so in the PR body. Do not arm auto-merge for an upstream code change. Show the owner the upstream compare link and commit range from the old pin to the new one in the PR body. Optionally require a second signal computed outside the job that runs the code (for example a check that Orca's lockfile adds no new packages, run in bump-pr-orca over git data only), and arm auto-merge only when the lockfile is unchanged.

**ci-supply-chain-06: Upstream build output can write the text of the Orca stop issue**
`.github/workflows/rebase-orca.yml:122` · CONFIRMED

The stage output is the first log line that starts with 'rebase-orca: FAIL -- '. The log includes everything Orca's build, typecheck and probes print, because capture() echoes their output. A hostile upstream can print such a line with any text and then exit non-zero. The report job writes that text into an issue created with the job token, in the repository's release-train label set. The text can carry @mentions, links and misleading 'Next step' instructions. It is a single line, so GITHUB_OUTPUT cannot be split.

*Fix:* Have rebase-orca.sh write its own stage line to a separate file descriptor or file that composed commands cannot write to, and read the stage from there. Restrict the published stage to the stage keyword plus a [A-Za-z0-9_./:@ -] detail of bounded length.

### packaging-prefs-network

**packaging-prefs-network-05: Notices record native executables in the Orca bundle only by their npm package licence, not by what the binaries statically include**
`scripts/lib/orca-shipped-packages.mjs:159` · CONFIRMED

build-orcad.mjs copies the prebuilt agent-browser native executable (an 18 MB Rust binary, agent-browser-linux-x64 and its per-OS siblings) and the ripgrep binary into out/orcad. orca-shipped-packages.mjs records each only as its npm package (agent-browser@0.39.4 Apache-2.0, @vscode/ripgrep-universal MIT) and takes the licence text from the package root. The crates statically linked into the agent-browser binary carry their own notice requirements, and the notices include none of them. ripgrep has its own resources/licenses/ripgrep folder, which is staged but not rendered into THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt.
