*Fix:* For each copied native executable, render any third-party licence file the package ships next to the binary, or set ORCAD_OMIT_AGENT_BROWSER=1 if the feature is not needed. Render out/orcad/ripgrep/licenses into the Orca section.

**packaging-prefs-network-06: uBlock Origin is pinned at 1.76.0 with automatic add-on updates off, so its security fixes reach users only through a PowerBrowser release**
`powerbrowser/packaging/bundled-xpi.json:4` · CONFIRMED

The pre-installed uBlock Origin is staged unmodified at the pinned version. extensions.update.autoUpdateDefault=false (branding prefs) means the daily versioncheck-bg check finds a newer AMO version but does not install it, as the v1.7 privacy text confirms. A bundled extension with broad host permissions that runs in every profile therefore keeps any vulnerability until the pin is bumped and a release ships, unless the user updates it by hand.

*Fix:* Allow automatic updates for uBlock0@raymondhill.net alone (the per-add-on applyBackgroundUpdates setting through a distribution or startup hook), or add a release-train job that bumps the pin when AMO publishes a new signed version.

### harness-driver

**harness-driver-04: orcad started through orca-run.mjs leaks when startOrcad rejects or the row is interrupted, and the leak leg cannot see it**
`scripts/lib/orca-run.mjs:179` · CONFIRMED · reported as medium

startOrcad spawns orcad with detached: true, which puts it in its own session and process group. On its timeout or ready-line parse failure it rejects without killing the child, and attaches the child only as error.child. Three callers ignore error.child: verify-orca-credential-store-live.mjs:146 (const started = await startOrcad(...)), verify-orca-browser-host-bundle.mjs:406 (run stays null, so the finally skips stopOrcad), and lib/orca-pane-harness.mjs:169 (child stays null). Only slotless-start and packaged-live recover it. When the driver interrupts a row, cleanup() sends `kill -- -$CURRENT_CHECK_PID`, which never reaches a detached orcad. The row's node then dies on SIGTERM without running its finally (liveHome's handler re-raises the signal), so orcad and its terminal daemon survive. The 2026-11-16 leak leg cannot catch any of this: it runs only while the row's own process group still has a member (`kill -0 -- -PGID`), and it only lists orcads whose session id equals the row's session.

*Fix:* In startOrcad, kill the child's process group (and the daemon pid if known) before rejecting. Register a process 'exit' and signal handler in orca-run.mjs that stops every orcad it started. Have the driver record the row's start ticks and run the leak leg unconditionally, matching orcad by an argument or working directory under the row's scratch roots (as stopStrays does) instead of by session, and also stop Orca's terminal daemon.

**harness-driver-07: Survivors of a row's process group other than orcad are neither failed nor stopped**
`scripts/verify-platform.sh:6991` · CONFIRMED

When `kill -0 -- -PGID` shows that something of the row outlived it, the driver runs only the orcad leg. Any other survivor (a PowerBrowser, a Theia sidecar, a stub agent, an HTTP fixture server) is left running, the row is PASS, and CURRENT_CHECK_PID is cleared right away, so cleanup() on EXIT never signals that group. Survivors accumulate across a multi-hour gate and can hold ports or profile locks that later rows meet.

*Fix:* After the leak leg, send SIGTERM and then SIGKILL to the row's process group (`kill -- -$PGID`). Report the survivors' argv as a row note, or as a FAIL under --gate.

**harness-driver-08: orcad-leak self-test proves the driver wiring by substring only; the gate and row verdict logic has no self-test**
`scripts/verify-orcad-leak.mjs:92` · CONFIRMED

The self-test confirms only that verify-platform.sh contains the text `scripts/verify-orcad-leak.mjs" --session "$CURRENT_CHECK_PID"`. It never runs the driver loop. A fault that keeps the substring but drops its effect stays green: `|| true`, removing `row_ok=0`, inverting the `kill -0` guard, or placing the line in a comment. Its live plants call the CLI directly with the plant's own pid as session, so the driver's `kill -0 -- -PGID` gate is never exercised. No check covers run_gate_mode, extract_failed_labels or ledger_entry_is_open.

*Fix:* Add a self-test that runs verify-platform.sh against a fixture registry (an env-selected CHECKS file) with a planted row that leaks an orcad-shaped child, one that prints SKIP, and one that fails under a closed and an open ledger entry. Require the summary lines and the exit status for each.

**harness-driver-09: live-scratch-home writes probe modules into scripts/lib, which is tracked and not ignored, during the quick gate**
`scripts/verify-live-scratch-home.mjs:212` · CONFIRMED

runHelperChild writes a copy (sometimes a planted variant) of firefox-bidi.mjs to scripts/lib/.scratch-home-probe-<pid>-<n>.mjs and deletes it only when the child exits. If the parent is killed (the completion hook's 480 s limit, a driver SIGINT that reaches the group, or OOM), the file stays in a tracked source directory that is not gitignored. Later treeFiles() scans and any `git add -A` by an agent will pick it up. In the same way, an interrupted orcad-leak self-test leaves its detached orcad-shaped plants running (`setInterval(() => {}, 1e9)`, own session), because they are killed only in its finally.

*Fix:* Write probes to a mkdtemp folder and import the helper there through a file URL with an import map. Alternatively add the probe pattern to .gitignore and sweep stale probes at start. Kill the orcad-leak plants from a process 'exit' and signal handler.

**harness-driver-10: ng-027 change lets a reader on one boundary object be satisfied by a call through the other**
`scripts/verify-ng-027-reader-callers.mjs:96` · CONFIRMED

v1.7 widened the method call pattern to `(PowerBrowserAPI|PowerBrowserProcesses)\.name\(`. deriveReaders does not record which object a two-space-indented method belongs to. A dead PowerBrowserAPI reader is therefore counted as called when a same-named method is called on PowerBrowserProcesses, and the reverse. Today the PowerBrowserProcesses methods (spawnProcessExact, listProcessTree, addNamedReapBlocker, writePrivateFile, platform) share no reader name with PowerBrowserAPI, so the drift risk is low. This is the only one of the five hash-locked ng files whose v1.7 change loosens an assertion. ng-018 adds a wait (the following expects are unchanged), ng-023 and ng-067 tighten their checks, and ng-072 only wraps env in liveEnv.

*Fix:* Record each method's owning object from the enclosing `export const X = Object.freeze({` span and build the call pattern from that object only. Add a self-test plant with the same name on both objects.

**harness-driver-11: macOS Orca merge plan skips symlinks, so they ship from the arm64 tree unchecked**
`scripts/lib/orca-artifacts.mjs:96` · CONFIRMED

packagedFiles walks out/orcad and out/web with readdirSync(withFileTypes) and keeps only e.isFile(). Symbolic links (files or directories) are neither paired, compared nor reported. package-macos.sh orca-universal then does `cp -a "$arm/out/orcad" "$arm/out/web"`, so every symlink and its target come from the arm64 tree, and an x64-only or differing link disappears from the universal package without a FAIL. The self-test plants no symlink.

*Fix:* Treat a symlink as a planned item: require identical link targets in both trees, or refuse with FAIL naming it. Add a symlink plant to the self-test.

### harness-static-hosting

**harness-static-hosting-04: Session-only pairing probe checks the absence of a hand-copied upstream localStorage key**
`scripts/lib/orca-offer-scan.mjs:35` · CONFIRMED · reported as medium

orca-offer-scan's main security assertion is that orca.web.runtimeEnvironment.v1, the key under which Orca's web client keeps its paired runtime environment, is absent from every 127.0.0.1 origin's localStorage (P65-17 session-only pairing). The key name is a literal in the lib, copied from orca/src/renderer/src/web/web-runtime-environment.ts:34 (ENVIRONMENT_STORAGE_KEY), and never derived from the pin. The scan reads only the key column (values may be compressed). pairing-containment's whole-profile scan excludes the Theia origin's storage folder as holder 4. So nothing else covers that origin. This is an absence assertion over a name that can only agree with the tree it was copied from.

*Fix:* Derive the key at check time from ENVIRONMENT_STORAGE_KEY in orca/src/renderer/src/web/web-runtime-environment.ts (red 'source moved' when absent), the way registryFilesFromPin derives holder 5. Better: require that the Theia origin's key set, minus the marker, is a subset of a set derived from the tree.

**harness-static-hosting-05: Logs sink control never travels the stdout/stderr capture path; a missing capture file reads as empty and the sink stays green**
`scripts/verify-orca-pairing-containment.mjs:333` · CONFIRMED · reported as medium

The logs collector's control is 'orcad ready on ' in any log text. The live run takes that line from OrcaService.getRecentLog(), which it reads through chrome evaluation. PowerBrowser's stdout and stderr are tee'd to files through PB_LIVE_STDOUT_PATH/PB_LIVE_STDERR_PATH and then read with `captured()`, which returns '' when the file is absent or unreadable. So the control is found even when nothing from stdout or stderr was captured, and the sink is green without having seen either stream. This is the CLAUDE.md rule 1 failure: the control is not on the path it vouches for.

*Fix:* Give each stream its own control: require that stdout.log exists and contains a line PowerBrowser always prints there (for example POWERBROWSER_SHELL_READY or a console marker the run writes on purpose), and that stderr.log exists. Turn a missing capture file into 'collector blind' instead of ''.

**harness-static-hosting-06: orca-offer-file's backend rule does not check the no-follow open or the lstat/fstat inode match that closes the TOCTOU window**
`scripts/verify-orca-offer-file.mjs:225` · CONFIRMED

The backend rule requires an lstat and offerFileVerdict before the first read, and accepts any of readNoFollow, readFile, readFileSync, createReadStream or .open( as that read. The protection against the file being swapped for a symlink or another file between lstat and read lives in readNoFollow: O_NOFOLLOW plus an ino/dev comparison with the judged stat. The check does not inspect readNoFollow, and no self-test plant removes either part.

*Fix:* Require that handOff's first read is readNoFollow(full, stat), and that readNoFollow's body opens with O_NOFOLLOW and compares ino and dev with the judged stat. Add plants for a plain readFile and for a removed inode comparison.

**harness-static-hosting-07: Credential-store backend clause reads two hand-picked files and never checks the key file's owner-only mode the sentence claims**
`scripts/verify-orca-credential-store.mjs:101` · CONFIRMED

Clause 3 builds the 'written backends' set only from orcad-secret-key-resolution.ts and orcad-plaintext-credential-migration.ts. A keyring write added in any other orcad file (orcad-secret-store.ts, orcad-secret-store-startup.ts, orcad-secret-sealing.ts, or a secret-tool store next to the existing lookup in orcad-legacy-keyring-key.ts) is invisible. The protection sentence also says the key file 'only your user account can read', but nothing derived from the pin checks the secure-file mode orcad writes. The live row is the only proof of that.

*Fix:* Scan every non-test file under orca/src/main/orcad for backend: object literals and for keyring program spawns (secret-tool store, security add-generic-password). Derive and assert the 0o077 restriction of the key record (restrictExistingRecord / the secure-file helper's mode) from the pin.

**harness-static-hosting-08: Non-loopback-peer collector scans only the orcad and Theia backend pids, not their children**
`scripts/lib/orca-offer-scan.mjs:459` · CONFIRMED

listenSockets joins /proc/net/tcp{,6} LISTEN rows only with the fd tables of the two pids it is given: orcad from getState().pid and the Theia backend. The terminal daemon (a separate process whose pid OrcaService knows), Theia's plugin-host child processes, and any helper orcad forks are not scanned. So a listener off loopback in a child process does not make the 'no remote agent port' sink red.

*Fix:* Expand each root pid to its descendant set (from /proc/<pid>/task/*/children or a /proc ppid walk) before joining sockets. Include the daemon pid from getState(), and add a self-test plant with a child pid listening on 0.0.0.0.

**harness-static-hosting-10: orcad's fatal failure codes are a hand-kept pair; the checks prove only that each still exists in the pin**
`scripts/verify-orca-service.mjs:985` · CONFIRMED

OrcaService treats exactly ORCAD_PINNED_PORT_CODE and ORCAD_LOCK_HELD_CODE as unrecoverable, and maps them to port-in-use. orca-failure-classes derives its code set from OrcaEnv's own exports (the same source as the subject). orca-service clause 9 only greps the pin for those two strings. Neither derives orcad's code set from the pin and compares it as set equality. A new unrecoverable orcad code therefore goes unnoticed and is retried as a recoverable 'starting orcad' failure.

*Fix:* Derive orcad's exit error-code set from the pin (the code: literals of orcad's startup error classes) and compare it as set equality with the codes OrcaService classifies. An unmapped pinned code goes red naming it.

**harness-static-hosting-11: Scenario A takes its expected environment from the same allowlist as the subject, and the steering deny list is hand-kept**
`scripts/verify-orca-service.mjs:934` · CONFIRMED

Scenario A compares orcad's spawned environment names with env.expectedEnvNames(...). That is built from ORCAD_ENV_ALLOWLIST, the same constant buildOrcadEnv uses, so adding a name to the allowlist moves the expectation with it. The only guard against a dangerous addition is the hand-kept STEERING list plus the /^(ORCA|CLAUDE|NODE|ELECTRON)_/ prefix test. Names that change how agents' shells and git run (BASH_ENV, ENV, GIT_SSH_COMMAND, GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL, PYTHONPATH, PERL5LIB, LD_AUDIT) are on neither.

*Fix:* Pin the allowlist with an independent expectation (for example a checked-in list whose change also needs a DECISIONS.md entry, compared as set equality). Or widen the deny rule to every LD_*, *_OPTIONS, BASH_ENV/ENV, GIT_* and interpreter-path variable.

**harness-static-hosting-12: shell-request-kinds clause 3 finds a handler only when it is called on the same line as its case label**
`scripts/verify-shell-request-kinds.mjs:149` · CONFIRMED

The sender-wall clause collects the handlers a case dispatches to with /case\s+"[^"]+"\s*:[^\n]*?return\s+(handle[A-Za-z]+)\s*\(/. That requires the call on the case label's line and a name starting with handle. A case written as a block (`case "orcaFoo": {` with the return on the next line), or a handler with another name, is not collected. Its callers outside the walled switch are then never checked. The 'broken instrument' red fires only when zero handlers are found, and the existing orcaStatus line keeps that from happening.

*Fix:* Derive handlers from every call expression inside each case arm (from the label to the next case/default), whatever its name or line. Add a plant with a block-form case whose handler has a second caller outside the switch.

### harness-static-browser

**harness-static-browser-04: Entry-points clause 3 accepts every reachable creator call with a hard-coded claim and cannot go red**
`scripts/verify-orca-browser-entry-points.mjs:453` · CONFIRMED · reported as medium

Clause 3 is documented as 'each caller is listed reachable (arrives as createPage through the client placement, proven live by orca-browser-agent-live) or dead with its derived reason'. In code, a caller of createBrowserTab or createWebRuntimeSessionBrowserTab that the pane entry's import graph reaches is pushed to `lines` with the constant REACHABLE text and never to `fails`. The claim is not checked. For createBrowserTab it contradicts the check's own clause 10 comment (D-34: createBrowserTab's managed-browser gate refuses a paired web client, so it does not arrive as createPage). A new reachable caller of a page-creating path is mislabelled and stays green. The clause asserts on a constant.

*Fix:* Make the reachable set an explicit derived-and-compared allowlist. createWebRuntimeSessionBrowserTab callers may be reachable; a reachable createBrowserTab caller is red, or must be named in a ratified list that is compared as set equality. Add a self-test plant that makes a createBrowserTab caller reachable and requires red.

**harness-static-browser-05: Host-bundle redaction clause scans only the script's own pre-redacted lines and an older build log, so it cannot go red**
`scripts/verify-orca-browser-host-bundle.mjs:552` · CONFIRMED

The PASS line claims 'redaction 0 matches'. The count runs over `lines`, which the script builds itself from verdict strings, a notes JSON and already-redact()ed error text, plus the newest .mozbuild/orca-build log. The secrets are the fresh token, key and endpoint of a scratch orcad started in this run, so they cannot appear in a build log written before the run. The real process output already passes through installOutputRedaction and is never inspected. This is an absence assertion over the script's own instrumentation (CLAUDE.md verification rule 1) and has no positive control.

*Fix:* Capture the row's real stdout and stderr (spawn the probe as a child, or tee the raw writes before redaction) and count secrets there. Prove the detector first by counting at least one match in the raw unredacted orcad ready line. Drop the stale build-log term or restrict it to logs written during the run.

**harness-static-browser-06: Every named SKIP in these rows exits 0 and is recorded as PASS (complete list)**
`scripts/verify-platform.sh:6985` · CONFIRMED

The driver has no SKIP state: a script row passes on exit 0. Every SKIP path in the assigned scripts exits 0. orca-browser-tab-scope: page-table clause SKIP when orca-browser-dispatch.ts is absent (:1134) and LoginManager-group clause SKIP without upstream/ (:1181). orca-browser-abilities, orca-browser-tools and orca-browser-entry-points, main and --self-test: SKIP when theia/node_modules/typescript is absent (abilities :268/:368, tools :222/:238, entry-points :421/:617). orca-browser-host-bundle: SKIP of both the static clauses and the attach probe when the bundle, orcad or the CLI is absent (:528-531). orca-agent-hooks: SKIP of the live stages and static clauses when orcad, the CLI or the launcher lib is absent (:658-661). orca-claude-sdk: loader-scenario SKIP without orca/, without the applied overlay or without esbuild (:291-294, :448-450), --built SKIP without out/orcad (:311), self-test SKIP without esbuild (:344-346). orca-hidden-electron: SKIP without a built orca/ (:218) and permanently on any host where an installed Orca exists at /usr/local/bin, /usr/bin or /opt/Orca (:225).

*Fix:* Give SKIP its own exit code (for example 77) across these scripts. Have the driver record it as SKIP in the summary and fail --gate for any SKIP not on the WINDOWS.md known-open list. Delete the page-table SKIP branch now that lane p66-02 is merged.

**harness-static-browser-07: Orcad survivors after host-bundle teardown are only a note, and the driver leak leg cannot see them**
`scripts/verify-orca-browser-host-bundle.mjs:503` · CONFIRMED

stopOrcad returns orcad and terminal-daemon pids still alive after SIGTERM/SIGKILL. The row stores the count in notes and still passes. The driver's leak leg only inspects the row's own session, but startOrcad spawns orcad with `detached: true`, which calls setsid. Orcad and its terminal daemon, which also setsids, are therefore outside the row's session. A leaked orcad or daemon from this row is never turned red.

*Fix:* Make survivors a FAIL in attachVerdict. Include the daemon's descendants in stopOrcad's pid set.

**harness-static-browser-08: orca-browser-tools compares two hand-kept lists; new Orca browser tools at a re-pin are never detected**
`scripts/verify-orca-browser-tools.mjs:76` · CONFIRMED

The expectation, the U-rows, is parsed from the spike README table, which is hand-written. It is compared with the hand-written ORCA_BROWSER_TOOLS map. Neither side is derived from the pinned Orca source, so a re-pin that adds a user-facing browser tool (a new toolbar control or command in components/browser-pane) leaves both lists unchanged and green. A `skip` entry also passes with any non-empty reason.

*Fix:* Derive the tool set from the pinned source, for example the command ids and toolbar components under orca/src/renderer/src/components/browser-pane, the way orca-browser-parity derives RPC abilities. Compare it as set equality with the U-row table, so a new upstream tool goes red until it is mapped.

**harness-static-browser-09: Raw-visibility structural clause matches only === and !== comparisons**
`scripts/verify-orca-browser-tab-scope.mjs:889` · CONFIRMED

The clause 'every overlay visibility read goes through overlayShownToUser' (A1) counts only `.style.visibility ===` or `!==`. A loose `==`/`!=`, a `switch (b.style.visibility)`, `b.style['visibility']`, `getPropertyValue('visibility')` or getComputedStyle(...).visibility read outside the predicate is not counted. Such a read misjudges a parked agent overlay (visible under the Theia frame) as shown to the user.

*Fix:* Match every read of the visibility property: `\.style(\.visibility|\[\s*['"]visibility)` not on an assignment's left-hand side, plus getComputedStyle/getPropertyValue('visibility'). Better, walk the acorn AST for MemberExpressions named visibility. Add self-test plants for the `==` and switch forms.

### harness-static-structure

**harness-static-structure-03: orca-pin goes SKIP, not FAIL, when both orca_tag and orca_commit are removed from configuration.toml**
`scripts/verify-orca-pin.mjs:135` · CONFIRMED · reported as medium

If neither [upstreams] key is present, check() returns skip and render() exits 0. Orca is integral to this tree, so absence of the pin is a defect, not a downstream configuration. None of the nine self-test plants covers removing both keys (tag-without-commit covers only one removed), so this path is unguarded. Nothing else in the quick tier derives that the keys must exist; the fetch-orca.sh refusal is not run in CI.

*Fix:* Make absence a FAIL in this tree. If a downstream with no Orca is genuinely supported, derive that from a flag such as orca-overlay/ or flake devShells.orca being absent, never from the pin alone. Add a plant for both keys removed.

**harness-static-structure-04: orca-packaged-tree compares only file names between the stage and the built tree, so tampered or stale file content ships green**
`scripts/verify-orca-packaged-tree.mjs:126` · CONFIRMED · reported as medium

The check claims the shipped Orca root is "exactly the built Orca tree's package.json, out/orcad/** and out/web/**". It compares only the two path sets. Only node-pty/build/Release is compared by sha256, and only when --prebuild-manifest is supplied. The release.yml macOS jobs and package-windows.sh pass --prebuild-manifest explicitly; the default linux run defaults it from the host orca/. Any other staged file whose bytes differ from the built tree's passes, such as a modified orcad.js, a stale out/web chunk, or a payload swapped between build and package. The macOS universal merge is likewise compared by names only.

*Fix:* Derive a sha256 per file from the built tree (or trees, for macOS thin trees) and compare the stage per path. Allow only the documented RUNPATH-stripped ELF exception, which the check already models.

**harness-static-structure-05: orca-build-shape and orca-pane-entry prove which tracked paths are modified, not that they hold the declared patch result**
`scripts/verify-orca-build-shape.mjs:140` · CONFIRMED

The claim is that Orca's tracked files differ from the pin in exactly the declared seams. The check compares the path set from `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no` with the seams.json paths. A seam path edited to any content other than the patch result still passes. Untracked or ignored additions to the orca/ tree are excluded by --untracked-files=no. orca-pane-entry clause b compares status text to a baseline file that the build itself wrote, which has the same limit.

*Fix:* Compare `git -C orca hash-object <seam path>` with the blob that applying the patch to the recorded upstream_blob yields. Derive that blob at check time from the recorded blob plus the patch, as the overlay replay already does.

**harness-static-structure-06: orca-overlay treats an absent seams.json, patches/ or files/ as zero seams and passes**
`scripts/verify-orca-overlay.mjs:157` · CONFIRMED

If orca-overlay/ or seams.json is deleted, rows = [] and the patch and file lists are empty. The check passes with "0 seam(s), 0 overlay file(s)". The seam cap is only an upper bound, and orca-build-shape derives its seam list from the same file, so it is equally satisfied. An overlay that silently vanishes, such as the browser-provider seam and the pane bundle files the product depends on, produces no red row in the quick or full tier. Only --upstream-prs and the live rows would notice, and --upstream-prs crashes on JSON.parse when the file is missing.

*Fix:* Fail when seams.json is absent. Compare the derived overlay against a derived requirement, for example that every orca-overlay/files path referenced by theia/extensions/orca-panes exists. Remove the "vacuous" PASS for orca-overlay-built once the first overlay file has landed.

**harness-static-structure-07: orca-no-pb-add-project cannot go red for any PowerBrowser project or folder control that is not an exact normalised match of an Orca string, and SKIPs its self-test when typescript is absent**
`scripts/verify-orca-no-pb-add-project.mjs:55` · CONFIRMED · reported as medium

The header claims PowerBrowser adds no "Add-project, folder-picker or project control". The check only fails when a PB command, menu, toolbar or keybinding string normalises to equal an Orca translate() default that matched /add (a )?project/i, or one inside the add-repo-opening function. A PB command labelled "Add Folder", "Open Folder", "New Project", "Add Workspace" or "Choose Repository", or a label built from a constant, template or import, passes. No folder-picker vocabulary is derived at all.
Separately, main() and --self-test both exit 0 with SKIP when theia/node_modules/typescript is absent (line 339), before the self-test runs. The self-test fixture is hermetic and does not need typescript present on disk, only a loadable one.

*Fix:* Broaden the derived vocabulary to Orca's folder-picker and add-repo dialog strings, not only the /add project/ pattern. Flag any PB registration whose label matches a derived project/folder/repo noun. Run the self-test on the fixture even when typescript is missing, or fail it when CI=true.

**harness-static-structure-08: Three checks guard their CLI entry point with a symlink-sensitive comparison; when it misses, the script does nothing and exits 0**
`scripts/verify-orca-landing-selectors.mjs:215` · CONFIRMED

landing-selectors compares `import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(process.argv[1]).href`. pane-css compares `resolve(process.argv[1]) === fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)`, at line 153. pane-entry does the same through INVOKED, at line 612. Node realpaths the ESM main module for import.meta.url but leaves process.argv[1] as typed, so any symlinked component in REPO_ROOT makes the guard false. The script then falls through with no output and exit 0, for both the check and --self-test. The other scripts in the shard use realpathSync on both sides. The canonical path in CLAUDE.md is under /home/chris/coding, which is not a symlink today.

*Fix:* Use the realpathSync(resolve(argv[1])) === realpathSync(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)) form already used by sibling checks, and make a false guard print a FAIL and exit 2 when the file was run as a script.

**harness-static-structure-09: orca-slotless-start trusts orcad's self-reported boundEndpoint for the loopback requirement, and can leak the terminal daemon when orcad fails before its ready line**
`scripts/verify-orca-slotless-start.mjs:97` · CONFIRMED

Loopback bind is proven only by orcad's own ready line, which is the subject. It is not compared with /proc/net/tcp or the listening sockets of the process tree. A defect that reports ws://127.0.0.1 while listening on 0.0.0.0 passes. The only independent control is the `--bind 127.0.0.1` argument this script itself passes in startOrcad.
On the failure path, probeOrcad catches a startOrcad rejection and sets run.child = e.child but run.ready stays null. The terminal daemon pid is only known from the ready line. stopOrcad is called with daemonPid null, so the daemon that orcad may have spawned is not stopped by this script. The driver's leak leg later names an orcad that is still in the row's session.
The row also SKIPs whenever an installed Orca exists in the real ~/.local/bin, even though orcad itself runs under liveEnv's scratch HOME.

*Fix:* After the ready line, read /proc/net/tcp and /proc/net/tcp6 for the listener inode of the orcad pid and require every listening address to be 127.0.0.1 or ::1. On a startOrcad failure, scan the process tree for descendants (descendants() already exists) and stop them as well.

**harness-static-structure-10: verify-upstream-pins' new rebase-orca.yml rules ban a schedule key and defaults, but do not assert the trigger set or script-injection safety**
`scripts/verify-upstream-pins.mjs:277` · CONFIRMED

workflowFailures for rebase-orca.yml requires workflow_dispatch and workflow_call with tag and commit inputs. It also requires no default, no schedule key, no pin literal, and replay inside nix develop .#orca. It does not assert that those are the only triggers, and does not forbid `${{ inputs.* }}` inside a run: body. Today the workflow reads the inputs through env (REBASE_TAG, REBASE_COMMIT), which is safe, but nothing keeps it that way. Adding `pull_request`, `pull_request_target` or `push` to the on: block, or interpolating inputs directly into the shell, still passes. This workflow clones and builds a third-party tree.

*Fix:* Require the set of direct children of `on` to equal {workflow_dispatch, workflow_call}, derived from the file and compared as set equality. Fail any `${{ inputs.` or `${{ github.event.` inside a run: body (allow only env: and with: contexts). Add one plant for each.

**harness-static-structure-11: Fixture git commits in several self-tests inherit the developer's global git config**
`scripts/verify-orca-overlay.mjs:302` · CONFIRMED

Fixture repositories are committed with only -c user.name and -c user.email set. They inherit the user's global and system git config: commit.gpgsign, gpg.program, core.hooksPath, core.fsmonitor, init.templateDir and similar. With commit.gpgsign=true the self-test can invoke the signing agent or fail. A global core.hooksPath runs the user's hooks inside the throwaway repositories. verify-orca-pin.mjs shows the intended form (-c commit.gpgsign=false -c tag.gpgsign=false).

*Fix:* Route fixture git calls through one helper that adds `-c commit.gpgsign=false -c tag.gpgsign=false -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null` and sets GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null and GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1.

**harness-static-structure-12: orca-bridge-dir verifies the writePrivateFile permission order with unanchored regexes over text, so a dead or conditional step still satisfies it**
`scripts/verify-orca-bridge-dir.mjs:158` · CONFIRMED

The A-11 order (0700 folder, exclusive empty create, 0600 before content, move) is checked by running each regex against the method body after stripping only full-line // comments. A step in a never-taken branch, a trailing // comment, or a string literal still matches. The self-test only plants removal or reordering of the exact existing text. The header acknowledges that modes on disk are proven only by the live row. This is a credential-adjacent file (the data-root hand-off).

*Fix:* Parse the member with the TypeScript or acorn AST. Require the calls as top-level awaited statements of the method, in order, and not inside an if other than the `posix` guard. Add a plant that wraps the 0600 call in `if (false)`.

**harness-static-structure-13: orca-pane-css scope check accepts any selector that merely contains the scope string**
`scripts/verify-orca-pane-css.mjs:100` · CONFIRMED

Clause c treats a selector as scoped if it includes PANE_ROOT or PORTAL_ROOTS anywhere. A selector such as `.leak:not(<scope>)`, or the scope used as an argument inside :is() or :where() that does not constrain the subject, passes. Self-test plants mutate the already-computed result strings, not the pass or the sheet, so they exercise compareFixtures rather than the clause c logic. Only the one unscoped-rule plant covers clause c.

*Fix:* Require the scope as a leading compound or ancestor of the selector by parsing it with postcss-selector-parser. Add a plant that wraps the scope in :not().

**harness-static-structure-14: orca-terminal-keys self-test copies orca/ source paths unconditionally, so it errors, not SKIPs, where the Orca half would SKIP**
`scripts/verify-orca-terminal-keys.mjs:863` · CONFIRMED

check() prints a named SKIP for the Orca half when orca/ is absent, and the header documents this. runSelfTest, however, does `cpSync(join(REPO_ROOT, rel), ...)` for the orcaTypes, orcaEffective, orcaDefs, orcaPane, InputPane, platform-defaults and chat-reveal paths with no existence guard. Without orca/ this throws ENOENT as an unhandled error, giving a red crash that is inconsistent with the sibling self-tests (pane-routes, editor-routes), which SKIP. In the hosted verify job, which has no orca/, the row is either red for an unrelated reason or has been tolerated. I could not run it to tell which.

*Fix:* Guard the self-test with the same orca/ presence check and print a named SKIP, or fetch orca/ in CI (see the first finding). Make the row's policy consistent across sibling checks.

### harness-live-security

**harness-live-security-07: Self-tests leave many live-row assertions without a planted fault**
`scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:1066` · CONFIRMED

The project rule requires a planted fault per assertion. The following assertions have none. orca-browser-agent-live: verdictActs (b) has no plant at all (title, click count, typed value). verdictRenderer is planted only for lease_stale (not placement kind, createPage not arrived, or missing credential). verdictReload is planted only for agentWidgetsAfter (not reattached, oldListed). verdictScreenshot: deviceDpr mismatch, outside pixel red. verdictDecoy: createOk, title, quietMs. verdictAuth: authorized. verdictLogins: agentSubmitted. verdictCreate: !agent, boundTab. verdictNoPane: newTabs>1, ms>CREATE_MS. verdictTyping: chrome focus moved. verdictActivation: user focus changed, !bound. verdictFirstDocument: !created. verdictContainer: userCookieSet. verdictRefusals: missing error.code. orca-agent-mark-live: Stop button missing, empty baseline, stopped.mark and stopped.stopped. orca-agent-status-live: bridgeDir empty, launcher not naming the bridge folder, wrapper missing, engine 'does not descend from the terminal daemon', 'no agent process', quit 'vacuous'. orca-service-live: reap 'old daemon alive', 'state not ready'.

*Fix:* Add one plant per fail string, and derive coverage instead of hand-keeping it: run every verdict over CONTROL with each field mutated in turn, or check that every literal fail message in the verdict source is named by at least one plant.

**harness-live-security-08: Assertions that cannot fire in a live run: (l) capturedAfter and status-live waitingNotDerivable**
`scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:203` · CONFIRMED

verdictLogins reds on capturedAfter > 0, a count of stored logins for 'pbagent' after the agent submit. Gecko's form-submit capture only offers a save doorhanger. A login is stored only if a user accepts it, so the count stays 0 even when the agent tab has a working LoginManager actor. The agentActor assertion is the only effective one. In orca-agent-status-live, agentVerdict reds when waitingNotDerivable is set while derivedWaitingEvent names an event. The live caller passes waitingNotDerivable: !derivedWaitingEvent, so the condition is always false.

*Fix:* For (l), observe the capture attempt directly, for example a password-save doorhanger or a PasswordManager:onFormSubmit message for the agent browser. For status-live, record whether the 'waiting not derivable' note path was taken as a separate observation, or drop the assertion.

**harness-live-security-09: no-remote-agent-port-live accepts a listener by port number only, at one startup snapshot**
`scripts/verify-no-remote-agent-port-live.mjs:107` · CONFIRMED

treeSockets() labels a listening socket 'sidecar', 'orcad', 'tab-store' or 'orcad-agent-hooks' when its port equals an accepted port, whatever the bound address. A sidecar or tab-store endpoint bound to 0.0.0.0 or :: is accepted and the row passes. orca-service-live checks loopback only for orcad's own pid. The shipped launch is sampled once, as soon as accounting is satisfied after the state files appear, so a debugging or agent listener opened later in the session is never observed. Processes that leave the descendant tree (reparented to init) are also not inspected.

*Fix:* Also require each accepted socket's local address to be loopback (127.0.0.0/8, ::1, ::ffff:127.x), and plant a 0.0.0.0 sidecar row in the self-test. Re-sample after a fixed settle and after one agent command.

**harness-live-security-10: credential-store-live judges refused starts with one process walk, so an orcad that started and exited is not detected**
`scripts/verify-orca-credential-store-live.mjs:213` · CONFIRMED

Steps 3 and 4 (temporary profile, plaintext override) assert 'no orcad' with a single /proc walk taken immediately after failureClass reaches the refusal class. The protected outcome is that orcad never ran with a disposable profile or plaintext storage, and so wrote no key or report. A regression that spawns orcad first and then classifies and stops it leaves nothing running at walk time, so the step passes. The row does not check for <dataRoot>/orca-secret-protection.json, which it already reads in step 2, or for a key under <scratch HOME>/.orca after a refused start.

*Fix:* In each refusal step, also assert that no protection report exists in the profile's data root and that no credential key file exists under the run's scratch HOME/.orca. Poll the walker across the whole refusal window, not once.

**harness-live-security-11: orca-service-live's environment set equality takes its expectation from the same allowlist that builds orcad's environment**
`scripts/verify-orca-service-live.mjs:100` · CONFIRMED

envVerdict compares orcad's /proc environ names with OrcaEnv.expectedEnvNames(pbNames), which is ORCAD_FIXED_ENV_NAMES plus ORCAD_ENV_ALLOWLIST. buildOrcadEnv copies exactly that same ORCAD_ENV_ALLOWLIST. Adding a credential-bearing name to the allowlist (GH_TOKEN, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY) changes the subject and the expectation together, so the live row stays green. The static guard in verify-orca-service.mjs keeps a hand-kept list of Node and loader steering names only. The GH_TOKEN self-test plant goes red only because the fixture's allowlist does not list it.

*Fix:* Keep one independent statement of the allowed set: a reviewed fixture with a hash pinned in DECISIONS, or a deny pattern for secret-like names (TOKEN, KEY, SECRET, PASSWORD, CREDENTIAL). Make the static row fail when the allowlist drifts from it.

**harness-live-security-12: failure-page-live recovery can pass over an empty id set, and the auth-refusal induction is a note, not red**
`scripts/verify-orca-failure-page-live.mjs:498` · CONFIRMED

recovered() polls `ids.every(...)`, which is true at once for an empty list. The offer-refusal and browser-not-attached recoveries pass `ids.filter(id => hostedIds().includes(id))`. If the views after the reload or relaunch carry different ids than chatId and termId, the filter is empty and the recovery passes without looking at any pane. Induction 3 (pairing-lost through a revoked device, A-7) records 'not achieved' as a note and never goes red, so the revoked-device path to the pairing-lost page is not asserted on any run.

*Fix:* Make recovered() red on an empty id list ('collector blind'). Judge recovery over the ids read after the relaunch. Turn the induction-3 note into a FAIL, or into a ledger-tracked exclusion that --gate surfaces.

**harness-live-security-13: The harness rewrites the owner's real ~/.claude/.credentials.json with a read-then-rename that can lose a concurrent write**
`scripts/lib/firefox-bidi.mjs:119` · CONFIRMED

scratchSignInSettled() reads the real credentials file, compares hashes, writes a temp file and renames it over the real file, keeping the other keys from the earlier read. Nothing locks against the owner's own claude processes, which write the same file (token refresh, MCP OAuth entries) between the read and the rename. A write that lands in that window is overwritten. The sweep in liveHome() also runs this write-back for every stale pb-home-* folder at the start of any live row. So rows that never seed a sign-in (credential-store, service, browser-agent) can also write the real credential store.

*Fix:* Re-read the real file immediately before the rename and abort if its mtime or contents changed. Better, never write the real store: keep the scratch folder and print the instruction (the existing fallback). Limit the sweep's write-back to rows that seeded a sign-in.

**harness-live-security-14: Clipboard clause (r) overwrites the real system clipboard on a headed run and restores text only**
`scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs:740` · CONFIRMED

orca-browser-agent-live does not refuse PB_LIVE_HEADED=1 or PB_REAL_INPUT=1, unlike the credential-store and service rows. withProbeShell forces headed mode under PB_REAL_INPUT. In a headed session, Services.clipboard is the desktop clipboard. Clause (r) saves only a text/plain reading, sets marker strings, and in finally restores only a non-empty text value. Non-text content (an image, files) and an empty clipboard end up replaced by a marker, and the restore is skipped if the process is ended by a signal.

*Fix:* Refuse headed or real-input mode in this row, as orca-credential-store-live does at lines 121-124, or skip clause (r) with a FAIL-class 'headless required' result when headed.

### harness-live-tabs

**harness-live-tabs-04: Reload stage does not prove that a reload happened**
`scripts/verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:700` · PLAUSIBLE · reported as medium

The reload is `location.reload()` via setTimeout, a fixed 1000 ms sleep, then a poll for FrontendApplicationStateService.state === 'ready'. If the unload is slower than 1 s (Orca's unload checkpoint or a beforeunload prompt can delay it), the old document still reads 'ready', all names are present, localStorage is the old one, and reloadVerdict is green with ms near 0. Nothing checks for a reload marker (performance.timeOrigin change, window.__pbAddWidgetLog reset). g3's second.reload() has the same shape.

*Fix:* Record performance.timeOrigin (or set a window flag) before the reload and require a different value, and the absence of window.__pbAddWidgetLog, before treating the frontend as reloaded; fail the stage otherwise.

**harness-live-tabs-06: not-gone clause waits for NaN when the restore-hold constant moves**
`scripts/verify-orca-relaunch-restore-live.mjs:496` · CONFIRMED

holdMs comes from sourceNumber(OBSERVER_REL, 'ORCA_RESTORE_HOLD_MS'), NaN if the constant is renamed. `await sleep(Math.max(0, connectedAt + holdMs + 2000 - Date.now()))` then sleeps about 1 ms, and not-gone is judged before the observer's restore bound has elapsed, so a widget disposed as gone later is not seen. Only the quick self-test row catches the rename; the live row does not fail.

*Fix:* In runLive throw unless Number.isFinite(holdMs).

**harness-live-tabs-07: Stage c (terminal-agent) claims the real claude TUI starts but only checks that a widget was added**
`scripts/verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:502` · CONFIRMED

The header states the real claude's TUI starts in the agent-opened terminal. The code only runs creationVerdict (a hosted terminal widget added within 1000 ms). No terminal read, TUI marker or echo is checked, so a claude that exits at once, or a stub, still passes.

*Fix:* Poll lib.terminalText for the TUI's banner (or at least a non-empty read) from c.handle and add it to the verdict, or reword the header.

**harness-live-tabs-08: Prompt-budget verdict and no-tab-chrome detector cannot fail from the live path as written**
`scripts/verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:576` · CONFIRMED

promptOnce returns false once promptsSent >= 1, so promptsSent never exceeds 1 and promptBudgetVerdict(promptsSent) is a constant-green assertion in the live run (only the self-test drives it red). Separately, chromeVerdict's detector is the total across all derived selectors (second.count sums matches), so one selector present in Orca's client makes every other derived selector's absence in Theia count as detected even if that selector never renders in the client.

*Fix:* Count prompts where the composer is written to rather than in the guard; compute detector matches per selector and require each judged selector to have matched in the client.

**harness-live-tabs-09: Self-tests leave several assertions without a planted fault**
`scripts/verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:1074` · CONFIRMED

CLAUDE.md requires a plant per assertion. In orca-tabs-live no case plants routeVerdict's absolute-path or entry-200 branch, reloadVerdict's missing-names or late-ms branch, closeVerdict's disposedMs branch, creationVerdict's wrong kind or wrong name branch, any pbSplitVerdict branch except remounts, chromeVerdict's no-selector branch, or the Theia-side error shape. In orca-terminal-keys-live the readKeys passthrough-versus-shell-keys equality, click.tab missing and restore pty branches are not planted. The route verdict guards a security boundary.

*Fix:* Add one plant per remaining branch, each naming the drift text.

**harness-live-tabs-10: Confirmation-dialog detector is broad and not baselined**
`scripts/verify-orca-terminal-keys-live.mjs:129` · CONFIRMED

CONFIRMATION_SELECTOR (also inlined in orca-tabs-live stage j) matches any `body > [role=dialog|alertdialog]` or `body > div [role=alertdialog]`, and the cancel step clicks the first dialog's cancel button found anywhere. A Theia or leftover dialog satisfies 'Orca's confirmation rendered'; the count is not compared with a pre-press baseline.

*Fix:* Scope to Orca's pane root and require the dialog count to rise from a recorded baseline; require its text to name the running terminal.

**harness-live-tabs-11: tab-strip-live skips the Orca-bundle boot control silently when orca/ is not built**
`scripts/verify-tab-strip-live.mjs:581` · CONFIRMED

The WINDOWS 62 fix requires the pb-panes bundle to have booted before the middle-click paste control, but only when orca/out/web/assets/pb-panes/pb-panes.js exists in this repo. When it is absent the check prints 'middle: PASS' with no note that Orca's document listeners were not covered. The test is on the repo path, not on the app under test (PB_BACKEND_MAIN can point elsewhere), so a built app with the bundle and a tree without it reproduces the original blind spot. A genuine boot failure is now correctly red 'NOT SCORED'.

*Fix:* Decide from the frontend under test (is the orca-panes module key defined or the extension wired?) and print an explicit 'Orca listeners not covered' line when it is skipped.

**harness-live-tabs-12: Live output after teardown is not redacted; failure reasons carry page error text**
`scripts/verify-orca-tabs-live.mjs:945` · PLAUSIBLE

withOrcaTabsSession and withOrcaProfile install output redaction and call restoreOutput() in finally. main() then prints stage reasons and notes after that, unredacted. Reasons include `second client: ${second.error}` built from `evaluate failed: ${e.message}` of the paired second-client page and the harness error text. The offer and pairing input are only redacted while the session runs.

*Fix:* Apply redact(text, secrets) to everything printed after the session, or keep redaction installed until main() returns.

**harness-live-tabs-13: Live rows run git against the real HOME and overwrite the desktop clipboard**
`scripts/lib/orca-tabs-live.mjs:311` · CONFIRMED

The scratch repository's git calls get HOME = the developer's real HOME (cliEnv.HOME from savedEnv.HOME), so ~/.gitconfig (commit.gpgsign, core.hooksPath, hooks) applies to the fixture commits and to makeConflict's merge. relaunch-restore's addRepository runs git with no env at all. terminal-keys-live writes the 100 KiB code, line text and sentinels to Gecko's clipboard and selection clipboard (setClipboard/setSelection), which are the developer's own when the run is headed, and does not restore them. The recorder files in .mozbuild/orca-terminal-keys-live are never removed.

*Fix:* Pass GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null, GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 and -c commit.gpgsign=false, core.hooksPath=/dev/null to fixture git; save and restore the clipboards or document that headed runs clobber them.

**harness-live-tabs-14: The documented live lock is never taken by the rows or the driver**
`scripts/verify-platform.sh:6840` · CONFIRMED

Every live row's header says to run under flock on .git/pb-live.lock with ORCA_* unset, but neither the scripts nor the registry rows take the lock (no flock in the registry). Two sessions on the same machine (the project runs concurrent Claude sessions) can run live rows at once, and a leaked orcad on a shared port masked a real defect once (WINDOWS 62). ORCA_* scrubbing is done by the scripts, the lock is not.

*Fix:* Have the live rows (or the driver for live-tier rows) take the pb-live.lock themselves.

### harness-live-panels

**harness-live-panels-02: Live harness rows share one fixed scratch tree and symlink but only landing-logo takes the live lock**
`scripts/lib/orca-pane-harness.mjs:75` · CONFIRMED · reported as medium

withOrcaPaneSession deletes and recreates .mozbuild/orca-pane-harness/repo and orca-profile and re-points the fixed alias pb-orca-pane-harness on every run. The headers say to run under flock pb-live.lock, but the registry rows are plain `node script` commands. Only verify-orca-landing-logo-live.mjs re-execs itself under flock; css-live, pane-standalone, add-project, workspace and side-panel do not.

*Fix:* Make withOrcaPaneSession (and the other live entry points) acquire the pb-live.lock itself, as landing-logo does, or use a per-process mkdtemp work dir and alias name.

**harness-live-panels-05: Squeeze assertion has only a lower bound, so 'stops at Orca's minimum' is not checked**
`scripts/verify-orca-side-panel-live.mjs:325` · CONFIRMED

widthVerdict requires the squeezed width to have narrowed and to be no less than min - tolerance. It never checks that the width reached Orca's minimum. A squeeze that stops at any larger width (for example a wrong clamp at 300) passes although the drag goes to the window edge.

*Fix:* Also fail when sq.after > min + tol, because the squeeze drag travels to the window edge.

**harness-live-panels-06: Pages clause f passes when only one of the derived sidebar entries is shown**
`scripts/verify-orca-side-panel-live.mjs:704` · CONFIRMED

Entries whose button is not found are skipped with `continue`. Only 'none shown' is red. A regression that hides all but one page entry from the hosted sidebar passes.

*Fix:* Compare the shown set to the derived set, naming each entry that is not shown (with a recorded allowance list derived from Orca's own conditions, e.g. the orchestration setting).

**harness-live-panels-07: css-live side-panel and portal probes can pass having compared nothing**
`scripts/verify-orca-pane-css-live.mjs:95` · CONFIRMED · reported as medium

probeVerdict checks controlDelta, theiaChanged, paneChanged and portalChanged but never that anything was compared. In sideProbes, side.measured is set true as soon as both sidebar roots exist, even if the Add-project button is absent in both documents (the addReady poll failure is swallowed); the tooltip step then compares the keys common to both documents, which can be none, giving portalChanged [] with portalCompared 0. Missing Theia elements record 'absent' in both snapshots and compare equal. The portal probe has no planted control at all.

*Fix:* Require portalCompared >= 1 and that side-add exists in both documents; add a planted portal control that must register; require a minimum count of non-'absent' Theia snapshot keys.

**harness-live-panels-08: Pairing containment clauses read the subject's own state and one hand-named localStorage key**
`scripts/verify-orca-pane-standalone-live.mjs:145` · CONFIRMED · reported as medium

apiVerdict accepts 'session-only pairing' when window.PbPanes.state().pairing reports sessionOnly true and deviceKeySupport 'session-no-ed25519' (the bundle's own report), and checks only that localStorage key orca.web.runtimeEnvironment.v1 is absent. No scan covers other localStorage keys, sessionStorage or IndexedDB for the pairing material. The control (marker read-back) proves only that the key reader works.

*Fix:* Enumerate localStorage, sessionStorage and IndexedDB names and values on the page and assert the pairing string (read from the harness's in-memory copy) appears in none, with a planted control that writes it.

**harness-live-panels-09: gateRow counts a loading skeleton as mounted and hand-lists two kinds as empty by design**
`scripts/lib/orca-pane-harness.mjs:283` · CONFIRMED

gateMount counts every descendant of [data-pb-orca-pane-root]; a spinner or skeleton satisfies descendantCount > 0 (stage 1's mountOne rejects the 'waiting' state, the gate does not). EMPTY_BY_DESIGN is a hand-kept two-entry list: a mount of orchestration or page:creation that renders nothing because it is broken is recorded as mounted, with no check that Orca's own state (setting off, nothing pending) is the cause.

*Fix:* Reject the waiting marker as stage 1 does, and for empty-by-design kinds read the setting or state that justifies emptiness and fail when it does not hold.

**harness-live-panels-11: workspace-live readiness SKIPs on a source-pattern match that goes stale**
`scripts/verify-orca-workspace-live.mjs:184` · CONFIRMED

readiness returns a named SKIP (exit 0, counted PASS) when pb-pane-mount.tsx does not match /\bsubscribeOpenFiles\b/ and /\bsubscribeWorktrees\b/. The reason text says 'lane p65-01 not merged' but the condition is a regex on a file, so a rename or move silently disables the row.

*Fix:* Make this a FAIL now that the lane is merged.

**harness-live-panels-12: workspace-live Monaco absence is scanned only over a terminal pane and diff expectations come from Orca's own record**
`scripts/verify-orca-workspace-live.mjs:391` · CONFIRMED

Clause c's absence scan runs over [data-pb-orca-pane] roots after opening only a New Orca Terminal pane, so an Orca editor pane would not be in the scan set unless one happens to be mounted. In clause b, the expected diff sides' revisions (mergeBase, headOid, commitOid, parentOid) are taken from the record Orca produced, then the text is compared to git for those same revisions; a wrong merge base chosen by Orca is not detected although the fixture's merge base is known.

*Fix:* Derive mergeBase, parent and commit ids from the fixture repo with git (merge-base, rev-parse) and compare them to the record; scan every [data-pb-orca-pane] kind that Orca can mount for an editor, including a mounted editor pane as the positive case.

**harness-live-panels-13: pane-standalone-live degrades files.open failure to a log line and retries stage 0 once**
`scripts/verify-orca-pane-standalone-live.mjs:383` · CONFIRMED

If the CLI's files.open produces no record within 5 s, the check prints 'unreachable from the paired client' and switches to openPane('editor'); apiVerdict accepts either driver, and the 1 s bound applies only to files.open. Stage 0 (isolated per-leaf mount) is rerun once after a failure and passes if the second attempt is green.

*Fix:* Fail on a files.open miss (record the driver as a red clause), and report a stage 0 first-attempt failure as a flaky failure rather than green.

**harness-live-panels-14: Egress collector is blind to IP-literal and proxied destinations**
`scripts/verify-orca-side-panel-live.mjs:819` · PLAUSIBLE

Clause i parses 'Resolving host [...]' lines from nsHostResolver logs. The control only proves hostname lookups are visible. A fetch to an IP literal, through a proxy, or by DoH is not a resolver line for that host, so a hard-coded IP callout from the panels is never observed.

*Fix:* Add a second control with an IP-literal URL and count socket-level connects (MOZ_LOG=nsSocketTransport:5), or add a CSP/connect-src check on the panels.

**harness-live-panels-15: Failure message prints up to 80 characters of the real system clipboard**
`scripts/verify-page-furniture-live.mjs:1377` · CONFIRMED

The v1.7 change adds the clipboard value to the Copy Image Link failure text. With PB_LIVE_HEADED=1 on a Wayland session, clipboardText reads the desktop clipboard, so a failing run prints the first 80 characters of whatever the user last copied (a password or token) into the log or CI output. The same file writes 'pb-sentinel' and 'alpha' into that clipboard in headed mode.

*Fix:* Print only the length and whether it equals a known sentinel, or redact unless the value matches the expected URL shape.

**harness-live-panels-16: Harness static server crashes on a malformed URL and fixture git calls read the user's global git config**
`scripts/lib/orca-pane-harness.mjs:126` · CONFIRMED

The request handler calls decodeURIComponent on the raw path with no try/catch; a request to /%zz throws URIError inside the handler and terminates the verification process. Any local process can send it to the loopback port. Separately makeRepo (and workspace-live's buildFixtureRepo) run git with the real HOME, so the user's global config (commit.gpgsign, core.hooksPath, hooks) applies to the fixture commits.

*Fix:* Wrap the handler body in try/catch returning 400; run fixture git with GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null, GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 and -c commit.gpgsign=false -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null.

**harness-live-panels-17: Self-tests cover only the pure verdicts, not the live collectors that feed them**
`scripts/verify-orca-add-project-live.mjs:281` · CONFIRMED

add-project, workspace, landing-logo, side-panel and css-live self-tests exercise comparator functions on fixtures. None plants a fault into the collection code (the add-project steps, the width-tab loop, the Theia snapshot key set, the pane scan set), so a collector that stops measuring cannot make the self-test red. The vacuous loops found above (web tabs, side probe compared count) are not reachable by any self-test case.

*Fix:* For each live collector add a fixture-driven or planted-fault self-test (an observation with zero compared items or zero actions executed must be red), and give each verdict an explicit 'nothing measured' clause.

### harness-other

**harness-other-01: Privileged agent kinds (webTabAgent, webTabAgentStop) are fenced only inside three extension directories; senders in orca-panes, modes or orca-agents are never read**
`scripts/verify-web-tab-bridge.mjs:795` · CONFIRMED · reported as medium

The privileged-kind rule for the two v1.7 agent kinds claims that webTabAgent is sent only from orca-browser-frontend-module.ts and that webTabAgentStop (WebTabWidget.agentStop()) is called only from the chrome bar's Stop click handler (P66-21). Both rules run only over the files readSources() loads: chrome-bar/src/browser, tab-uris/src/browser, orca-browser/src and a few named files. theia/extensions/orca-panes (orca-pane-observer.ts, orca-pane-routes.ts) and theia/extensions/modes (group-model.ts, setups-service.ts, organising-widget.ts) already import WebTabWidget and can reach its public request() channel, and orca-agents is next to them. None of these are scanned, so the fence has a blind spot exactly where a second sender is most likely to appear.

*Fix:* For the sender and caller fences, read every .ts/.tsx under theia/extensions/*/src (derive the set from the directory listing, not a hand-kept list). Add plants that put a webTabAgent literal and an agentStop( call in an orca-panes fixture file and require each to go red.

**harness-other-04: Release licence-rulings guard is checked only for position: continue-on-error or '|| true' on it stays green, and the Windows guard is not checked at all**
`scripts/verify-release-shape.mjs:560` · CONFIRMED · reported as medium

Rule 12 (P68-21: no release carries Orca's closure while an owner licence ruling is pending) checks only that a step whose run text contains `scripts/verify-licence-allowlist.mjs --rulings` comes before the build-orca.sh step in build-linux. The rulings step is not passed through failOpenFaults(), which the script applies to the SHA gate, the key gate and the purge step. A `continue-on-error: true`, an `|| true`, a `set +e` or a changed `if:` on it defeats the guard with rule 12 still green. The same guard in build-windows (release.yml:576-582) is checked by no rule in verify-release-shape.mjs or verify-build-windows-shape.mjs, because neither file mentions 'rulings' outside rule 12's build-linux scope.

*Fix:* Apply failOpenFaults(step, "steps.key_gate.outputs.build == 'true'") to the rulings step. Add the same position and fail-open rule for build-windows in verify-build-windows-shape.mjs. Add plants for continue-on-error, '|| true' and removal of the Windows guard.

**harness-other-05: Quick-tier checks for bundled extensions, the bundled XPI and Orca licences skip their substantive legs in CI and still exit PASS**
`scripts/verify-bundled-xpi.mjs:212` · CONFIRMED

These scripts print one SKIP line for each leg whose input is not on disk, then exit 0, and the driver counts exit 0 as PASS. verify.yml runs --quick over a built Theia with no fetched orca/ and no .mozbuild/bundled-xpi cache. In that setting: (1) bundled-xpi skips the cached XPI's sha256, manifest id and version, signature presence, and the D-85 endpoint leg (every default-on uBlock filter-list host must have an allow row); (2) bundled-extensions skips the Orca pnpm-lock equality leg (verify-bundled-extensions.mjs:120) and possibly the LICENSE quotation leg (:126); (3) licence-allowlist skips the per-package Orca licence rule (verify-licence-allowlist.mjs:502), the bundled XPI licence line (:303) and the LICENSE digests of absent trees (:115). Each SKIP is named, but in --quick and CI these legs never go red. stage-bundled-xpi.sh re-checks sha256 when it stages, and rebase-orca.sh runs licence-allowlist with SKIP treated as red, so integrity has other guards. The D-85 endpoint-allowlist leg and the SDK lock-equality leg have none in CI.

*Fix:* Either stage the pinned XPI in verify.yml (the download is sha256-pinned) so the cached legs run in CI, or give a SKIP its own exit code that the driver reports as SKIP, and make --gate refuse a SKIP for rows whose input CI can produce. At minimum, derive the endpoint leg's host set from a tracked copy of assets.json, so it does not depend on the cache.

**harness-other-06: Orca failure-page copy scope SKIPs, and the row passes, if orca-panes' failure/ directory moves**
`scripts/verify-shell-error-copy.mjs:1929` · CONFIRMED

The 67-03 Orca hosting copy check (the user-facing copy rule: no internal identifier, names PowerBrowser, each sentence names exactly the buttons it renders) runs only while theia/extensions/orca-panes/src/browser/failure/ exists. If that directory is renamed or moved, both the main run and the self-test print a SKIP line and the row passes. The reinstall-copy scope added in the same diff fails when its sources are missing, so the two scopes are inconsistent. The Orca scope is now in the tree, so a missing directory is drift, not a lane that has not merged yet.

*Fix:* Make the absent failure/ directory a FAIL whenever theia/extensions/orca-panes exists, as the reinstall scope already does. Or locate the copy table by the ORCA_FAILURE_COPY export across orca-panes/src, not by a fixed path.

**harness-other-07: Agent-contract exemption in the web-tab bridge check reactivates silently if orca-browser/src disappears**
`scripts/verify-web-tab-bridge.mjs:58` · CONFIRMED

Lane-merge scaffolding that is still in place: when no file under theia/extensions/orca-browser/src exists, the kinds parity, push parity and privileged-kind rules leave out webTabAgent, webTabAgentStop and agentControlChanged. orca-browser is merged now, so the exemption is off. If the extension is renamed or moved, chrome's privileged webTabAgent arm, which drives a page on an agent's behalf, drops out of the parity and owner-file fences again, and the only signal is a note on the PASS line.

*Fix:* Remove the lane-merge skip now that orca-browser is merged. A missing orca-browser source while PowerBrowserAPI serves webTabAgent should be a FAIL naming the owner file.

**harness-other-08: Permanent boundary gate now admits Cc[ and Ci. in the actor child by File:Line alone; the XPCOM contract on a catalogued line is not pinned**
`scripts/check-internals-boundary.sh:118` · CONFIRMED

This is a new exemption in a permanent gate. The reason is in the code (A2, P66-13: nsITextInputProcessor and nsIConsoleAPIStorage). The waiver is wider than that reason. catalogue_has_row matches only a table row whose File:Line cell equals GroupActorChild.sys.mjs:<n>, so any XPCOM contract or interface on a catalogued line is admitted. Today line 675 creates a system principal in the content process (Cc["@mozilla.org/systemprincipal;1"].createInstance(Ci.nsIPrincipal)), in the actor that agent-driven pages run beside. A later edit that changes the contract on lines 293, 674 or 675 without moving them stays green in the boundary scan and in ng-040, which checks the line and the method name, not the contract. The new self-test plant for Services. uses an uncatalogued line, so it does not prove the comment's claim that a Services. line is an offense 'whatever the catalogue says'.

*Fix:* For Cc[/Ci. waivers in the actor child, require the row's first cell to name the exact contract ID and interface found on the line, and compare them. Add a plant that keeps the line number but swaps the contract, and one that catalogues a Services. line and requires red.

**harness-other-09: Windows native check exempts every i386 .exe outside orca/, not just the NSIS helper**
`scripts/verify-package-target-natives.mjs:83` · CONFIRMED

The comment justifies one exception, the NSIS uninstall/helper.exe, which is 32-bit by design. isInstallerHelper instead exempts any .exe outside orca/ whose PE machine is 0x014c, other than node.exe and rg.exe, wherever it sits in the stage. No self-test case shows that an i386 .exe at another path (for example under the Theia sidecar) still goes red, or that helper.exe is exempted (a positive control).

*Fix:* Limit the exemption to the NSIS helper's actual staged path (derive it from the NSIS script or package-windows.sh). Add plants: an i386 .exe elsewhere outside orca/ must be red, and helper.exe at its path must be green.

**harness-other-11: Snapshot fixture generator runs the pinned agent-browser with the developer's full environment and HOME config**
`scripts/gen-orca-snapshot-fixtures.mjs:93` · CONFIRMED

The generator runs orca/node_modules/agent-browser (third-party, from the Orca pin) with { ...process.env }, so every AGENT_BROWSER_* variable in the developer's shell (PROFILE, AUTO_CONNECT, INIT_SCRIPTS, EXTENSIONS, PROXY, CONFIG) and ~/.agent-browser/config.json under the real HOME apply to the run. The committed reference fixtures, which verify-orca-browser-snapshot-live compares PowerBrowser's snapshot against, then depend on ambient configuration. A set AGENT_BROWSER_AUTO_CONNECT or AGENT_BROWSER_PROFILE drives the developer's real browser profile, not a fresh Chromium.

*Fix:* Build the child env from an allowlist (PATH, plus a scratch HOME and XDG dirs from mkdtemp), drop every inherited AGENT_BROWSER_* variable except EXECUTABLE_PATH, and remove the scratch HOME afterwards.

## Info findings (13)

Each entry gives the defect and the suggested fix. The scenario, evidence and verifier reasoning are in the findings JSON next to this report.

### orcad-hosting

**orcad-hosting-09: The environment allowlist passes the SSH agent socket, the session bus and proxy settings to every skip-permissions agent**
`powerbrowser/shell/OrcaEnv.sys.mjs:53` · CONFIRMED · cites D-12

ORCAD_ENV_ALLOWLIST correctly keeps POWERBROWSER_* secrets, NODE_OPTIONS and foreign ORCA_* names away from orcad. It deliberately passes SSH_AUTH_SOCK, DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS, DISPLAY/WAYLAND_DISPLAY and the HTTP(S)_PROXY values, which may hold user:password credentials, to orcad and through it to every agent, and agents run with skip-permissions (D-12). A prompt-injected agent can therefore use the user's loaded SSH keys and the unlocked Secret Service keyring over the session bus without any further step. A same-uid process can reach most of these anyway, so this is not a boundary crossing. It is worth recording that the allowlist is a hygiene filter, not a containment boundary for agents.

*Fix:* Record the agent capability set in the threat model and docs. If containment is ever wanted, gate SSH_AUTH_SOCK and DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS behind a user setting.

### pairing-credentials

**pairing-credentials-02: The offer endpoint's port check compares the endpoint with a port read from the same file, so T-67-13's 'equal to orcad's port' check enforces nothing**
`theia/extensions/orca-panes/src/node/offer-file-policy.ts:188` · CONFIRMED · reported as low

offerEndpointVerdict is meant to accept only orcad's own port (T-67-13: 'equal to orcad's port when known'). The port it compares with comes from offerPortOf(pairing), which parses the same untrusted text. The policy also accepts a bare offer that is not in URL form, and then no port is checked at all. Anyone who can write web-pairing can therefore point the hosted Orca client at any loopback port from 1024 to 32767, along with a publicKeyB64 of their choice. A listener on that port then serves the Orca runtime protocol to a client that renders inside the privileged Theia origin. The backend never learns the real orcad port independently, although chrome knows it (OrcaService._port).

*Fix:* Give the backend orcad's port from an independent source. One option is a separate chrome-written bridge file (orca-port), or the value TheiaService already passes through POWERBROWSER_ENV. Require the endpoint to equal that port, and refuse a bare offer that is not in URL form. Write the offer's own fields the same way and compare them, rather than trusting the text.

**pairing-credentials-05: orca-panes-dir, which picks the JavaScript executed in the privileged Theia origin, is read with less hardening than the offer and its target folder is never checked**
`theia/extensions/orca-panes/src/node/orca-panes-backend-module.ts:120` · CONFIRMED · reported as low

The offer path uses lstat, then an O_NOFOLLOW open with an fstat inode match. The orca-panes-dir path uses lstatSync and then readFileSync by path, which follows a symlink swapped in after the lstat. The folder named in the file is realpath'd and served with no check of its owner or mode, or of the files in it. Its pb-panes.js runs as a module script in the Theia document, which holds the backend token cookie and the Orca pairing. This makes it the most sensitive file in the bridge hand-off, and it gets the weakest check.

*Fix:* Read orca-panes-dir through readNoFollow. Require the bundle folder realpath to be under the realpath of the Orca web client root that chrome resolved. Refuse a folder or entry file that is writable by group or other, or owned by another uid other than root.

### chrome-actor

**chrome-actor-07: INTERNAL-APIS.md and the actor-child header no longer describe GroupActorChild's privileged reach; v1.7 child touchpoints are uncatalogued**
`powerbrowser/INTERNAL-APIS.md:164` · CONFIRMED · reported as low

Several v1.7 Firefox-internal touchpoints in GroupActorChild.sys.mjs have no catalogue row. In the agent child: window.synthesizeMouseEvent (trusted pointer input), windowUtils.sendWheelEvent and windowUtils.sendContentCommandEvent('insertText'). In the edit-state child: the new Do and Target arms read the stock ContextMenu actor's internal `target` (`this.manager.getActor("ContextMenu").target.controllers`) and run controller.doCommand or docShell.doCommand. The PowerBrowserEditStateChild row still says it 'answers one boolean ... no DOM write', but the child now executes edit commands. The file header still says the file has 'ZERO privileged reach' and 'loads solely in documents matching the Theia local origin ... so stock-window web content never runs it'. In fact the agent child creates a system principal, and the OrcaLogo child loads in every loopback top document, stock windows included. The boundary check does not catch these because its child patterns are only wrappedJSObject, JSWindowActorChild, Cc[ and Ci.

*Fix:* Add catalogue rows for each of these child touchpoints, update the EditState and file-header claims, and add windowUtils., synthesize and getActor( to the check's actor-child patterns so new occurrences need a row.

### agent-browser

**agent-browser-07: connectionWhenReady adds a window listener every second while the link is not ready**
`theia/extensions/orca-browser/src/browser/orca-browser-frontend-module.ts:323` · PLAUSIBLE · reported as low

Each retry adds a {once:true} LINK_EVENT listener and a 1 s timer. When the timer wins, the listener stays registered. The link dispatches LINK_EVENT only once, and only when Orca's status turns ok. While orcad stays unavailable (the Phase 67 failure state), listeners build up at one per second for the life of the Theia document.

*Fix:* Remove the listener when the timer fires (keep a named handler and call removeEventListener in the timer callback), or wait on one shared listener.

### overlay-renderer

**overlay-renderer-05: Runtime hooks on Orca's window.api are not recorded as seams**
`orca-overlay/seams.json:2` · CONFIRMED · cites D-26

seams.json lists the three patches, and each patch touches exactly the one file and upstream blob it names. They match. The overlay also changes upstream behaviour at runtime in ways the seam list does not record: staleNavigationEchoes replaces window.api.runtimeEnvironments.subscribe and rewrites publicationEpoch and snapshotVersion; observePrimarySelectionPasteChoice replaces window.api.settings.set; hostedTerminalKeys overrides settings.terminalShortcutPolicy, primarySelectionMiddleClickPaste and two keybindings in the store on every change; pb-browser-host-link replaces api.prepareBrowserClientHostPlacement; presetSessionOnly calls Orca's test-only setWebDeviceKeySupportForTests. A weekly re-pin (D-26) can change the shapes these hooks rely on, and no seam row flags them for review. The session-only path fails closed (assertSessionOnly), but the epoch rewrite does not.

*Fix:* Add a runtime-hook section to seams.json (or a sibling inventory) naming each upstream symbol the overlay wraps or overrides and the upstream file it lives in, and have the re-pin job diff those files as it diffs patch blobs.

**overlay-renderer-06: Orca's whole renderer runs in the authenticated Theia document**
`orca-overlay/files/src/renderer/src/pb/pb-pane-mount.tsx:12` · CONFIRMED · cites D-41

The bundle imports Orca's main.css and mounts the Sidebar, RightSidebar, Settings, Tasks, Automations, Skills, Artifacts, NativeChatView, the dialogs and AppBackgroundServices into the Theia document. That document holds the Theia token channel and the PowerBrowser chrome actors. In the overlay files I found no HTML sink fed by Orca, repository, terminal or agent data: no innerHTML, dangerouslySetInnerHTML, eval or computed import. The upstream sinks I traced (markdown preview, CommentMarkdown, Mermaid, the chat visual frame) sanitize their input or isolate it in a sandbox='allow-scripts' srcdoc frame. Agent visual links are restricted to http and https. There is still no second boundary: any future upstream renderer XSS reached through agent output, issue or PR text, or file names yields script in the Theia origin. T-64-12 accepted this, and D-41 ratifies Phase 64's recorded picks.

*Fix:* Keep the accepted risk visible: have the weekly re-pin grep the pinned renderer for new dangerouslySetInnerHTML, innerHTML, rehypeRaw-without-sanitize and srcdoc-without-sandbox uses, and set a CSP on the Theia document that forbids inline and remote script except the pane-bundle route.

### agents-extensions

**agents-extensions-04: Pre-installed uBlock Origin never updates itself; it stays at the shipped version until a PowerBrowser release bumps the pin**
`powerbrowser/packaging/bundled-xpi.json:6` · CONFIRMED

The XPI is pinned by AMO URL and SHA-256. stage-bundled-xpi.sh fetches it over HTTPS, checks the hash on every staging, deletes the file and fails on a mismatch, and copies it unmodified into distribution/extensions/. There is no ExtensionSettings policy, so the user can remove it, and Gecko's extensions.installedDistroAddon pref keeps a removed copy removed. A newer XPI in a later PowerBrowser release upgrades an existing copy when the app version changes. The branding prefs set extensions.update.autoUpdateDefault=false, so the AMO background check finds updates but never installs them. A security fix in uBlock Origin (a privileged add-on with access to all pages) reaches users only through a PowerBrowser release or a manual update.

*Fix:* Make the uBlock Origin pin part of the weekly re-pin job, or set a per-add-on auto-update override for the distribution add-on, so security updates do not depend on a full release.

**agents-extensions-05: Agent hooks are upstream orcad's: it rewrites the user's real ~/.claude/settings.json and ~/.orca/agent-hooks, and the hook endpoint token is shared by every pane**
`theia/extensions/orca-agents/bin/claude-wrapper.mjs:102` · CONFIRMED · cites D-13

PowerBrowser registers no hook of its own, and the wrapper writes no settings, hooks or credentials. It only sets ORCA_PANE_KEY, ORCA_TAB_ID, ORCA_WORKTREE_ID and ORCA_AGENT_HOOK_ENDPOINT=<dataRoot>/agent-hooks/endpoint.env, and it strips every inherited ORCA_* name. The hooks come from upstream orcad: startOrcadManagedAgentHooks reconciles managed hooks into the real HOME, because HOME is on ORCAD_ENV_ALLOWLIST. The hook command runs "$HOME/.orca/agent-hooks/claude-hook.sh", which `.`-sources the file ORCA_AGENT_HOOK_ENDPOINT names. Two consequences follow. (1) Running PowerBrowser edits the user's ~/.claude/settings.json and the ~/.orca/agent-hooks scripts that an installed Orca desktop also manages; a version skew between the two is handled only by upstream's owner and lock logic.
