(2) endpoint.env holds one port and token for all panes. claude, and every tool process it runs (skip-permissions on, D-12), can read it and post status for any paneKey, so a prompt-injected agent can spoof other panes' status. Hook payloads are parsed by upstream's server, not by PowerBrowser code.

*Fix:* Record in INTERNAL-APIS.md or DECISIONS.md that PowerBrowser's orcad manages the user's real agent hook configuration. If panes must not spoof each other, upstream would need per-pane hook tokens.

### ci-supply-chain

**ci-supply-chain-05: sign-windows signs Orca PE files from the legion zip after matching names only, although the admitted SHA256SUMS carries a digest for each file**
`scripts/win-sign-list.mjs:161` · CONFIRMED · reported as low

sign-windows admits windows-orca/SHA256SUMS against the hosted job's digest output. orcaChecks then compares the PE files under orca/ in the legion-built zip with the SHA256SUMS entries by path only. The per-file sha256 values in the admitted manifest are parsed and thrown away, and the staged bytes are never hashed against them. legion (persistent, and now running Orca's Linux build) can therefore swap the content of any Orca PE file and have it Authenticode-signed under the project identity. Signing is currently skipped (no Azure account, R14), so this is latent.

*Fix:* In orcaChecks, keep the digest from each SHA256SUMS line and require sha256(<pkg>/orca/<path>) to equal it for every staged orca/ file, PE or not. Fail before signing on any mismatch.

### packaging-prefs-network

**packaging-prefs-network-07: The GPL-3.0 source offer for the bundled uBlock XPI points only at a third-party GitHub tag**
`inventory/licence-allowlist.json:72` · CONFIRMED · cites D-44

The GPL-3.0 obligation for the distributed uBlock object code is met only by a link to https://github.com/gorhill/uBlock/tree/1.76.0 in THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt. GPL-3.0 section 6(d) requires the distributor to keep the Corresponding Source available for as long as it offers the object code. A link to a repository someone else controls does not ensure that.

*Fix:* Mirror the source tarball for each pinned version beside the release assets (for example on downloads.powerbrowser.org or the Codeberg mirror) and point the notice at that copy.

### harness-live-tabs

**harness-live-tabs-15: Real-agent run is refused, not skipped, near sign-in expiry (correct)**
`scripts/lib/orca-tabs-live.mjs:222` · CONFIRMED · cites D-30

For agent 'real', seedClaudeLogin() returns a reason when the access token has under 60 minutes left or no sign-in exists; withOrcaTabsSession throws, main() catches and prints 'FAIL -- harness: the real agent cannot run: ...' and exits 1. orca-tabs-live therefore cannot go green on a run that did not exercise the agent. Stages f and g2 additionally go red through agentVerdict when claude or the SDK directory is absent. mode-switch-tabs-live uses the stub agent and its Orca seeds print a named SKIP only while theia/extensions/orca-panes is absent, red when it is present but unwired.

*Fix:* none

### harness-live-panels

**harness-live-panels-18: landing-logo records the gh call count but asserts nothing about it**
`scripts/verify-orca-landing-logo-live.mjs:282` · CONFIRMED

The stub gh on PATH counts calls from orcad (P67-27, 'orcad inherits PATH through its allowlist') but the count is only printed as 'recorded'. If the requirement is that orcad's Landing makes no gh call, nothing can go red.

*Fix:* If P67-27 requires a bounded or zero gh count, assert it with a planted control (the stub called directly once must register).

## Refuted findings (11)

A second agent read the cited code and found the claim wrong or blocked by a guard. They are listed so the reasoning can be checked; none needs action unless the refutation is itself wrong.

**orcad-hosting-04: The terminal daemon's identity is taken from whatever process holds the reported pid now, not from the daemon's own record** (reported as low)
`powerbrowser/shell/OrcaService.sys.mjs:504`

*Refutation:* The stated scenario (a stale daemon pid record after a reboot, reported while the daemon fails to come up) cannot occur. orcad-health.ts collectTerminalDaemonHealth returns pid:null whenever getDaemonEndpointFacts() is null. getDaemonEndpointFacts (daemon-provider-state.ts:53-56) is null unless an adapter is installed. installDaemonProvider runs only at daemon-provider-init.ts:170, after newSpawner.ensureRunning() succeeded at :75. ensureRunning either adopts a daemon that answers on its socket or spawns a new one, and the new daemon writes its own pid record. After a reboot, no old daemon answers, so the record is fresh. If daemon start fails, initDaemonPtyProvider throws before the install, startOrcadDaemon returns 'unavailable', and the reported pid is null. That leaves a residual race: the adopted or spawned daemon crashes in the few seconds between adoption and OrcaService's readProcessStartTicks at :504, and its pid is reused in that window. That is far narrower than the finding claims and is not the stated path.

*Guard:* orca/src/main/daemon/daemon-provider-init.ts:75 (ensureRunning) and :170 (installDaemonProvider only after success). daemon-provider-state.ts:53-56 (no adapter means no facts). orcad-health.ts collectTerminalDaemonHealth returns pid:null when facts are null.

**sidecar-gate-backends-04: The served Orca bundle directory is not checked for ownership or mode, but its files run as script in the sidecar origin** (reported as low)
`theia/extensions/orca-panes/src/node/orca-panes-backend-module.ts:215`

*Refutation:* The code matches the description: bundleRootReal realpaths the pointed-to directory without checking its owner or mode (orca-panes-backend-module.ts:215, 246-251). The precondition, however, is equivalent to the capability gained. The pointer always names `${orcaRoot}/out/web/assets/pb-panes` (OrcaService.sys.mjs:559, 567), with orcaRoot being `${appDir}/orca` beside the PB binary or the powerbrowser.orca.root pref (OrcaService.sys.mjs:218-224). That same tree holds out/orcad/orcad.js, which PB runs with node as the user, and out/web, which orcad serves as its own web client. Anyone who can write into the bundle directory or an ancestor can already replace orcad.js and get code execution as the user without going through the Theia origin. The described scenarios are a group-writable checkout or a lax /opt tree, and in both the whole Orca install tree is writable.

*Guard:* OrcaService.sys.mjs:218-224 (orcaRoot, orcadMain = ${orcaRoot}/out/orcad/orcad.js, webClientRoot = ${orcaRoot}/out/web) and :559 (panesDir = ${webClientRoot}/assets/pb-panes): the served directory sits inside the tree whose orcad.js PB executes as the user, so write access to it is already code execution.

**sidecar-gate-backends-05: OrcaGitService accepts any repository on disk as a root; the Orca-roots restriction exists only in the frontend** (reported as info)
`theia/extensions/orca-workspace/src/node/orca-workspace-backend-module.ts:55`

*Refutation:* The code facts hold. validateRoot (orca-git-protocol.ts:61-70) checks only shape, realpath and toplevel. The Orca-root restriction is in the frontend OrcaGitResourceResolver.trustedRoots/resolve (orca-git-resource.ts:55-70). routeHelpers calls resolveHead(file.worktreePath) directly (orca-editor-routes.ts:400). Both stated reasons for recording the finding fail. First, the comment does not overstate: orca-workspace-backend-module.ts:6-7 says the backend validates that 'the root is an absolute, realpath-stable repository toplevel', which is exactly what validateRoot does, and it makes no claim of an Orca-root restriction. Second, the precondition, code already running in the authenticated Theia frontend, already holds Theia's filesystem service and terminal/process spawn over the same socket. That code can read any user-readable file and run git directly. readBlob adds no capability.

*Guard:* orca-workspace-backend-module.ts:6-7 (the comment matches validateRoot at orca-git-protocol.ts:61-70). Precondition equivalence: the authenticated frontend already has Theia FileSystem and terminal RPC, so it can run `git cat-file` itself.

**chrome-actor-01: Orca 'navigate' commands reach agent tabs through the user webTabNavigate kind, which skips the agent wall, so Stop does not stop navigation** (reported as medium)
`powerbrowser/shell/PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:2816`

*Refutation:* The chrome half is described correctly. webTabNavigate (PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:2816-2821, 4145-4169) checks only the tabId shape and the http(s) scheme. It has no agentControl.stopped, entry.agent or owner check, and it never lights the D-19 mark. The PB dispatcher does send Orca's 'navigate' through widget.navigate (orca-browser-dispatch.ts:148-151, web-tab.ts:1221). The claimed bypass needs orcad to send 'navigate' to a page that is already bound and that the user has stopped, and I found no path in upstream Orca that does that. Outside tests, only two places issue a navigate command. (1) browser-client-page-opening.ts:106-113 navigates right after createRuntimeBrowserClientPage, which is a new page and so a fresh, unstopped tab. createPage for an id that is already bound is refused at orca-browser-dispatch.ts:124-126; createRuntimeBrowserClientPage then throws, and no navigate follows. (2) runtime-browser-client-page-recovery.ts:183-203 first closes the page (closeUnavailablePage, or retirement), then sends createPage, then navigates the new tab. The PB dispatcher's closePage drops and closes the stopped tab (dispatch.ts:134-142), so the navigate reaches a new tab. browser.goto and every other agent navigation go through 'automation', which reaches the wall at PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11758. A stopped tab therefore never receives an Orca navigate. Stop is defined per tab, so a fresh tab is a new lifetime, and an agent can open one anyway with 'tab new'. What remains is an observation: the initial URL load of a new agent tab does not light the D-19 mark.

*Guard:* orca-browser-dispatch.ts:124-126 refuses createPage for a bound id. orca/src/main/runtime/browser/browser-client-page-opening.ts:106-113 navigates only a page it just created. orca/src/main/runtime/runtime-browser-client-page-recovery.ts:171-203 closes (via closeUnavailablePage) and re-creates before it navigates. No other non-test issuer of { type: 'navigate' } exists in orca/src/main.

**agent-browser-01: browser.fill on an <input type=file> attaches any local file, because the value is set with system-principal privileges** (reported as high)
`powerbrowser/shell/GroupActorChild.sys.mjs:567`

*Refutation:* Most of the cited path checks out. The browser.fill row (GroupActorChild.sys.mjs:560-569) writes `element.value = params.value`. agentEnabled (line 240) checks only `disabled`, and nothing checks the input type. HTMLInputElement's value setter is NeedsCallerType (HTMLInputElement.webidl:98). A write from a system-principal JSWindowActorChild therefore reaches SetValue with CallerType::System, and SetValue then calls MozSetFileNameArray for a non-empty value (HTMLInputElement.cpp:1828-1843).

The finding stops at that call. MozSetFileNameArray throws NS_ERROR_DOM_NOT_SUPPORTED_ERR whenever XRE_IsContentProcess() is true, before any nsIFile or File is created (upstream/dom/html/HTMLInputElement.cpp:2373-2376).

The agent overlay is a content-process browser. PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:3607-3620 sets remote="true" and remoteType "web" (or the opener's remote type) on every web tab, agent overlays included. agentRunInChild (PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11716) gets the actor from that browser's currentWindowGlobal. registerAgentActor (11841-11846) limits the actor to the agent message manager group. So PowerBrowserAgentChild runs in the web content process that hosts the hostile page. No pref in the PB tree turns off e10s or Fission (a grep for remote.autostart and fission.autostart outside upstream/ and orca/ found nothing).

A web page cannot run in the parent process, even with maychangeremoteness. On a file input, then, the setter throws. The row returns a failure (browser_error), and the input is never given a File for the path. The Gecko check that blocks MozSetFileNameArray in a content process is the guard, so the browser.upload "unavailable" decision is not bypassed.

What remains is hardening only. The fill row could refuse type=file explicitly, so the refusal does not depend on that Gecko check.

*Guard:* upstream/dom/html/HTMLInputElement.cpp:2373-2376 (MozSetFileNameArray throws NS_ERROR_DOM_NOT_SUPPORTED_ERR when XRE_IsContentProcess()); powerbrowser/shell/PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:3607-3620 (agent overlay browser is remote="true", remoteType "web", so the actor child runs in a content process)

**agent-browser-05: Agent tabs from every worktree are one pool: PowerBrowser never checks a command's worktree against the page's binding** (reported as low)
`theia/extensions/orca-browser/src/common/orca-browser-dispatch.ts:144`

*Refutation:* The code matches the description: dispatchCommand resolves table.tabOf(pageId) with no worktree comparison (orca-browser-dispatch.ts:144-160). But the worktree is not a privilege boundary an agent is held to, so the precondition already grants what is gained (check 4). In upstream orcad the worktree of a browser command is whatever the caller passes. resolveBrowserCommandTarget resolves params.worktree through resolveWorkspaceSelector for any selector (orca/src/main/runtime/browser/browser-command-targets.ts:111-113), and an absent worktree with an explicit page id is unscoped by design (:126-129). An agent in worktree A can therefore name worktree B outright, with tab list and every page command. It can also run the orca CLI with any worktree selector from its shell. PowerBrowser adding a check that the caller controls would not create separation. The shared agent container (agent-browser-03) is the real cross-worktree exposure.

*Guard:* orca/src/main/runtime/browser/browser-command-targets.ts:111-113: the worktree is caller-supplied and any selector resolves. :126-129: explicit page ids are unscoped upstream. Worktree scope is not an access control an agent is bound by.

**agent-browser-08: A losing concurrent createPage removes the winning page's stored binding** (reported as low)
`theia/extensions/orca-browser/src/browser/orca-browser-frontend-module.ts:377`

*Refutation:* The Theia-side ordering is as described. If two createPage events for one id overlapped, the loser's openAgentTab would write bound and sessionStorage and connect onTabClosed (orca-browser-frontend-module.ts:374-382). dispatchCommand would then close the loser's tab (orca-browser-dispatch.ts:129-131), and onTabClosed would delete the winner's sessionStorage entry and its this.bound entry (:399-405). The precondition is blocked upstream. orcad reserves a page id before it issues createPage, and reserveClientPage throws browser_page_reconciliation_reservation_pending while a reservation for that id exists. A second createPage for the same id cannot be issued while the first is outstanding. I found no path that replays a createPage after a reattach. A reattach uses a new pb-host-<uuid> client id and a new lease, and an uncommitted page is closed (closeUncommittedClientPage), not re-created. The only residual path is a caller-chosen page id (browser-client-page-opening.ts:75, params.page) retried after the first create timed out, while PB's local openAgentTab is still pending. That needs openAgentTab to outlast orcad's creation timeout, which is not a realistic overlap. The defect is latent hardening at most.

*Guard:* orca/src/main/runtime/browser-host/browser-host-page-placement.ts:87-88 `if (this.reservationsByPageId.has(browserPageId)) throw new Error('browser_page_reconciliation_reservation_pending')`, reached from browser-host-client-page-creation.ts:61 before createPage is issued at :77.

**theia-pane-hosting-06: Layout-restored OrcaPaneOptions are not validated; a prototype-key kind reaches Orca's title lookup** (reported as low)
`theia/extensions/orca-panes/src/browser/orca-pane-widget.tsx:88`

*Refutation:* The described code behaviour is accurate. The factory (orca-panes-frontend-module.ts:171-178) binds the replayed options without validation. For a tabless widget, init (orca-pane-widget.tsx:85-92) passes options.kind to viewTitle, and viewTitle (orca-overlay/files/src/renderer/src/pb/pb-workspace-api.ts:225-227) indexes a plain object literal, so 'constructor' returns Object() = {}, which is truthy. It fails check (4). The only actor who can supply such a kind is one with write access to the same user's profile shell state, and that actor can already damage or replace the UI and the profile directly. Version drift from another build does not trigger it: drifted kinds are real route strings such as 'page:x', which return undefined and then ''. The result is self-inflicted rendering damage, with no boundary crossed. As hardening, an own-property check in viewTitle or route validation in the factory is still reasonable.

*Guard:* Precondition is equivalent to the capability: write access to the same user's profile shell state. Drift does not produce prototype keys, because pb-workspace-api.ts:226 returns '' for any non-prototype unknown kind.

**harness-static-hosting-09: orca-routes scan matches only a receiver literally named app, so a route on another receiver escapes the gate rule** (reported as low)
`scripts/verify-sidecar-origin-gate.mjs:231`

*Refutation:* The scan does match only receivers named app (verify-sidecar-origin-gate.mjs:229-231). The finding misreads it in two places. First, `this.app.get(` does match `\bapp\.`, because '.' to 'a' is a word boundary, so that example is caught and judged. Second, and decisively, gate() is not tied to configure(). PowerBrowserTokenGateContribution.initialize unshifts gate into EarlyExpressMiddleware.handlers (token-gate-backend-contribution.ts:82), and Theia's BackendApplication.configure applies those handlers before any contribution's configure(app) (theia/node_modules/@theia/core/lib/node/backend-application.js:145-162). Any route or Router later added to that Express app, in configure, onStart or anywhere else, sits behind gate() in the stack. The only ways ahead of the gate are early middleware and raw request/upgrade listeners, and the same rule checks both (verify-sidecar-origin-gate.mjs:243-247). A Router route the scan misses therefore does not escape the gate. The gap is only a less complete route list.

*Guard:* theia/extensions/token-gate/src/node/token-gate-backend-contribution.ts:82 (earlyMiddleware.handlers.unshift(gate)); theia/node_modules/@theia/core/lib/node/backend-application.js:145-162; scripts/verify-sidecar-origin-gate.mjs:243-247 (EARLY HANDLER and RAW LISTENER rules)

**harness-other-02: pb-orca URI rows SKIP, and the run exits 0, when orcad or the pane bundle fails to boot** (reported as medium)
`scripts/verify-uri-roundtrip.mjs:419`

*Refutation:* The pb-orca rows run inside lib.withOrcaTabsSession() with needOrca defaulting to true (scripts/lib/orca-tabs-live.mjs:199). Before fn(ctx) runs, that session polls OrcaService for 90 s. It throws if the state is 'failed', and pollFor throws on timeout (scripts/lib/pb-relaunch.mjs:139). It then runs BOOT_WAIT and throws 'the Orca pane bundle did not boot' if booted is false (orca-tabs-live.mjs:299-304). These throws are caught at scripts/verify-uri-roundtrip.mjs:550-551 and become { ok: false } rows, which set failCount and exit 1. The scenarios named in the finding (orcad exits at start, or the pane bundle throws before booted is set) therefore produce FAIL, not SKIP. The skip at :419 is reached only after the session has already confirmed state().booted, so it is effectively dead code for those scenarios. The one remaining case is booted turning false between the session's check and the in-page check, which I cannot settle statically and which is not the claimed path.

*Guard:* scripts/lib/orca-tabs-live.mjs:299-304 (throws on OrcaService not ready / bundle not booted); scripts/lib/pb-relaunch.mjs:139 (pollFor throws on timeout); scripts/verify-uri-roundtrip.mjs:550-551 (catch -> ok:false row)

**harness-other-10: bundled-xpi live plant sessions are never registered; the live row's discrimination is proven only by a static audit** (reported as low)
`scripts/verify-bundled-xpi.mjs:610`

*Refutation:* Part of the finding is true. The LIVE_PLANTS sessions run only under --self-test --live (:479-486), and verify-platform.sh registers only --self-test (:4167, the static liveAuditSelfTest path via selfTest()), --packaged (:6922) and --live (:6927). The stated scenario, though, is caught by the registered bundled-xpi-live control session itself, because that session asserts both directions against the same fixture with the same 2 s window. The 'disable' scenario at scripts/verify-bundled-xpi.mjs:354 calls loadOnce with the add-on disabled and fails with 'with the add-on disabled the filtered request still never arrived' when the fixture request does not arrive within 2 s of the control. 'removed-after-relaunch' at :370 does the same with the add-on removed. A Gecko change that delays the unfiltered fixture request past 2 s therefore turns the control run red. The removal-sticks claim is also a direct AddonManager assertion (:369, st.present), not an inference from timing. One narrower gap remains. filterHolds (:298-303) retries for up to 90 s and accepts the first load that shows no fixture request, while the disable step takes a single load. An intermittent delay, late on some loads and on time on others, could let filterHolds pass with no active filter while the single disable load happens to be on time. That is a flakiness-level weakness, not the consistent-delay path the finding describes.

*Guard:* scripts/verify-bundled-xpi.mjs:353-354 (disable: loadOnce must see the fixture arrive) and :370 (removed-after-relaunch: loadOnce must see the fixture arrive); both run in the registered --live row via liveSession({plant: null}) at :477

## Surface summaries and questions that need a running system

Each reviewer listed what it could not settle by reading code. These are candidates for live checks.

### orcad-hosting: orcad process hosting: spawn, environment, ports, lifecycle

orcad hosting is mostly sound. The argv is an explicit vector with no secrets. The environment is an exact allowlist passed through spawnProcessExact, so no POWERBROWSER token, NODE_OPTIONS or foreign ORCA_* names reach orcad. The bind is fixed to 127.0.0.1 and checked against the child's own ready line. A busy port is refused, never adopted. The pairing offer stays in memory and goes to a 0600 file in a 0700 folder. Bare-pid signals are gated on /proc start ticks. No pref added in powerbrowser.js or powerbrowser-sidecar.js weakens a Gecko security control.

Two medium findings, both lifecycle:
- A browser crash leaves orcad, its systemd-scoped terminal daemon and every skip-permissions agent running unsupervised until the next launch. There is no parent-death tie, and D-07 covers quit only.
- Restart Orca from the failure page reaps with includeDaemon:true, which kills the daemon and agents that the crash-respawn path had kept alive on purpose.

Six low findings:
- A fixed-port denial of service by any local user.
- The daemon's identity ticks are read from whatever process holds the reported pid, not from the daemon's own record.
- A ready-versus-quit race can leave the pairing credential file on disk after exit.
- Stale hand-off files from a crashed session are not cleared and are consumed by Theia before OrcaService starts.
- Post-ready crash loops restart without limit.
- Rejected starts orphan the daemon.

One info finding: the environment allowlist passes the SSH agent socket, the session bus and proxy settings to the agents.

Not assessed statically:

- Whether orcad's E2EE key pair and runtime device rows survive an orcad restart (sets the real impact of the stale web-pairing hand-off after a crash; 67-03 says rows are session-only).
- Whether Theia's extension manager can replace <profile>/powerbrowser-theia/deployedPlugins/powerbrowser.claude-agent-sdk (through an Open VSX update or a same-ID install after the user uninstalls the bundled one). orcad imports code from that path via ORCA_CLAUDE_AGENT_SDK_DIR, so a replacement would run inside orcad and every Claude agent session. Needs the registry namespace state and a running Theia.
- Whether orcad's stderr can ever carry a raw base64 pairing code or device token without the #pairing= / orca://pair? / pair%3F prefixes that OrcaEnv.redact matches.
- Whether Gecko's Subprocess kills still-running children at shutdown, which decides whether an endOwn() kill started after _stopTree finished (spawn racing a quit) completes before exit.
- Whether the detached terminal daemon inherits orcad's stdout/stderr pipe fds (would keep the old pump open after an orcad crash and let a daemon descendant write to a pipe PB reads).
- Whether the 20.5 s stopAndReap budget fits inside the AsyncShutdown appShutdownConfirmed crash timeout under load with many agent descendants.
- Actual live behaviour of the squatting and crash scenarios on a running system (port-in-use page, frontend reconnect against a squatter, agent survival after SIGKILL).

Files read: 28.

### pairing-credentials: credential store, pairing offer and offer-file handling

I reviewed the credential-store, pairing-offer and offer-file surface. The offer file handling is sound against other local users: the bridge folder must be 0700 and owned by the user, the file is checked with lstat and read through a no-follow open that must match the same inode, refusals return only a reason code, and the offer reaches the frontend only over Theia's JSON-RPC socket, which checks both the token cookie and the sidecar's own origin. The failure page shows only fixed text keyed by a failure class that is checked against a closed set. Nothing from orcad or the network is rendered as HTML, and I found no path where the offer reaches a URL, log line, diagnostics row or localStorage (pairing is session-only in page memory). The defects found are: (1, medium) a race in which the frontend can get the offer before orca-panes-dir is written, which leaves the pane bundle broken until the document reloads, because both bootstrap layers cache the failed attempt and Reconnect does nothing before boot; (2, low) T-67-13's port check compares the endpoint with a port read from the same file, so it enforces only that the endpoint is a loopback address; (3, low) stale offer files are never cleared at start, on a restart, or when no new offer arrives, and are served as current; (4, low) each orcad start leaves a runtime-scope token row in orcad's registry that is never revoked, contrary to the code's assumption that the row dies with orcad; (5, low) the file that names the bundle folder, whose JavaScript runs in the Theia origin, is checked less strictly than the offer file, and the folder itself is never checked. Every attack path found needs a process running as the same user, or a crash or backup copy. None is reachable from web content or off-host in this tree.

Not assessed statically:

- Whether Gecko sends Sec-Fetch-Site on every request a web tab makes to http://127.0.0.1:<theiaPort> (origin-policy.ts admits a request with neither Origin nor Sec-Fetch-Site). This decides whether a cross-site no-cors subresource load of /orca-panes/* can carry the host-only token cookie. The bundle holds no secret, but this needs a live probe.
- Whether an Orca agent driving a web tab (browser.goto, browser.eval) can load the Theia origin with the token cookie. The agent container uses its own userContextId and so should lack the cookie, but this needs a live check.
- Whether hosted Orca UI in the Theia origin, or the orca CLI an agent controls, can make orcad create a mobile-scope pairing or relay binding that is reachable off-host (OVL-03, D-23). orcad is spawned with --bind 127.0.0.1 and no --pairing-address, but runtime-RPC mobile-pairing methods were not traced.
- The real file mode of orcad's credential key in HOME/.orca, which ORCA_PROTECTION_SENTENCE tells users only their account can read. PowerBrowser does not verify it, and upstream creation was not traced.
- Whether the Orca session-key migration falls back to legacy token auth after 32 forks of one token row within 12 h (MAX_FORKS_PER_TOKEN_ROW), and so whether repeated reloads or Reconnects within one orcad lifetime ever fail pairing.
- Whether Theia JSON-RPC tracing or devtools recording, when enabled, captures the handOff reply payload.

Files read: 16.

### sidecar-gate-backends: sidecar origin/token gate coverage and Theia backend services added in v1.7

I found no bypass of the gate itself. The token gate and Origin policy are unchanged in v1.7 (the merge-base is the RUN-08 hotfix 2b5f4609). originVerdict allows only an absent Origin or exactly http://127.0.0.1:<own port>, and only Sec-Fetch-Site absent, none or same-origin. 'null', localhost, IPv6 and trailing-dot origins all fail the exact match. The port is undefined before announce, which fails closed. The token is compared with timingSafeEqual, is read only from an HttpOnly host-only cookie, and never travels in the URL. DNS rebinding cannot carry that cookie.

Routes and services v1.7 added:
- HTTP GET/HEAD `/orca-panes/` (orca-panes), registered in configure() after the front-inserted gate.
- JSON-RPC `/services/powerbrowser-orca-git` (orca-workspace).
- JSON-RPC `/services/powerbrowser-orca-panes` (handOff of the pairing offer).
- JSON-RPC `/services/powerbrowser/orca-agents` (getClaudeWrapperPath).
- JSON-RPC `/services/powerbrowser-bundled-extensions` (absent/reinstall, ids limited to shipped ones).

All JSON-RPC services ride Theia's socket.io, whose allowRequest runs PowerBrowserTokenGateContribution.allowWsUpgrade (Origin plus token). No v1.7 path is registered before the gate, and none uses raw request or upgrade listeners. The only token-free answer is the pre-existing static webview shell on *.webview.localhost.

orca-workspace git:
- git runs through execFile with no shell.
- rev is limited to a 40/64-hex object id or a :0 to :3 stage.
- paths cannot start with '-', be absolute, or contain '..', empty or '.' segments, backslashes or control characters, and `--` precedes the spec.
- the root must be realpath-stable and the repository toplevel.
- GIT_* is stripped and fsmonitor is off.
- cat-file reads objects, so a symlink yields its target text and the filesystem is not followed.

I found no argument injection or traversal. A hostile repository cannot plant '..' conflict paths because git rejects them in the index.

Findings:
- Medium: Orca's third-party renderer bundle runs in the token-holding sidecar origin with no CSP. Any sanitizer bypass in Orca's rendering of GitHub or Jira content becomes Theia RPC, which means local code execution (ratified design D-15).
- Low: the git reads do not disable promisor lazy fetch, credential prompts or askpass, and a timeout leaves the fetch children running.
- Low: a cookie with a longer path, planted from any 127.0.0.1 page, shadows the real token in the gate's first-match parser and locks the user out of the sidecar.
- Low: the served Orca bundle directory has no owner or mode check.
- Info: the backend's restriction of git reads to Orca roots exists only in the frontend.

orca-editor-routes opens only file: and pb-orca-git: URIs built through URI.fromFilePath or the shared grammar, so there is no scheme confusion. Orca-supplied edit paths can open any file the user can read in an editor, but that is display only, and agents already run with skip-permissions (D-12).

Not assessed statically:

- Whether Gecko actually orders a planted longer-path POWERBROWSER_TOKEN cookie ahead of the HttpOnly one on sidecar requests, and whether it rejects a same-name non-HttpOnly cookie from another loopback port page (needs a live profile).
- Whether `git cat-file blob <oid>:<path>` triggers a promisor lazy fetch in the git version shipped with the Theia backend's PATH, and whether SSH_ASKPASS is present in the sidecar's environment on KDE.
- Whether Theia 1.76 honours the Claude Code extension's `scope: machine` for claudeCode.claudeProcessWrapper when an Orca worktree's .vscode/settings.json sets it (orca-root-sync auto-adds every local Orca worktree as a root while security.workspace.trust.enabled is false). No folder-scoped setting with an execution sink was confirmed in the tree.
- Whether Orca's openFiles can hold a record whose worktree is not yet in worktreesByRepo (worktreePath '' resolves sides to /<relativePath>); this looked self-correcting through the signature and was not reported.
- Whether socket.io polling requests after the handshake (sid known) are re-validated; the sid is not readable cross-origin, so this was judged safe without a live check.
- Whether any Orca sanitizer currently has a bypass (upstream; out of scope except as the dependency reported).

Files read: 24.

### chrome-actor: privileged chrome actor surface: PowerBrowserAPI and the group actor

I reviewed the privileged chrome actor surface: the agent bridge, the agent, agent-page, edit-state and logo actor pairs, the group parent dispatch, adoption of new windows opened by agent pages, and the Theia web-tab agent plumbing. The actor registrations are scoped as intended. The agent pairs are limited to the 'powerbrowser-orca-agent' message manager group and created only by chrome's getActor. The logo child loads in every loopback top document, but the parent gates it on orcad's port, the ready state and a non-shell embedder. Every webTabAgent request passes type and range validators and agentWallVerdict (owner, agent flag, own container, not stopped). URL loads are limited to http(s) and about:blank with a null triggering principal. The new-window, download and external-protocol walls are present, and sandbox flags carry across BrowsingContext replacement. I found three medium issues and four low ones.

1. Orca's non-automation navigate command, which orcad uses for agent page creation and recovery, goes through the user webTabNavigate kind. That skips the agent wall, so Stop does not stop navigation.
2. WebTabWidget re-creates a crashed or lost agent tab without the agent flag. The tab becomes a user overlay in the user's cookie jar, with the login manager, the extension tab host and no sandbox or clipboard walls, while Orca keeps it bound and can keep navigating it. An agent can trigger this by crashing its own content process.
3. The chrome sender wall trusts the Theia document as a whole, and v1.7 loads Orca's third-party renderer bundle into it. Any script injection there reaches every privileged kind, readClipboardText included. Planning accepted this as T-64-12; owner item T-61-04 is still open.

The low findings are: one container and one cookie jar shared by all agents (D-41 accepts that the container is visible), agent visits written into the user's history and suggestions, page-menu edit rows failing on agent-tab text fields, and catalogue and header claims that understate GroupActorChild's reach. INTERNAL-APIS.md otherwise covers the PowerBrowserAPI touchpoints the diff added.

Not assessed statically:

- Whether a parked agent overlay (z-index -1, visibility visible) is ever visible or hit-testable through a transparent region of the Theia frame, or while the Theia frame is hidden during a sidecar restart.
- Whether SANDBOXED_AUXILIARY_NAVIGATION and SANDBOXED_DOWNLOADS on the top agent BrowsingContext actually reach the top-level document's loadInfo for every navigation type (bfcache, session-history restore through restoreWebTabHistory, process switch). Source review shows ReplacedBy copies the flags; not measured.
- Whether the PowerBrowserAgentPage DOMWindowCreated listener runs before page script in every frame type (same-process about:blank iframes created by script, out-of-process iframes), so Clipboard.prototype is always replaced first.
- Whether Places records visits for in-shell overlays without disableglobalhistory, which the history-seeding finding depends on.
- Whether Orca's page recovery (runtime-browser-client-page-recovery.ts) sends a navigate to PB after a PB content-process crash, which would trigger the agent-tab downgrade with no agent action.
- Whether a script-injection flaw exists in Orca's renderer at the pinned tag (the precondition for the trusted-document finding).
- Whether drawSnapshot and canvas allocations for a fullScreenshot of a page reporting a 16384 px document stay within gfx allocation limits in the parent process, rather than exhausting parent memory.
- Whether the pb-orca-git: content resolver (docs/URI-SCHEMES.md) limits <absolute file path> to Orca worktrees. It is outside this surface and was not read.

Files read: 15.

### agent-browser: agent-driven browser control: PowerBrowser web tabs as Orca's browser

The dispatch path denies by default: SERVED_AUTOMATION_METHODS equals chrome's frozen AGENT_METHODS, each method has a params validator, the child looks up arms with Object.hasOwn, and the page table uses Maps. So no tool or argument reaches an unlisted handler. Chrome's wall admits only overlays this frontend opened with the agent flag and requires them to be in the agent container, so the user's own tabs and stock windows are unreachable. Navigation is http(s) only with a null principal. No CDP, BiDi or Marionette port is opened. The host-bundle link and the shell request events live only in the Theia document, which web pages cannot reach. The main findings: (1) browser.fill writes a file input's value with system privileges, which attaches any local file and gets around the 'upload unavailable' decision (high). (2) A crashed or lost-view agent tab is reopened without the agent flag, in the user's cookie jar with the login manager and none of the D-35/D-37/D-38 walls (high, medium confidence). (3) One persistent agent container is shared by all agents, and cookie.get returns httpOnly values for any host (medium). (4) Stop is per tab and is hidden while the mark is idle, so an agent continues through new background tabs (medium). Lower findings: no worktree scope for agent tabs, no host wall for loopback and LAN targets, a listener leak while Orca is not ready, and a createPage bind race.

Not assessed statically:

- Whether nsITextInputProcessor keys sent by browser.keypress (Control+s, Control+p, Control+o, F12) reach chrome shortcuts or open a native file picker, save dialog or print dialog from an agent tab.
- Whether window.print() or a beforeunload/print/permission prompt from a parked agent page opens a stock window or dialog the user may act on unknowingly.
- What orcad serves on its loopback port to a plain top-level GET from the agent container (web client, pairing offer), and whether the agent can learn anything from it.
- Whether the Theia frame is opaque over every parked agent overlay (z-index -1), so a parked page can never receive the user's clicks.
- Whether HTTP auth prompts in the agent container are prefilled from the user's saved logins (LoginManagerAuthPrompter is not tied to the message manager group).
- A live confirmation that the system-principal CallerType applies to the actor child's Xray value write on a file input (Gecko source says it does).
- Whether orcad replays createPage on reattach in a way that triggers the concurrent-bind race.

Files read: 13.

### overlay-renderer: Orca overlay: pane mount, side mounts, workspace API, CSS scope, patches

I reviewed the ten assigned overlay files and followed the calls they depend on into the Theia orca-panes loader and widgets, OrcaEnv.sys.mjs and upstream Orca. I found no script-injection sink in the overlay. Orca, repository, terminal and agent data reach the DOM only through Orca's own components. The upstream sinks I traced either sanitize their input or isolate it in a sandboxed opaque-origin frame, and agent visual links are restricted to http and https. The overlay adds no postMessage, BroadcastChannel or computed-import path. The CSS scoping pass leaves only custom properties unscoped on the Theia root, and in the built sheet none of them collide with PowerBrowser's --color-* fallbacks or Theia's --theia-*/--vscode-* names, so Orca's stylesheet cannot hide PowerBrowser chrome. The three patches do not weaken upstream checks: 010 only empties the installed-Electron candidate list. 070 loads the SDK only from the realpath of a directory under the user's own profile, so another local actor cannot redirect it without already being that user. 080 makes the SDK external, and the other SDK imports are type-only. seams.json matches the patches' files and blobs. Defects found: (1) medium: closePane routes a split close through Orca's tab-wide window event, and every leaf widget's TerminalPane handles that event and closes its own manager's active pane, so closing one PowerBrowser terminal tab can kill a sibling split's shell or several splits; (2) low: the CSS pass treats `.dark\:` utilities as theme roots, so about 96 dark-mode utilities never apply in hosted panes; (3) low: a failed bootstrap is cached forever and Reconnect cannot recover it; (4) low: mountErrors grows without bound; plus two info observations, on runtime hooks missing from the seam list and on the accepted risk of Orca's renderer running in the authenticated Theia document.

Not assessed statically:

- Whether the pane managers of several TerminalPane instances sharing one tabId actually differ in active pane at runtime, and which leaf a background-split close kills. This needs a live run that closes a non-focused split.
- Whether a Radix modal opened from a hosted Orca surface (body pointer-events:none) blocks clicks on PowerBrowser chrome, including the agent mark's Stop button, while it is open. This needs the live GUI.
- Whether the Theia document has a Content-Security-Policy that would contain an upstream renderer XSS. The loader injects an inline module script, which suggests none.
- Whether Tailwind emits `--color-primary`, `--color-border` or `--color-accent` on :root under a future Orca pin, which would override the var() fallbacks in PowerBrowser chrome CSS. At this commit's built pb-panes.css none are emitted.
- Whether the SDK package's exports map resolves through createRequire().resolve (the 'require'/'default' condition) in seam 070. This needs a run against the shipped extension directory.
- Whether multiple simultaneous mounts of singleton Orca surfaces (two 'sidebar' or two 'settings' panes after a layout restore) misbehave.

Files read: 18.

### theia-pane-hosting: Theia side of pane hosting: widgets, routes, restore, side panel, modes, chrome bar

I reviewed the Theia side of Orca pane hosting: the widgets, the route table, the name map and restore opener, the side panel and its sync, the right-sidebar toggle, the Orca mode, and the chrome-bar and branding hunks. The route table is closed: unknown kinds are only logged, and page words are looked up with hasOwnProperty. The name-map loader type-checks entries and regex-checks names, so there is no prototype-pollution path. Orca-supplied titles and labels reach only text sinks: Lumino labels, and Theia hover, which uses textContent for strings. The sync loops are guarded by writing only changed values.

Six findings:
- **Medium (correctness):** restored hosted tabs cannot be closed while the bundle has failed or never finishes booting, because onCloseRequest never disposes them locally.
- **Medium (low confidence):** revealBrowserTabs focuses an agent web tab on any change to Orca's active flag, whoever made it. A prompt-injected agent may be able to take keyboard focus this way (ratified D-34 / P66-09).
- **Low:** the launch prune treats an unreadable setups.json as naming nothing. That releases tombstoned names a Setup still uses, which breaks the T-65-13 guarantee.
- **Low:** a pre-existing custom mode named "Orca" is silently dropped on upgrade and erased by the next mode save.
- **Low:** pb-orca:creating opens the creation surface with no creation pending, which contradicts URI-SCHEMES.md.
- **Low:** layout-restored widget options are not validated, and a prototype-key kind reaches Orca's viewTitle.

The recent fixes (125d27f4, 47f3bc9b) look complete for the paths they target. detachOtherModesViews removes Orca's side containers in other modes, and the right-sidebar toggle hides on a perspective change. I found nothing in this surface that bypasses TabUriRegistry or ties Theia to a full-window layout. The toggle host's direct writes to the main panel's DOM are the one coupling to note.

Not assessed statically:

- Whether an agent's browser.tabSwitch, browser.focus or terminal.focus runtime call changes the paired PB web client's store activeTabId for PB-hosted browser rows, which is what makes the revealBrowserTabs focus steal reachable from a prompt-injected agent.
- Multi-window behaviour: each Theia frontend boots its own OrcaPaneObserver and OrcaPaneNameMap over one profile-wide ProfileStorageService map with last-writer-wins saves. Whether two windows mirror the same Orca pane twice (two React roots on one pane) or mint conflicting names cannot be settled statically.
- Whether Orca's store keeps or clears its tabs during an orcad crash that does not go through hosting.onWillRepair. After the 15 s bound, an empty list disposes every hosted tab and tombstones every name.
- Whether the 125d27f4 guard (perspective id equal to the mode attribute) can stay false outside a switch, for example after a perspective change not made through ModeService, which would leave side containers un-reasserted.
- Whether Theia's tab renderer throws or degrades when title.label is a non-string object (the layout-tamper finding).

Files read: 32.

### agents-extensions: agent launcher, agent hooks and pre-installed extensions

I found no critical, high or medium issue on this surface: two low correctness findings, one low Windows finding and two informational ones.

- **Wrapper launch:** the launcher runs PowerBrowser's own Node from fixed quoted paths, never a shell, and nothing is found through PATH. The wrapper runs Orca's CLI only from a data root that a private file (0600 file in a 0700 folder, owner checked) names. It strips every inherited ORCA_* variable and writes nothing to ~/.claude.
- **Wrapper findings (low):**
  - A half-created Orca pane leaks when `terminal list` fails or a signal arrives during the synchronous CLI phase.
  - Claude start-up can block for up to 60 s while an unresponsive orcad still has its data-root file in place.
  - On Windows a POSIX sh launcher is still registered as the Claude Code wrapper default (open as WINDOWS 53, deferred by D-40).
- **RPC:** the orca-agents service has one method, it takes no arguments and only returns a path. The reinstall service copies only ids it finds among the shipped folders.
- **Bundled-extension seed:** it copies through a temporary sibling and a rename, skips symbolic links, and keeps a user's uninstall.
- **Supply chain:**
  - The Claude Agent SDK is pinned exactly in the lockfile with sha512 hashes from registry.npmjs.org. It is installed with `npm ci --ignore-scripts`, which verifies the integrity hashes.
  - The uBlock Origin XPI is pinned by AMO URL and SHA-256, fetched over HTTPS, and checked before staging; a mismatch deletes the file and fails. The user can remove it, but it never updates itself because autoUpdateDefault is false (info).
- **Agent hooks (info):** they come entirely from upstream orcad, which rewrites the user's real ~/.claude/settings.json and ~/.orca/agent-hooks. All panes share one hook token, so an agent can spoof another pane's status.

Not assessed statically:

- Whether the Claude Code extension honours a default-scope override of claudeCode.claudeProcessWrapper (research A5), and whether it spawns the wrapper with a shell (a bridge path containing spaces).
- Whether the extension starts claude for short-lived calls (version, auth, MCP probes), each of which would create and close an Orca pane through the wrapper.
- How the extension stops a session (SIGTERM, which the wrapper forwards, or SIGKILL, which leaves the pane and possibly claude orphaned).
- Whether bin/claude-wrapper.mjs is shipped and resolvable through require.resolve from the esbuild-bundled backend in packaged Linux, Flatpak and macOS builds.
- Whether the SDK loaded from the deployed extension with --omit=optional and --legacy-peer-deps (no zod, @modelcontextprotocol/sdk or platform binaries) works at runtime for every code path orcad uses; Orca passes pathToClaudeCodeExecutable, so the platform binaries look unneeded.
- Whether Theia 1.76's uninstall always removes deployedPlugins/powerbrowser.claude-agent-sdk, so the reinstall command and orcad's SDK directory agree.
- Orca CLI `terminal create --worktree path:<cwd>` behaviour for a cwd outside any registered Orca worktree.
- Permissions of orcad's agent-hooks/endpoint.env and its hook server's authentication (upstream).

Files read: 26.

### ci-supply-chain: CI workflows, weekly re-pin, release path and build scripts

I reviewed the CI, re-pin and release surface for v1.7 and found six issues: two high, two medium, two low. The re-pin pipeline itself is well built:
- every action is pinned by commit SHA;
- workflow inputs reach shell only through env and are checked against strict regexes;
- the replay job is read-only and does not persist its credential;
- the bot job recomputes the PR content from tracked data and mints its App token only after committing;
- the Orca pin is verified against its peeled commit;
- export-orca-seam-pr.mjs publishes nothing; it only writes local files.

The main risks are in how the new Orca build jobs connect to the release path.
1. High: the new hosted Orca build jobs share a workflow run with sign-publish. sign-publish signs the release-linux artifact with no digest tied to the job that built it, so code from a compromised Orca build could replace the Linux MAR before it is signed. This contradicts "Linux ships alone": a Windows or macOS job can silently change the Linux bytes.
2. High: build-linux runs Orca's build, and its TypeScript imports, directly on the persistent legion runner, outside the podman container used for Gecko. The Orca pin moves weekly (D-26).
3. Medium: the `replayed` result that arms auto-merge is computed by the very upstream code it is meant to check, and the required `verify` check never builds Orca.
4. Medium: rebase-orca can replace the renovate-licensing-notices and maintenance-state artifacts in the same release-train run.
5. Low: sign-windows checks Orca PE files by name only. The admitted SHA256SUMS has per-file digests, but the signing list ignores them; this matters only once Azure signing is configured.
6. Low: upstream build output can write the text of the stop issue.

The remaining scripts (fetch, build, panes, browser host, detector, train clock) have no defects worth reporting beyond these.

Not assessed statically:

- Whether ACTIONS_RUNTIME_TOKEN taken in windows-orca, macos-orca-* or rebase-orca can still delete or upload artifacts of the run after its job has finished. This decides whether the release-linux replacement needs a job still running when build-linux uploads.
- Whether the live main-review ruleset actually requires the owner's approving review for bot PRs with auto-merge armed (repo-controls read-back); auto-merge safety depends on it.
- Whether the GitHub runner on legion allows pb-runner to read its own .credentials and .runner files and to write anything that later jobs execute (the NixOS github-runners module sandbox settings).
- Whether git-lfs filters are configured system-wide on ubuntu-latest, and so whether a .gitattributes/.lfsconfig in the cloned Orca tree makes network requests during bump-pr-orca's --replay-only (no credential is present at that point).
- Whether regenerate-patches.sh can silently empty or delete a seam patch when upstream has absorbed the change; bump-pr-orca stages orca-overlay/patches with git add -A and runs no seam row itself.
- How long build-linux takes compared with the 90-minute timeout of the hosted Orca jobs.

Files read: 21.

### packaging-prefs-network: packaging, system unit, default prefs, network endpoints, privacy claims, licensing

I reviewed v1.7's packaging, the legion system module, the branding prefs, the endpoint allowlist, the privacy page, the crash policy and the licensing generation, and found 7 issues. Most serious (high): systemd/legion-pb.nix puts the release runner user in libvirtd, which is root-equivalent on legion. The runner is persistent, and v1.7 adds an Orca build from source that runs upstream and npm build-time code on the host as that user, outside the Gecko container. Licensing (medium): Orca's own MIT copyright notice (Lovecast Inc.) appears neither in THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt nor in the staged <GreD>/orca tree. Privacy (medium): the privacy page says api.anthropic.com is contacted only after an Anthropic API key is added, but Orca agents send page and repository content to model providers without one; the page also omits the Orca cloud and Google favicon hosts. Egress (medium): the www.google.com and cdn.jsdelivr.net 'allow' rows, justified by Widevine and uBlock, silently admit Orca's favicon and emoji requests, and several hosts Orca can reach have no row at all. Lower-severity items: native binaries in the Orca bundle are attributed only by their npm package licence; uBlock is pinned with automatic updates off; and the GPL source offer for uBlock is a link to the upstream GitHub tag (accepted by D-44). Checks that came back clean: the Orca staging (no source maps, .env files, setuid or world-writable files, symlinks or host paths in orca/out); artifact admission through check-win-sidecar.sh; the win-sign-list Orca PE set comparison, which fails closed; strict SPDX token matching in verify-licence-allowlist; the uBlock XPI sha256 pin; the permissions.isolateBy.userContext pref, which strengthens isolation; and the CRASH-POLICY auto-submit claim, which matches upstream (the crash client reads env_bool from the live environment). The codeberg-mirror unit is well hardened, and no network listener is exposed. Questions that need a running system are listed in not_assessed.

Not assessed statically:

- Whether an idle orcad started by OrcaService contacts *.onorca.dev or any other host before a user action (relay or pairing minting at start); this needs a live egress capture (orca-service-live).
- Whether Orca mode at launch loads the side panel and fetches www.google.com/s2/favicons before any click.
- Whether orcad's credential key file under ~/.orca is created mode 0600 at run time, as the privacy sentence 'a key file that only your user account can read' claims.
- Whether the agent-tab clipboard wall (GroupActorChild PowerBrowserAgentPage) also covers iframes and initial about:blank windows, which the privacy claim 'An agent page cannot read or change your clipboard' depends on.
- Whether Gecko's distribution add-on install path still enforces the AMO signature for <GreD>/distribution/extensions/uBlock0@raymondhill.net.xpi in the release configuration.
- Whether the NixOS services.github-runners unit's own sandboxing blocks the libvirt socket (the file itself says this is unverified until the first job).
- Whether the Claude Agent SDK assembled with npm ci --omit=optional can run without its platform binary package, or falls back to a claude executable found on the user's PATH at run time.
- Whether agent CLIs spawned by orcad (Claude Code, Codex) honour DO_NOT_TRACK and ORCA_TELEMETRY_DISABLED or send their own telemetry.

Files read: 32.

### harness-driver: gate driver, registry, exclusions and shared run helpers

The gate driver's main weakness is that it has no SKIP verdict. Every v1.7 live row that guards a security requirement (credential store, pairing containment, no remote agent port, agent tab scope, real-agent status) exits 0 after printing SKIP when a build artefact, typescript, the Claude sign-in margin or a lane file is missing. The driver records PASS, and --gate reports every check green. This is high; D-60 ratifies it for orca-agent-status-live only. Related medium findings:
- Lane-merge SKIP rules in scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs and orca-tabs-live.mjs still treat a missing AGENT_METHODS, orca-browser or orca-panes as a SKIP on the merged tree.
- verify.yml never fetches orca/, so the Orca-derived clauses of the quick security rows skip on every push and fork PR.
- The leak leg is blind to orcad started detached by scripts/lib/orca-run.mjs. Three callers drop error.child on a startOrcad rejection, and a driver interrupt never reaches detached orcads.
- The WINDOWS 30 exclusions now cover seven rows the entry does not name and excuse any failure reason (D-59).
- redate-windows-self-test in --quick loads code from ~/.claude and cannot run in CI.

Exit handling is otherwise sound: there is no pipeline or subshell loss, a missing command fails, and an exclusion stops applying when its entry is closed. There are no per-row timeouts, so a hung row hangs the gate. Every verify-orca-*.mjs has a registry row and none is registered twice; the only unregistered new scripts are the build-orca*.sh builders. Tier placement matches the comments: network and live rows are in the full tier, and orca-mobile-skew is full. The five hash-locked ng files: ng-018 adds a wait before unchanged expects, ng-023 now opens a Theia web tab and requires source 'tab' (stricter), ng-067 waits on entries after a mark (stricter), ng-072 only wraps env in liveEnv, and only ng-027's widened call pattern loosens its assertion (low). Live rows reach a scratch HOME through liveEnv and assertScratchHome, and the scratch-home lifetime and compare-and-swap write-back are exercised by real child processes. The orcad-leak self-test checks the driver wiring by substring only, and nothing self-tests the gate logic. No secret printing or shell-string injection was found in the assigned files: commands are built from REPO_ROOT and argv arrays, and the pairing URL is redacted.

Not assessed statically:

- Whether any of the roughly 5,000 pre-v1.7 function-check lines in scripts/verify-platform.sh (check_* bodies) contain an exit-0 path; I reviewed the v1.7 hunks, the registry, the run loop, the leak leg and the gate in full.
- Whether a full --gate run on the owner's host currently records SKIP-as-PASS for orca-credential-store-live, no-remote-agent-port-live or orca-pairing-containment-live (depends on the built state at run time).
- Whether verify.yml is already red for other reasons, which would make the redate-windows-self-test CI red redundant.
- Whether startOrcad's 60 s timeout is reachable in practice on the reference host (sets how often the orca-run leak happens).
- Whether Firefox or OrcaService put orcad in a different process group in any packaged launch path, which would also defeat the driver's kill -0 gate for PowerBrowser-spawned orcad.
- Whether an Orca re-pin's out/orcad tree on macOS actually contains symlinks (pnpm layout) that the merge plan would skip.

Files read: 30.

### harness-static-hosting: static checks for credentials, pairing, offer, service, failure classes, origin gate

I read all ten assigned scripts, the registry rows, the CI workflows, and the product files they inspect. Every script is registered in a sensible tier, and every one has a --self-test that plants faults. The realistic regressions in the question all go red on a developer machine with orca/ fetched: offer file world-readable or a symlink, endpoint off loopback, handOff reading before the verdict, a new shell kind or a handler call ahead of the sender wall, orcad's origin admitted, an Orca route outside configure(), and the plaintext override in the allowlist. Main gaps found:

- **High: pin-derived checks never run in CI.** The RUN-09 credential-store clauses, failure-classes' Orca reason unions, offer-file's decode agreement, orca-service's pinned codes and orca-mobile-protocol all SKIP with exit 0 when orca/ is absent. verify.yml never fetches orca/, the driver counts exit 0 as PASS, and rebase-orca.sh's ROWS leave these rows out. So a re-pin that breaks the credential-store guarantees can merge green.
- **Medium: orca-mobile-skew runs downloaded code.** It runs code from the newest unpinned mobile-android tag on the upstream remote on developer machines, through import() of the fetched constants file.
- **Medium: an exception in the shared collector logic reads as a pass.** runCollector drops a read() exception whenever the control was found, so the bridge-folder mode and stray-copy faults can disappear into a green line.
- **Medium: session-only pairing rests on a copied key name.** It is proven only by the absence of a localStorage key name copied by hand from upstream, while the Theia origin's storage is excluded from the whole-profile scan.
- **Medium: the logs sink's control does not cover stdout/stderr.** The control is found without those streams being captured, and a missing capture file reads as empty.
- **Low:**
  - offer-file does not check the no-follow, same-inode read.
  - credential-store reads only two Orca files and never checks the key file's mode.
  - the listener scan covers only two pids.
  - the origin-gate route scan only sees a receiver named app.
  - orcad's fatal codes are a hand-kept pair.
  - Scenario A's environment expectation comes from the same allowlist as the subject, and the steering deny list is hand-kept.
  - the sender-wall check only finds handlers called on the case line.

No secret printing was found. Pairing values go through installOutputRedaction, temp paths use mkdtemp, child processes use execFileSync without a shell, and the live work folders are removed.

Not assessed statically:

- Whether withFirefoxPage, on the launch path withOrcaProfile uses, actually writes PB_LIVE_STDOUT_PATH/PB_LIVE_STDERR_PATH during orca-pairing-containment-live (needs a live run).
- Whether the gate host that runs --gate has orca/ fetched and a built PowerBrowser, so the live rows orca-pairing-containment, orca-offer-scan and orca-credential-store-live execute rather than print SKIP and count as PASS.
- Whether branch protection or the bump-PR flow requires a local --quick run with orca/ fetched before a re-pin merges, which would partly offset the CI SKIP gap.
- Whether the Orca remote protects mobile-android-v* tags, which affects how reachable the unpinned skew import is.
- Whether the chrome bar input really changes value per activated Orca tab in the live address-field collector, or can keep the control tab's marker value (needs DOM observation).
- Whether Gecko writes any Orca offer material into compressed stores (LSNG snappy values, IndexedDB, jsonlz4 outside sessionstore-backups) that the raw-byte whole-profile scan cannot see.

### harness-static-browser: static checks for the agent browser, agent hooks and Claude SDK

I read all eight assigned harness scripts, their registry rows, the driver loop and the product code each one inspects. All eight are registered in the right tiers, and each has a --self-test that plants faults per assertion. The tab-scope, hidden-electron and claude-sdk self-tests are strong. Two checks have gaps that a realistic regression could pass through. (1) The agent-hooks static clause looks only for literal `process.env.X`, but the wrapper reads its environment through a `baseEnv = process.env` parameter. The live stage plants no decoy ORCA_USER_DATA_PATH or ORCA_PANE_KEY, so a wrapper that routed agents into the user's desktop Orca would stay green. (2) orca-browser-tab-scope proves the wall only through the webTabAgent arm. It never derives the callers of agentRunInChild or AGENT_NATIVE_ROWS, so a second entry point that bypasses the wall is invisible to it. A systemic gap: the driver records every exit 0 as PASS. CI's quick job does not fetch orca/, so the BRW-02/03 ability and entry-point comparisons SKIP-as-PASS on every push and PR, including clauses that need no orca/ at all. Entry-points clause 3 accepts any reachable creator call with a hard-coded claim. The host-bundle redaction clause scans only its own pre-redacted lines and a stale build log, so it cannot go red. Host-bundle orcad survivors are only a note. orca-browser-tools compares two hand-kept lists. No secret leak, no shell injection, and no real-HOME write was found: liveEnv and assertScratchHome relocate HOME for the host-bundle orcad, and agent-hooks and hidden-electron use their own scratch HOMEs.

Not assessed statically:

- Whether orca-browser-entry-points currently lists any createBrowserTab caller as reachable from the PB pane entry (needs a run over the fetched orca/ tree).
- Whether the full-tier rows (orca-browser-host-bundle, orca-agent-hooks, orca-hidden-electron) actually leave orcad or terminal-daemon survivors on this host (needs a live run).
- Whether theia/node_modules contains esbuild in CI, which decides whether orca-claude-sdk-self-test runs or SKIPs there.
- Which gate runs so far recorded SKIP output from the assigned rows as PASS (needs the gate logs).
- Whether Orca's CLI or orcad writes anything under the liveHome() scratch HOME that persists between rows (needs a live run; liveEnv relocates HOME, so the real ~/.claude is protected).

### harness-static-structure: static checks for overlay, panes, pins and build shape

Audited 16 static-check scripts and followed calls into verify-platform.sh, lib/orca-run.mjs, verify.yml, rebase-orca.yml and fetch-orca.sh. The self-tests are generally strong: each one plants faults against a real anchor, fails a plant whose anchor is absent, and requires the failure message to name the drift. The main weaknesses are structural. The hosted PR gate never fetches orca/, so the Orca-dependent static rows cannot reach their real logic there. The driver counts every SKIP as PASS. A few checks compare path sets rather than content, or can silently disappear (orca-pin with both keys absent, overlay with no seams.json). I found nothing that leaks a secret, and no shell string concatenation anywhere in the shard. Every git and node child process uses execFileSync or spawnSync with an argv array.

- Whether the orca-terminal-keys-self-test row actually fails or crashes in the hosted verify job without orca/ (needs a run).

- Real sha256 or content equality of a packaged Orca tree against a built one (needs a container-built release tarball).

### harness-live-security: live checks for credentials, agent browser, remote port, origin gate, service

I reviewed the 12 assigned harness files and followed calls into the driver (scripts/verify-platform.sh), orca-tabs-live, pb-live-probe, orca-run, hidden-electron, slotless-start and OrcaEnv. Every assigned script is registered, and in the right tier: live rows in the full block (lines 6082-6933), pure self-tests in quick.

The most serious problem is clause (f) of orca-browser-agent-live, which cannot go red. The row sets process.env.HOME to a decoy folder. liveEnv() then counts that folder as a real home, because realHomes() includes os.homedir(), which returns $HOME, and so it swaps in the .mozbuild scratch HOME. PowerBrowser's orcad never sees the decoy.

The driver records any exit 0 as PASS. So the SKIP paths found here are counted as passes:
- orca-browser-agent-live and orca-agent-mark-live skip when a regex for AGENT_METHODS fails to match or when built outputs are missing (including pb-browser-host.js), and the row passes with clause (f) skipped when a system-wide Orca is installed.
- credential-store-live skips when typescript is not installed.
- no-remote-agent-port-live skips in both the live row and its self-test live half.
- service-live, failure-page-live, snapshot-live and the quick orca-snapshot-fixtures row skip on missing builds or sources.
- agent-status-live skips with no claude on PATH or a sign-in near expiry (the known case).

failure-page-live moves the shared pb-browser-host.js aside. Its finally block does not run when liveHome()'s SIGTERM handler re-raises the signal, so the bundle stays moved and the agent-browser rows then SKIP and pass. Seven live rows claim the pb-live.lock but neither they nor the driver take it.

Three clauses of browser-agent-live, (h), (i) and (e), turn a failed CLI read into a pass. Lower findings:
- many unplanted assertions (clause (b) has no plant at all);
- two assertions that cannot fire in a live run;
- port-only acceptance in no-remote-agent-port;
- a single point-in-time process walk for the credential-store refusals;
- a same-source environment expectation in service-live;
- a vacuous empty-id recovery in failure-page;
- a non-atomic write-back into the real ~/.claude/.credentials.json;
- clobbering of the real clipboard on headed runs.

Positives: no tokens or pairing values are printed. Commands avoid shell concatenation (the bash -c uses positional arguments). Negative legs assert refusals the product emits (error codes, recorded sidecar 403 rows, the remote-port control) rather than the harness's own timeouts. Processes are signalled only by recorded pid plus start ticks.

Not assessed statically:

- Whether this host has a system-wide Orca at /usr/bin, /usr/local/bin or /opt/Orca/orca-ide, which would make clause (f) SKIP on every run here.
- Whether Gecko in the PB shell window ever stores a submitted login without an accepted doorhanger, which decides whether (l) capturedAfter can go red.
- Whether liveHome()'s SIGTERM re-raise ends failure-page-live before its finally block in a real driver interrupt (the static trace says it does).
- Whether OrcaService ever starts orcad before classifying temporary-profile or plaintext-setting; currently the classification looks like a pre-spawn step in OrcaEnv's step table.
- Whether the Theia sidecar and tab-store endpoint bind loopback only: no assigned live row asserts their addresses.
- Whether restored hosted-tab widget ids stay stable across a reload or relaunch, which decides whether failure-page recovery can run over an empty id set.
- Whether the CLI 'tab list' and 'cookie get' calls fail in practice after the reload in clause (i), which would make clauses (h), (i) and (e) pass vacuously.

### harness-live-tabs: live checks for tabs, terminal keys and relaunch restore

Reviewed the six assigned scripts, the shared session library and the registry and driver rows that run them. The pure verdicts are mostly well built: stages run in order with detectors first, each has a self-test over passing and failing fixtures, and the harness FAILs instead of skipping when the Claude sign-in is under 60 minutes from expiry. The most important defect is systemic. verify-platform.sh decides PASS from the exit code alone, so SKIP lines from orca-tabs-live, orca-terminal-keys-live and orca-relaunch-restore-live are recorded as PASS, including stages l and m of orca-tabs-live, which guard route and pairing-offer containment. The next group are absence judgments that can go green without inspecting anything: the Theia-side tab-chrome and storage probes (which return error objects instead of throwing), and an offer-containment control whose detector reads from the same file as the offer. The reload stage does not prove a reload happened, and relaunch-restore never checks that the Setup restore ran. The remaining findings are low: unreachable or constant assertions, plants missing from the self-tests, a broad dialog selector, unredacted output after teardown, git running against the real HOME, and a documented lock that nothing takes. tab-strip-live's WINDOWS 62 fix (a boot check on the Orca bundle plus a planted foreign-paste fault) is sound, apart from skipping silently when orca/ is not built. mode-switch-tabs-live's Orca seeds are non-vacuous and go red when the extension is present but unwired.

### harness-live-panels: live checks for side panel, pane CSS, standalone panes, workspace

Read all eight assigned files and the registry. No code execution or secret disclosure reachable from web content was found; the pairing offer stays in memory and is redacted on output. ### harness-other: checks for bundled extensions, XPI, licences, workflows, release shape and shared gates

I reviewed the harness only: 20 assigned scripts plus the driver's run loop. The driver records any exit 0 as PASS (verify-platform.sh:6983), so every SKIP path in these scripts reads as a pass.

**Findings**
- **Medium (4):**
  - verify-web-tab-bridge fences webTabAgent and webTabAgentStop only within chrome-bar, tab-uris and orca-browser. orca-panes and modes already import WebTabWidget and are not scanned.
  - verify-uri-roundtrip reports an orcad or pane-bundle boot timeout as SKIP and exits 0, so a product failure passes.
  - verify-workflows' orca-repin rule asserts nothing if bump-pr-orca or detect-orca is renamed.
  - release-shape checks only the position of the P68-21 licence-rulings guard. It does not catch continue-on-error or `|| true` on it, and nothing checks the Windows copy of the guard.
- **Low:**
  - The quick-tier bundled-xpi, bundled-extensions and licence-allowlist legs skip in CI (no cached XPI, no orca/), including the D-85 endpoint leg.
  - shell-error-copy skips the Orca failure-copy check if the failure/ directory moves.
  - The web-tab-bridge lane-merge skip for the agent kinds is still in place.
  - check-internals-boundary's new Cc[/Ci. waiver matches the catalogue by line number, not by XPCOM contract. The child now creates a system principal at line 675.
  - package-target-natives exempts every i386 .exe outside orca/, not just the NSIS helper.
  - The bundled-xpi live plant sessions are never registered as a row.
  - gen-orca-snapshot-fixtures inherits AGENT_BROWSER_* variables and the real HOME config.

**Did the v1.7 diffs narrow what each check covers?**
- **scan-brand-residue:** not narrowed. It adds the tracked_residue_probes list ('orca'), and the --extra-root pass is unchanged and justified in code. The scope.exclude list in inventory/brand-tokens.json is unchanged.
- **check-internals-boundary:** narrowed. It adds Cc[ and Ci. to the actor-child allowance. The reason is in the code, but the waiver is wider than that reason.
- **verify-web-tab-bridge:** narrowed in two ways.
  - It drops Ci. from the child-purity tokens and admits it only on catalogued lines (justified, A2).
  - It adds the conditional agent-contract skip, which is now dormant.
- **verify-package-target-natives:** broadened its universe to judge files by magic bytes, but added an i386 .exe exemption that is wider than its stated reason.
- **verify-workflows:** moved the FIX_DIFF expectation from job env to step env, which GitHub requires. This is justified, and plants cover it.
- **verify-shell-error-copy:** added EXPECTED_NAV_COPY entries for the agent mark, under the existing set-equality rule, plus new Orca and reinstall scopes (the Orca scope can skip).
- **Not narrowed:** verify-mode-toggle-commands, verify-release-shape, verify-repo-controls, verify-build-windows-shape, verify-site-identity (memoization only), verify-first-boot-surface, verify-uri-roundtrip (new rows that can skip) and generate.mjs. These add coverage only.

**New scripts**
- **Solid:** verify-crash-autosubmit-env. Its expectation is derived from the tree, it has no skip path, and each of its six plants is checked to have applied.
- **Registered in the right tier:** verify-licence-allowlist, verify-bundled-extensions and orca-browser-parity. Each has a self-test with a control, but each skips when its input is absent.

**Safety:** I found no shell-concatenation injection. The scripts use execFileSync or spawn with argument arrays, python3 -I, and mkdtemp scratch directories. verify-repo-controls passes its token to the gh child as GH_TOKEN only, and its self-test proves that.

Not assessed statically:

- Whether yarn build in CI populates theia/applications/browser/plugins/.user/<id>/extension/node_modules/<dep>/LICENSE.md, i.e. whether the bundled-extensions LICENSE quotation leg runs or SKIPs in CI

# Layer 2

## Layer 2: per-phase GSD passes

### Code review at deep depth

Each changed product file was reviewed once, in the first phase whose SUMMARY lists it. Verification scripts, generated files and documents were not in these reviews. The reviewers' "critical" label means a blocking defect, not an exploitable vulnerability; the next subsection gives the severity on this audit's scale.

| Phase | Files | Critical-labelled | Warning | Info | Total | Report |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 59 Carry-forward from v1.5 and v1.6 | 8 | 3 | 5 | 6 | 14 | `.planning/workstreams/orca/phases/59-carry-forward-from-v1-5-and-v1-6/59-REVIEW.md` |
