#### 59-CR-01: Runner work directory is never created, so the runner unit fails at namespace setup

- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Severity:** medium (reported as critical)
- **Location:** `systemd/legion-pb.nix:42`
- **Scope:** host config (legion release runner, systemd/legion-pb.nix, owner-installed and not yet deployed)
- **Full finding and suggested fix:** `.planning/workstreams/orca/phases/59-carry-forward-from-v1-5-and-v1-6/59-REVIEW.md`, section CR-01

**Verifier.** (1) systemd/legion-pb.nix:42 sets workDir = "/var/lib/pb-runner". The file's only tmpfiles rule (line 71) creates /var/lib/powerbrowser-release, not the work directory. users.users.pb-runner (lines 51-56) is isSystemUser with no home and no createHome. The header's install steps 1-5 do not create the directory.
(2) The module on disk is legion's root channel nixos-26.05 (.version-suffix .14894.ab5c18f), at nixos/modules/services/continuous-integration/github-runner/service.nix. Line 43 uses cfg.workDir when it is non-null. Line 256 sets WorkingDirectory = workDir and line 312 sets BindPaths = [ cfg.workDir ], both without the "-" prefix. StateDirectory, RuntimeDirectory and LogsDirectory (lines 250-254) are github-runner/powerbrowser-release, so systemd never creates /var/lib/pb-runner. The module has no tmpfiles rule. The workDir option text in options.nix:270-282 does not say the module creates the directory.
The local systemd.exec(5) man page says a BindPaths entry "may be prefixed with '-', in which case it will be ignored when its source path does not exist". Without the prefix, a missing source fails namespace setup (exit 226/NAMESPACE), and a missing WorkingDirectory without "-" is fatal (200/CHDIR). Both are checked before any ExecStartPre runs, including the "+" root unconfigure script, whose `find -H "$WORK_DIRECTORY"` (line 165) would fail anyway on a missing directory. The secondary claims also hold: HOME = workDir (line 56), and the work directory is wiped on every start (line 165).
(3) Nothing else in the tree creates the directory. rg finds /var/lib/pb-runner only in this file, in docs and in planning text. docs/RELEASING.md:1989 says incorrectly that the work directory "comes back" declaratively.
(4) Counter-evidence check: the unit is not deployed. /run/current-system (legion-git-c575adc-26.05.14630) has no github-runner-* or codeberg-mirror unit. systemctl list-units/cat find no github-runner-powerbrowser-release. /var/lib/pb-runner does not exist. /etc/nixos is not visible from this sandbox, so I could not read configuration.nix directly. Since nothing is deployed, no recorded live or gate result contradicts the claim, and no STATE, VERIFICATION or WINDOWS entry records the runner coming online. The defect is latent: the failure happens on the owner's first nrs after installing the file.
Severity: medium. This is a release CI path that fails on start. The owner sees it at once and the fix is one line. It crosses no privilege boundary and breaks no shipped user feature.

*Checked at:* /nix/var/nix/profiles/per-user/root/channels/nixos/nixos/modules/services/continuous-integration/github-runner/service.nix:256 (WorkingDirectory = workDir) and :312 (BindPaths = [ cfg.workDir ], no "-" prefix); systemd.exec(5) BindPaths "-" prefix semantics; systemd/legion-pb.nix:71 has the only tmpfiles rule, and it does not cover /var/lib/pb-runner; unit absent from /run/current-system/etc/systemd/system (not yet deployed, so no live result exists)

#### 59-CR-02: Module default hardening makes the rootless podman that release.yml needs impossible on this runner

- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Severity:** medium (reported as critical)
- **Location:** `systemd/legion-pb.nix:32-56`
- **Scope:** linux product release path: release.yml build-linux and build-windows on the self-hosted legion runner (host config systemd/legion-pb.nix)
- **Full finding and suggested fix:** `.planning/workstreams/orca/phases/59-carry-forward-from-v1-5-and-v1-6/59-REVIEW.md`, section CR-02

**Verifier.** I traced the whole path from source on disk.

(1) The cited code exists as described. systemd/legion-pb.nix:32-50 defines services.github-runners.powerbrowser-release with user/group pb-runner, extraPackages including podman, and no serviceOverrides. :54 sets autoSubUidGidRange = true with the comment "rootless podman". The header comment at :27-28 names only the libvirt socket as a sandboxing risk. No serviceOverrides or RestrictNamespaces setting exists anywhere in the repo outside orca/ and upstream/. The only other mention is a libvirt-related suggestion in deploy phase 54 BLOCKED.md:174.

(2) The failure path:
- The host's nixpkgs 26.05 module (channel /nix/store/fyjl37xdh4z542jbd34s40ky9hp9zmmc-nixos-26.05, github-runner/service.nix:274-304) sets these as mkDefault: NoNewPrivileges, PrivateUsers, RestrictNamespaces, PrivateMounts, PrivateDevices, RestrictSUIDSGID and ProtectProc=invisible.
- service.nix:293-304 adds, through mkBefore, a SystemCallFilter that denies @mount, @clock and capset.
- service.nix:332-339 only switches off DynamicUser for a static user. The only override path is cfg.serviceOverrides at :340, and the file leaves it empty.
- release.yml:72-73 (build-linux) and :501-503 (build-windows) run on [self-hosted, powerbrowser-release], the label this file registers. They run podman build at :179 and :603, and podman run at :187, :201, :615 and :652.
- The on-disk systemd 260.5 man page, systemd.exec(5), says of RestrictNamespaces: "If true, access to any kind of namespacing is prohibited". So unshare/clone with CLONE_NEWUSER returns EPERM.
- The podman 5.8.9 man page on disk (podman-unshare(1)) states that rootless podman runs in a new user namespace whose subuid/subgid ranges are mapped by the newuidmap/newgidmap helpers.
- On this host those helpers are setuid wrappers (/run/wrappers/bin/newuidmap, mode r-s--x--x). NoNewPrivileges=true neutralises the setuid bit.
- PrivateUsers=true maps only the service uid, so subuid ranges cannot be mapped even if namespace creation succeeded.
- ~@mount blocks the bind mounts that -v needs.

Rootless podman therefore cannot start a container under this unit, and pb-runner has no path to rootful podman.

(3) Nothing prevents it. No override exists in the repo. The live /etc/nixos is not readable from this sandbox. systemctl reports that github-runner-powerbrowser-release.service "could not be found", so the module is not applied on the host and no host-side override is active.

(4) No recorded result contradicts the finding:
- No release.yml run on the self-hosted runner is recorded in .planning, WINDOWS.md, STATE.md or the orca VERIFICATION files.
- 68-VERIFICATION.md:110 marks the Linux container release as "UNCERTAIN (first release)" and points to WINDOWS entry 30 (owner steps).
- docs/RELEASING.md:422-423 itself lists "whether rootless podman works for pb-runner" as not verified.

Severity: this is a release build path that fails, closed and before any artifact exists. It is not an integrity or user-facing failure, so it is medium rather than critical. It is also currently hidden behind CR-01: the unit fails at namespace setup before any job runs.

Problem with the proposed fix: serviceOverrides is mkMerge'd in, so a plain SystemCallFilter list is appended to the module's mkBefore deny list rather than replacing it. ~@mount and ~capset would remain, and crun needs capset. Relaxing the filter needs lib.mkForce. PrivateDevices=true also hides /dev/fuse, which fuse-overlayfs needs if native rootless overlay is unavailable. The fix should be checked on the host after it is applied.

*Checked at:* /nix/var/nix/profiles/per-user/root/channels/nixos/nixos/modules/services/continuous-integration/github-runner/service.nix:274-304,340 (defaults; only override hook is serviceOverrides, unset in systemd/legion-pb.nix); .github/workflows/release.yml:73,179,187,201,503,603,615,652; systemd.exec(5) RestrictNamespaces 'If true, access to any kind of namespacing is prohibited'; podman-unshare(1) on disk; /run/wrappers/bin/newuidmap is setuid; no recorded runner build (68-VERIFICATION.md:110 UNCERTAIN, docs/RELEASING.md:422-423 not verified)

#### 63-CR-01: The claude wrapper is never staged in packaged builds, so EXT-02 is silently inert in every shipped artifact

- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Severity:** medium (reported as critical)
- **Location:** `theia/extensions/orca-agents/src/node/orca-agents-backend-module.ts:44 (with powerbrowser/packaging/package-linux.sh:101-117)`
- **Scope:** linux product (packaged tarball/Flatpak/MAR), also macos build and windows build staging; dev tree unaffected
- **Owner decision cited:** D-59
- **Full finding and suggested fix:** `.planning/workstreams/orca/phases/63-theia-terminals-and-agent-hooks-for-orca/63-REVIEW.md`, section CR-01

**Verifier.** I traced every step from source on disk.
(1) Line 44 calls require.resolve('@powerbrowser/orca-agents/bin/claude-wrapper.mjs') inside a try block. The catch at lines 49-51 logs a console.warn and leaves launcherPath undefined.
(2) esbuild does not rewrite that call. The built bundle theia/applications/browser/lib/backend/main.js:506348 still holds the literal require.resolve("@powerbrowser/orca-agents/bin/claude-wrapper.mjs"). gen-esbuild.node.mjs (platform node, cjs, external ['electron'], plus 'better-sqlite3' from esbuild.mjs:28) has one copy() asset, Theia's shell-integrations, and it does not copy orca-agents/bin. So the packaged code calls Node's own require.resolve, which searches node_modules folders upward from <GreD>/theia/lib/backend.
(3) package-linux.sh:101-117 stages only lib/, package.json, plugins/ and node_modules/better-sqlite3 under $STAGE/theia. package-macos.sh:379-391 and release.yml:339-341 stage the same set. git grep finds claude-wrapper outside .planning only in the extension's own sources, verify scripts, scripts/lib/orca-offer-scan.mjs and inventory/brand-tokens.json. No packaging step names the wrapper. No NODE_PATH is set in powerbrowser/, the packaging scripts or theia/applications. In the packaged tree no node_modules folder above <GreD>/theia/lib/backend contains @powerbrowser/orca-agents, so require.resolve throws MODULE_NOT_FOUND.
(4) The bridge directory is set in packaged runs (TheiaService passes it, and the package starts Orca since 21898a67), so execution does reach line 44.
(5) The frontend (orca-agents-frontend-module.ts:31-34) calls registerOverride for claudeCode.claudeProcessWrapper only when a path comes back, so in packaged builds no default is registered.
(6) The dev tree works because theia/node_modules/@powerbrowser/orca-agents exists there. That explains why the dev-tree live rows pass.
Counter-evidence I checked:
- verify-orca-packaged-tree.mjs asserts only on Orca's out/orcad tree (node-pty, no Electron). It makes no wrapper or orca-agents assertion.
- verify-orca-packaged-live.mjs never mentions hook, agent or wrapper.
- verify-orca-agent-hooks.mjs and verify-orca-agent-status-live.mjs run from REPO_ROOT, the dev tree.
- WINDOWS 54 is about stopping an orcad left running after a packaged run. WINDOWS 53 is about the Windows .cmd twin. Neither covers this path.
No recorded result contradicts the finding.
Severity: the code path fails in every packaged build. But D-59 (2026-11-18) moved "the EXT-01 live session (plan 60-05) and the extension half of EXT-02" to the next version, and the Claude Code extension is not bundled (plugins/ is git-ignored, and EXT-01 is deferred). So for v1.7 this is a real packaging defect in a deferred feature: a user who installs the Claude Code extension in a packaged build gets no Orca pane or hook status, only a console warning. That puts it at medium, not high.
The finding's claim that the gap is "untracked" is partly wrong. 63-04-SUMMARY:247, STATE.md:173 and 68-CONTEXT P68-42 all name "the wrapper path in packaged builds", and D-59 defers the extension half of EXT-02. It has no WINDOWS or ledger row, though.

*Checked at:* theia/applications/browser/lib/backend/main.js:506348 (literal require.resolve kept in bundle); powerbrowser/packaging/package-linux.sh:101-117 (staging set without orca-agents); theia/applications/browser/gen-esbuild.node.mjs:55-63 (only shell-integrations copied); theia/extensions/orca-agents/src/browser/orca-agents-frontend-module.ts:31-34 (no override without a path); DECISIONS.md D-59 (extension half of EXT-02 moved to next version)

#### 65-CR-01: A diff side whose path is absent at its rev fails the whole diff (added, deleted and untracked files never open)

- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Severity:** medium (reported as critical)
- **Location:** `theia/extensions/orca-workspace/src/browser/orca-editor-routes.ts:139-154 (diffSides), theia/extensions/orca-workspace/src/node/orca-workspace-backend-module.ts:55-65 (readBlob), theia/extensions/orca-workspace/src/browser/orca-git-resource.ts:28-34 (readContents)`
- **Scope:** linux product (the Theia frontend's Orca editor routing and the pb-orca-git backend; the same code ships on every OS)
- **Full finding and suggested fix:** `.planning/workstreams/orca/phases/65-orca-workspace-in-theia/65-REVIEW.md`, section CR-01

**Verifier.** I traced every step from source on disk.

1. Routing. diffSides (orca-editor-routes.ts:136-153) turns only a null object id into an empty side. The staged rule sends left = HEAD oid and right = ':0'. The branch rule sends mergeBase and headOid, and the commit rule sends parentOid and commitOid. Every one of these is a commit or index spec that has nothing to do with the individual file. The unstaged default sends left = ':0' unless area === 'untracked'. That area is passed only for combined diffs (line 189 passes `undefined` for a single diff).

2. Orca's inputs. openUncommittedEntry (orca/.../open-uncommitted-entry.ts:21-35) sends every untracked or added file that is not markdown to openDiff(..., staged=false). open-unstaged-diff.ts:34 then sets diffSource 'unstaged'. The overlay's openFileRecords (orca-overlay/.../pb-workspace-api.ts:104-140) copies diffSource and the commit-level branchCompare/commitCompare oids. For a single diff it carries no area, no file status and no per-file blob id. Orca does not send a null oid for an added or deleted path. It sends null only for an unborn HEAD, a missing merge base or a root commit. So the counter-evidence named in the task does not hold.

3. Backend. readBlob short-circuits only rev === ''. Otherwise it runs `git cat-file blob -- <rev>:<path>` through execFile. When the path is absent at that tree or index, git exits 128, runGit resolves {reason:'git-failed'}, and readBlob returns {ok:false}. OrcaGitResource.readContents then throws a plain Error.

4. Theia. node_modules/@theia/monaco/lib/browser/monaco-editor-model.js:371-376 converts only ResourceError.NotFound and rethrows anything else. resolveModel is created at line 83, so a rethrow rejects it, which rejects load() in monaco-text-model-service.js:83 and createModelReference. MonacoEditorProvider.getModel (monaco-editor-provider.js:71-82), used for both diff sides at lines 208 and 371, therefore rejects, and the open rejects. A NotFound would not rescue this either: it leaves the model invalid, and getModel then throws MODEL_IS_INVALID at line 79. The proposed fix as written would therefore probably not work. An empty-string side, which readBlob already supports, would.

5. Router. openRecord rejects. apply (lines 297-301) catches the rejection, logs it once and stores {signature, uri: undefined}. The signature does not change, so the record is never retried.

6. Other paths. Nothing else prevents the failure. The resolver passes the spec unchanged, and no wrapper maps git failures to an empty side. Per D-01, Orca's own editor is not mounted.

7. Recorded evidence. Nothing contradicts the finding. verify-orca-editor-routes.mjs (lines 346-359) tests only the null-oid empty sides (unborn HEAD, null merge base, root commit), combined untracked and both_added. It has no single-diff case for an added, deleted or untracked file. The fixture in verify-orca-workspace-live.mjs (buildFixtureRepo, lines 194-209) creates only modified files: a.txt and s.txt in the working tree and index, b.txt on the branch, conflict.txt in the commit. The 65-VERIFICATION and STATE records mention no such case.

Severity: medium, not high. Diffs of modified files work. Added, deleted and untracked files fail visibly: nothing opens, and the path cannot be retried for the rest of the session.

*Checked at:* theia/node_modules/@theia/monaco/lib/browser/monaco-editor-model.js:371-376 rethrows any error that is not NotFound. monaco-editor-provider.js:71-80,208 rejects the diff when either side's model fails. orca/src/renderer/src/components/right-sidebar/source-control/listing/open-uncommitted-entry.ts:33 together with open-unstaged-diff.ts:34 gives an untracked file diffSource 'unstaged' with no area. scripts/verify-orca-workspace-live.mjs:194-209 plants only modified files.

#### 66-CR-02: Every pageMetadata call is refused by orcad's schema, and the failure is swallowed

- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Severity:** medium (reported as critical)
- **Location:** `theia/extensions/orca-browser/src/browser/orca-browser-frontend-module.ts:73, 441-452; orca-overlay/files/src/renderer/src/pb/browser-host/pb-browser-host.ts:201-217`
- **Scope:** linux product (also every platform: Theia orca-browser extension plus the Orca thin host bundle talking to orcad)
- **Full finding and suggested fix:** `.planning/workstreams/orca/phases/66-powerbrowser-web-tabs-as-orca-s-browser/66-REVIEW.md`, section CR-02

**Verifier.** I traced every step from source on disk.
(1) reportMetadata (frontend-module.ts:446) builds only { url, title }. HostSession.pageMetadata at line 73 declares only those two fields. reportMetadata is the only caller of pageMetadata in theia/ or orca-overlay/; it is wired at line 205 from the channel's 'state' push. Line 450 swallows any rejection with .catch(() => undefined).
(2) The host bundle (pb-browser-host.ts:206-216) forwards metadata.loading, canGoBack and canGoForward unchanged. All three are undefined, so JSON.stringify in send() (line 163) drops them from the sealed frame. Its own BrowserHostPageMetadata type (lines 38-44) declares them as required booleans, but the Theia side calls it through its own narrower interface, so the type checker never sees the mismatch.
(3) Upstream schema: orca/src/shared/browser-client-page-metadata-protocol.ts:8-19 declares loading, canGoBack and canGoForward as bare z.boolean(). They are required, not optional and not defaulted. url and title are capped at max(8192) and max(4096). The method is declared with params: BrowserClientPageMetadataParams at orca/src/main/runtime/rpc/methods/browser-client-host.ts:195-198. dispatcher-request-parsing.ts:19 runs safeParse before the handler and returns invalidArgumentResponse on failure. getCompiledSchema uses zod 4.6.7's compile(), which only speeds up the runtime parser and does not loosen it. So every call fails: frame.ok is false, call() throws, and the catch swallows it.
(4) Effect: updatePageMetadata (runtime-browser-page-registry.ts:105) is the only path that writes page url and title after creation. The only other writers are creation/opening and recovery, through browser-client-page-opening.ts:113 and runtime-browser-client-page-recovery.ts:203. browser-tab-catalog.ts reads this registry for the tab list, so tabList, tabCurrent and tabShow keep reporting the creation URL and title.
(5) No recorded result contradicts this. The orca-browser-agent-live clauses do not read metadata from orcad's table. (b) and (f) check the title in the goto command's own result. (h) checks that the page is gone from tab list. (j) checks activation. None of them asserts a url or title that only pageMetadata could set. The project's own records match the defect: STATE.md:176 says "the store row's title stays 'New Tab' (page metadata does not update it)". 66-07-SUMMARY.md:224-226 claims "the host's pageMetadata updates orcad", and the schema shows that claim is wrong.
Severity: this is not a security issue. Agents driving tabs still work; the agent's tab list and current tab carry a stale URL and title, and the error is never logged. That is a user-visible defect under normal use, so medium rather than critical.

*Checked at:* orca/src/shared/browser-client-page-metadata-protocol.ts:16-18 (loading/canGoBack/canGoForward: z.boolean(), required); orca/src/main/runtime/rpc/dispatcher-request-parsing.ts:19-24 (safeParse then invalidArgumentResponse); theia/extensions/orca-browser/src/browser/orca-browser-frontend-module.ts:446,450 (only url/title sent; rejection swallowed); STATE.md:176 records the title staying "New Tab"

#### 66-CR-03: Re-attach with any bound agent tab is refused by Orca's attach schema, so the browser host cannot recover

- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Severity:** medium (reported as critical)
- **Location:** `theia/extensions/orca-browser/src/browser/orca-browser-frontend-module.ts:232, 241-246, 464-483`
- **Scope:** linux product (all platforms running the Orca browser host)
- **Full finding and suggested fix:** `.planning/workstreams/orca/phases/66-powerbrowser-web-tabs-as-orca-s-browser/66-REVIEW.md`, section CR-03

**Verifier.** I traced every step from source.
(1) Each run() mints a new id `pb-host-${crypto.randomUUID()}` (line 232). onDetached (241-246) clears only this.session and the placement. this.bound and pages.table are not touched. The only code that drops table entries is onTabClosed (line 407) and the dispatcher's closePage (orca-browser-dispatch.ts:140). No detach path, Phase 67 path or chrome path closes bound agent tabs.
(2) inventory() (464-483) copies browserHostClientId from the stored createPage event. That event is built by orcad's BrowserHostCommandLedger (orca/src/main/runtime/browser-host/browser-host-command-ledger.ts:124-133) by spreading `...this.authority`. The authority is the lease's BrowserHostLeaseAuthority, which carries the browserHostClientId of the host that held the lease when the page was created. So after a detach, every entry carries the previous session's id.
(3) The host bundle sends `pageInventory: options.inventory?.() ?? []` with pageInventoryProtocolVersion 1 (pb-browser-host.ts:264-275). Orca's BrowserClientHostAttachParams.superRefine (orca/src/shared/browser-client-host-protocol.ts:166-174) adds an issue for each entry whose browserHostClientId differs from params.browserHostClientId.
(4) The attach method declares `params: BrowserClientHostAttachParams` (rpc/methods/browser-client-host.ts:21-23). dispatcher-request-parsing.ts:19-23 runs safeParse through getCompiledSchema and returns invalidArgumentResponse on failure. getCompiledSchema uses zod 4.6.7 compile(). In node_modules/zod/v4/core/compile.js:520-536, compile() keeps superRefine check functions; it does not drop them. The reply is therefore ok:false.
(5) onAttachFrame (pb-browser-host.ts:232) calls end('attach_refused:...'). That runs onDetached and rejects the promise. run() catches it and calls setState('failed').
(6) runReconnect (orca-hosting-status.ts:180-183) and its re-pair branch (208-210) call browserHost.attach(), which goes through the same path. closeEndedPages only handles pages the table no longer binds (line 416), and it runs only after a successful attach, so it cannot help.
No overlay patch changes the schema: orca-overlay/patches has only 010, 070 and 080, none touching browser-client-host.
Recorded evidence: the Phase 67 live runs (run6/8/9, 67-VERIFICATION.md:92) show recovery, but verify-orca-failure-page-live.mjs never opens agent tabs. Those runs do not cover this precondition and do not contradict the finding.
Severity is medium, not high. The failure needs at least one agent tab open when the detach happens. Two workarounds exist: close every agent tab (onTabClosed drops it from the table), or reload the frontend. Either way the user sees a browser-host failure page that Reconnect does not clear.

*Checked at:* orca/src/shared/browser-client-host-protocol.ts:166-174 (superRefine id mismatch); orca/src/main/runtime/browser-host/browser-host-command-ledger.ts:124-133 (event carries lease authority id); orca/src/main/runtime/rpc/dispatcher-request-parsing.ts:19-23 (invalid_argument reply); orca-overlay/files/src/renderer/src/pb/browser-host/pb-browser-host.ts:232,273 (attach_refused, inventory sent)

#### 66-CR-04: Every automation call is capped at the bridge's 5 s acknowledgement timeout and reported as browser_error

- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Severity:** medium (reported as critical)
- **Location:** `theia/extensions/orca-browser/src/browser/orca-browser-frontend-module.ts:386; theia/extensions/tab-uris/src/browser/web-tab.ts:702-725`
- **Scope:** linux product (all platforms that run the Orca browser host: Theia frontend to chrome bridge)
- **Full finding and suggested fix:** `.planning/workstreams/orca/phases/66-powerbrowser-web-tabs-as-orca-s-browser/66-REVIEW.md`, section CR-04

**Verifier.** I traced the whole path from source.

1. The cited code exists and does what the finding says. `agentCall` at orca-browser-frontend-module.ts:386 calls `this.channel.request({ kind: 'webTabAgent', ... })` with no timeout argument. `WebTabChannel.request` (web-tab.ts:702-725) arms `window.setTimeout(() => settle({ ok: false, reason: 'timeout' }), WEB_TAB_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS)`, and that constant is 5000 (web-tab.ts:44). `settle` removes the GROUP_RESPONSE_EVENT listener, so a later reply is dropped. `outcomeOf` (orca-browser-dispatch.ts:79-81) maps any `ok !== true` reply, timeout included, to `failed('browser_error')`. `dispatchCommand` (:160) sends that outcome back to Orca through `session.commandResult`.

2. There is no separate long-running path and no early acknowledgement. GroupActorChild.sys.mjs:46-79 forwards the request with a single `sendQuery("PowerBrowserGroupMutation", msg)` and calls `sendResponse` only when that query's promise settles. On the parent side, PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:4556 routes the message to `handleWebTabAction`, which goes to `handleWebTabAgent` (:5591, :11746-11795). That function awaits the full method before it returns: the native row, or `agentRunInChild` (`actor.sendQuery(AGENT_RUN_MESSAGE)` raced only against a tab-close or Stop abort). For child rows it then waits in `agentSettleOpens`, a 100 ms beat and up to 3000 ms more while a document loads. So one reply arrives only after the whole command finishes.

Chrome's own deadlines are longer than 5 s:
- `browser.wait` uses `params.timeout`, or 30000 ms when none is given (GroupActorChild.sys.mjs:612).
- `agentPageEval` defaults to 30000 ms (:393).

Orca expects long waits on this hop. orca/src/shared/browser-wait-timeout.ts sets `BROWSER_WAIT_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS = 25_000` and gives forwarding hops `max(60_000, timeout + 30_000)`. So any `orca wait` whose condition is met after 5 s, or an eval whose promise resolves after 5 s, is answered `browser_error` while chrome keeps running the command.

3. No other path prevents the failure. There is no subclass or override of `request`, and no per-call timeout parameter. `agentCall` is the only route for automation methods.

4. No recorded result contradicts this. The live harness drives the same `WebTabChannel.request` (scripts/lib/orca-browser-live.mjs:208, `agentRaw`), but every agent-live clause in scripts/verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs is a short call: evals of `location.pathname` or `window.open`, clicks, and the like. None waits past 5 s. The PASS results in 66-VERIFICATION.md, coord-6609, do not exercise this case.

One part of the finding is inaccurate: "browser_timeout is never produced". When chrome's own deadline is under 5 s (for example `wait` with `timeout: 2000`), the child throws `AgentRowError("browser_timeout")` (GroupActorChild.sys.mjs:380), and that code passes through `outcomeOf`'s 'failed' branch. The local 5 s timeout itself does map to `browser_error`, and the core defect stands.

Severity is medium, not high. Short commands work, and long waits, slow evals and large screenshots or PDFs fail with the wrong error code.

*Checked at:* web-tab.ts:44 (WEB_TAB_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS = 5000) and :721; GroupActorChild.sys.mjs:46-79 (one reply, sent after sendQuery resolves) and :612 (wait default 30 s); PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11746-11795 (handleWebTabAgent awaits the full method and the settle step); orca-browser-dispatch.ts:79-81; orca/src/shared/browser-wait-timeout.ts (Orca's default wait is 25 s); no live clause in verify-orca-browser-agent-live.mjs waits past 5 s

#### 67-CR-01: Automatic re-pair never runs after Restart Orca, and the page hides while orcad is still starting

- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Severity:** medium (reported as critical)
- **Location:** `theia/extensions/orca-panes/src/browser/failure/orca-hosting-status.ts:167-169, 262-270, 287-297`
- **Scope:** linux product (all platforms: frontend hosting status and chrome OrcaService)
- **Full finding and suggested fix:** `.planning/workstreams/orca/phases/67-hosting-hardening/67-REVIEW.md`, section CR-01

**Verifier.** I traced the state sequence on disk.

Chrome side:
- OrcaService.restart() (OrcaService.sys.mjs:156-167) runs only from the failed state. It sets _state="starting", _restarting=false and _failureRows=[], then starts _startSequence() without awaiting it and returns true.
- handleOrcaShellMessage (PowerBrowserAPI.sys.mjs:11888-11891) replies {accepted} at once.
- OrcaEnv.failureClassOf (OrcaEnv.sys.mjs:504-519) returns null when restarting is false and state is "starting". It returns a class only for restarting===true, for failed, and for ready with a pairing or browser-host reason.
- The state becomes "ready" only at the end of _spawnAndGate (OrcaService.sys.mjs:531). That is after a Node spawn and orcad's ready line, so it takes well over one poll round trip.

Frontend side:
- restart() runs send(orcaRestart) and then poll(). That poll's take() sees state "starting" with failureClass null.
- In take() (line 288), `recovered` needs before∈REPAIR_AFTER, next.state==='ready' and next.failureClass===null. Here next.state is "starting", so it is false.
- this.chrome becomes {starting, null}. failureClass therefore goes to null (held is null, clientReason is unset), and orca-pane-widget.tsx:108-111 unmounts OrcaFailurePage. Its effect cleanup calls hide() (orca-failure-page.tsx:31).
- schedule() (line 263) then stops the interval, because pages is empty and bootSettled is true.
- The next sample comes only from onClientStatus (line 255-256, transport not ready). By then `before` is null, so when ready arrives `recovered` stays false and reconnect() never runs.
- repair() has no other caller (grep: only pb-session-pairing.ts:107, reached from runReconnect).

So the page hides while orcad is still starting, and the automatic re-pair is skipped.

Consequence: recorded live evidence shows the old client is refused by a restarted orcad. Its session device row died with the old orcad. See 67-07-SUMMARY.md:59-70 and D-36: the refusal comes 6-10 s later as verification blocked / access-revoked. onClientStatus then reports 'unauthorized', and the user gets a pairing-lost page and must press Reconnect.

Precondition: the pane bundle booted against an earlier orcad. orcad then crashed, and _supervise → _launch ended in _fail. That happens after 5 attempts or on the non-recoverable pinned-port or lock-held code (OrcaService.sys.mjs:385-397, 480-486), giving could-not-start or port-in-use. These are the only classes with a Restart button (orca-failure-copy.ts:66-75).

Counter-evidence checked:
- The orca-failure-page-live port-in-use clause (scripts/verify-orca-failure-page-live.mjs:466-483) presses Restart Orca and requires the content back within 60 s, and it passed in run6/8/9 (67-VERIFICATION row 1). But it induces port-in-use at relaunch, so the bundle has never booted. bootSettled stays false, polling continues, and the loader's own hand-off loop pairs with the new offer. No re-pair is needed there, so the pass does not exercise this path.
- That clause checks only that the page is gone and content is back. It does not check that the page stays up until ready, so it does not contradict claim 1 either.

The only narrow exception to "always": a timer poll already in flight when the restart reply arrives is shared and returns the pre-restart failed sample. Then, if orcad became ready before the next 1 s tick, the re-pair would fire. That is unlikely given the spawn and ready-line cost.

Severity: the result is user-visible and recoverable by Reconnect, under real but secondary preconditions (a crash followed by a failed respawn). That is medium on this scale, not critical: there is no security boundary, and the feature is not dead for all users.

*Checked at:* OrcaService.sys.mjs:161-165 (state starting, restarting false); OrcaEnv.sys.mjs:504-519 (null for starting); orca-hosting-status.ts:288 (recovered requires before in REPAIR_AFTER and next ready); orca-pane-widget.tsx:108-111 + orca-failure-page.tsx:28-32 (unmount → hide); orca-hosting-status.ts:263 (poll stops); 67-07-SUMMARY.md:59-70 and DECISIONS.md D-36 (the restarted orcad refuses the old client without a re-pair); verify-orca-failure-page-live.mjs:466-483 (the Restart clause runs only on a first-launch failure with an unbooted bundle, so it does not contradict)

#### 68-CR-01: Every hosted re-pin replay stops at "row licence-allowlist skipped" because the uBlock XPI is never cached on the runner

- **Verdict:** CONFIRMED
- **Severity:** medium (reported as critical)
- **Location:** `scripts/rebase-orca.sh:55,67-69,218-227; scripts/verify-licence-allowlist.mjs:273-278,303,508; .github/workflows/rebase-orca.yml:77-106`
- **Scope:** ci
- **Full finding and suggested fix:** `.planning/workstreams/orca/phases/68-three-os-artifacts-weekly-re-pin-and-upstream-seams/68-REVIEW.md`, section CR-01

**Verifier.** I traced the whole path from source.

1. **What the row does.** rebase-orca.sh puts licence-allowlist in ROWS (:50). stage_rows (:218-227) runs `verify-platform.sh --only <label>` through capture, which merges stderr into stdout. It then calls skip_line(OUT), which matches any line containing the word SKIP. The only exemption is the regex `^licence-allowlist: SKIP -- upstream/ absent`. Any other SKIP line stops the replay with `red "row $label skipped: ..."`.

2. **verify-platform.sh passes the output through unchanged.** The licence-allowlist row (:3886) is `node $REPO_ROOT/scripts/verify-licence-allowlist.mjs`. The run loop (:6963-6990) runs it with `setsid $cmd &` and inherited stdio, so the child's lines reach capture as they are. The row's PASS/FAIL depends only on the exit status.

3. **The script emits a SKIP for a missing cache.** In default mode (:508), verify-licence-allowlist.mjs calls `bundledCheck(allowList, bundledInputs(REPO_ROOT), cachedXpiFirstLine...)`.
   - bundledInputs reads powerbrowser/packaging/bundled-xpi.json, which pins uBlock0@raymondhill.net 1.76.0.
   - inventory/licence-allowlist.json:65-73 has a gecko-distribution row for that id.
   - cachedXpiFirstLine (:273-278) returns null when `.mozbuild/bundled-xpi/uBlock0@raymondhill.net-1.76.0.xpi` does not exist.
   - bundledCheck (:303) then pushes `licence-allowlist: SKIP -- uBlock0@raymondhill.net 1.76.0 is not cached in .mozbuild/bundled-xpi/; ...` (NAME is 'licence-allowlist', :58).
   - render() prints that line and exits 0, because a SKIP is not a FAIL. The line does not match SKIP_OUT_OF_SCOPE, so skip_line returns it.

4. **Nothing stages the cache on the hosted runner.**
   - .gitignore:42 ignores `.mozbuild/`, and git ls-files shows nothing under it, so the actions/checkout workspace starts without it.
   - rebase-orca.yml has five steps before the replay: checkout, free disk, install Nix, brand scan, then rebase-orca.sh. None of them fetches or stages the XPI.
   - In rebase-orca.sh, run_replay full mode (:269-281) runs pin-move, fetch, seam-apply, build, three typechecks, coverage, then rows. None of these stages the XPI.
   - A repo-wide grep finds only package-linux.sh, package-windows.sh and package-macos.sh calling stage-bundled-xpi.sh (the only writer of the cache, which downloads with curl). Neither build-orca.sh, fetch-orca.sh nor the flake references it.

5. **The recorded evidence does not contradict this.** STATE.md:162 (da97a4e9, 2026-11-18) records a local identity re-pin PASS with "6 rows green". That run was on the owner's host, and this worktree has `.mozbuild/bundled-xpi/uBlock0@raymondhill.net-1.76.0.xpi` (dated Nov 11, after 68-08's d8643e27 on Nov 10). That cached file is why the local run did not SKIP. No hosted rebase-orca.yml run is recorded anywhere. 68-VERIFICATION.md:22/:147 say the hosted path is "not yet run live". The self-test fixture never produces the bundled SKIP line, so its PASS proves nothing about this path.

6. **One nuance.** On a hosted run, a stage before stage_rows, or one of the earlier rows (orca-build-shape … orca-mobile-protocol), could fail first. In that case the run would stop there instead. Either way, a hosted replay can never reach "replayed" while this row behaves as described. So the claim holds: the automatic re-pin (OVL-02) cannot succeed on the runner.

7. **Severity: medium.** A CI/release-train path fails closed: it opens a stop issue and no bump PR. Nothing ships wrongly and no boundary is crossed.

8. **Owner decision: none applies.** D-59 moves the first weekly re-pin tick to the next version. That postpones the run that would expose the defect, but it neither accepts nor defers the defect itself.

*Checked at:* scripts/verify-licence-allowlist.mjs:274-275 (null when cache file absent) and :303 (SKIP push), :508 (default-mode call); scripts/rebase-orca.sh:55 (only exemption is upstream/), :224-225 (SKIP -> FAIL); .github/workflows/rebase-orca.yml:77-106 (no XPI staging step); .gitignore:42 (.mozbuild/ ignored); only callers of stage-bundled-xpi.sh are powerbrowser/packaging/package-{linux,windows,macos}.sh; local PASS at .planning/workstreams/orca/STATE.md:162 explained by the existing local cache file .mozbuild/bundled-xpi/uBlock0@raymondhill.net-1.76.0.xpi

#### 68-CR-02: The Windows sidecar build always fails: download-plugins.mjs now runs spawnSync('npm', ...) on windows-latest

- **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE
- **Severity:** medium (reported as critical)
- **Location:** `scripts/download-plugins.mjs:127-129 (also :241 in pack(), unreached here)`
- **Scope:** windows build (release.yml windows-sidecar -> build-windows -> sign-windows); Linux product unaffected
- **Owner decision cited:** D-40
- **Full finding and suggested fix:** `.planning/workstreams/orca/phases/68-three-os-artifacts-weekly-re-pin-and-upstream-seams/68-REVIEW.md`, section CR-02

**Verifier.** (1) The cited code exists as described. Line 127 runs spawnSync('npm', ['ci', '--ignore-scripts', ...], { cwd: scratch, encoding: 'utf8' }) with no shell option. Line 129 throws when r.status !== 0, using r.stderr || r.error?.message. The catch at :150-152 calls die(), which does process.exit(1). The loop goes over every bundled-extensions/<folder> that has a package.json. bundled-extensions/claude-agent-sdk/ is tracked and has one, so the spawn always happens. Commit 48b41481 (68-09) added it.

(2) How the build gets there: release.yml windows-sidecar has runs-on: windows-latest (:265) and runs `cd theia; yarn build` (:319-325). theia/package.json `build` runs `yarn --cwd applications/browser build`. That script is `yarn -s rebuild && yarn -s download:plugins && theia build`, and download:plugins is `node ../../../scripts/download-plugins.mjs`. theia/applications/browser/package.json has no theiaPlugins block, and configuration.toml has no [[extensions]]. So `declared` is empty, and the stock `.bin/theia` spawn (:70) and pack() (:241) are never reached. The review's claim that only the new spawn is unconditional is correct. `defaults.run.shell: bash` has no effect: spawnSync without `shell` goes straight to libuv's CreateProcess path, not through bash. Node's spawnSync does not throw on ENOENT. It returns status null with `error` set, so line 129 throws and die() exits 1. yarn build then fails, and so does the windows-sidecar job.

(3) Nothing else prevents the failure. No other configuration, environment variable or build step bypasses the loop. On Windows, `existsSync(bundledRoot)` is true for the checked-out tree.

(4) No recorded result contradicts the finding. No hosted Windows run is on record: 68-10-SUMMARY.md:289 says "hosted Windows runs are shape-checked only, first real run deferred". Upstream Orca's own config/scripts/pnpm-cli-invocation.mjs corroborates the Windows behaviour: on win32 it falls back to command 'pnpm.cmd' with shell: true, which is the handling an extensionless spawn needs.

The step I cannot settle from source on disk: libuv's Windows search_path (src/win/process.c). It tries a name without an extension only as <name>.com and <name>.exe, and Node (since the CVE-2024-27980 fix) refuses .cmd/.bat without shell. Together these mean that no `npm.com` or `npm.exe` is on the windows-latest PATH, and so the spawn returns ENOENT. Only libuv headers are in /nix/store, not the Windows process.c source. The runner image's PATH contents are also runtime state. Both points are well documented and consistent with Orca's upstream workaround, but neither can be proven from files on disk.

Severity: medium. This is a CI/build path that fails, and only the Windows artifact path is affected. The windows-sidecar job feeds only build-windows ("a failure here never reaches a Linux job", release.yml:263), and D-40 removes Windows from v1.7's supported platforms, so this is not high.

D-40 covers this defect. It moves "the macOS and Windows halves of PLAT-01 and PLAT-02" to the next version, naming build-windows, which needs windows-sidecar. The defect is therefore deferred, not refuted. The fix (run npm-cli.js through process.execPath, at :127 and :241) is still owed before the first real Windows run.

*Checked at:* scripts/download-plugins.mjs:127-129,150-152 (unconditional for bundled-extensions/claude-agent-sdk/package.json); .github/workflows/release.yml:265,319-325; theia/applications/browser/package.json:110-111 (no theiaPlugins block); 68-10-SUMMARY.md:289 (no hosted Windows run recorded); orca/config/scripts/pnpm-cli-invocation.mjs (upstream uses pnpm.cmd + shell on win32)

#### 68-CR-03: The macOS Orca trees lose their executable bits in the artifact round trip, and the universal merge copies the lost mode

- **Verdict:** PLAUSIBLE
- **Severity:** low (reported as critical)
- **Location:** `.github/workflows/release.yml:2181-2189,2296-2304 (upload), :2384-2400 (download/admit/merge); powerbrowser/packaging/package-macos.sh:70,75,81 (orca_universal), :405-406 (per-arch staging)`
- **Scope:** macos build
- **Owner decision cited:** D-40
- **Full finding and suggested fix:** `.planning/workstreams/orca/phases/68-three-os-artifacts-weekly-re-pin-and-upstream-seams/68-REVIEW.md`, section CR-03

**Verifier.** (1) The cited code is as described. Both thin jobs upload the loose directory `$RUNNER_TEMP/macos-orca-<arch>/` with actions/upload-artifact v7.0.2 (release.yml:2182-2189, 2297-2304). No step tars or zips the tree first, and no step writes a mode list. macos-orca-universal downloads both trees with download-artifact v8.0.2 and admits them with `check-win-sidecar.sh check`, which compares only the SHA256SUMS digest and runs `sha256sum -c` (check-win-sidecar.sh:32,58). It then merges them. In orca_universal, `cp -a` copies the downloaded modes (:70). `chmod --reference` copies the arm or out mode onto each lipo output (:75, :81), and `cp -p` copies it to the paired name. Neither step sets 0755. The per-arch path admits PB_MAC_ORCA_DIR, which the usage text at :349 calls the downloaded macos-orca-<arch> artifact, and stages it with `cp -a` (:405-406). Nothing restores executable bits on either path. The only chmod is `chmod 755` on the Node binary (:398). verify-orca-packaged-tree.mjs and verify-package-target-natives.mjs never check modes.
(2) Third-party behaviour. The step I cannot settle from disk is the premise that upload-artifact v7.0.2 plus download-artifact v8.0.2 extract every file as 0644. Neither action's source is on disk. Only orca/node_modules/@actions/expressions exists, and no @actions/artifact copy was found. The README text the finding quotes ("File permissions are not maintained during artifact upload") matches the documented v4+ behaviour, but I could not read it from source. The downstream effects I did read from source. node-pty 1.1.0 passes `native.dir + '/spawn-helper'` as argv[0] to posix_spawn (lib/unixTerminal.js:29,96; src/unix/pty.cc:414,424). Orca resolves the packaged ripgrep by path, with no chmod and no PATH fallback when packaged (src/main/ripgrep/bundled-ripgrep-path.ts:25-47).
(3) Mitigations the finding misses. Orca chmods two of the three binaries at runtime. `preflightPtySpawn` and `preflightPtySpawnHealth` call `ensureNodePtySpawnHelperExecutable()` (orca/src/main/daemon/pty-subprocess/spawn-preflight.ts:126,243). That function does `chmodSync(candidate, mode | 0o755)` on spawn-helper when its mode has no x bit (src/main/providers/local-pty/local-pty-executable-spawn.ts:71-95). agent-browser's binary is chmodded 0755 on an access failure (src/main/browser/agent-browser/agent-browser-dependency-binary.ts:4-12). So "cannot spawn a PTY" and "cannot start agent-browser" hold only when the bundle files are not writable by the user, for example a root-owned install or App Translocation onto a read-only mount. That is runtime behaviour I cannot settle statically. Ripgrep has no such repair, so file search and listing would fail with EACCES if the modes are lost.
(4) No recorded live or gate result contradicts the finding. The macOS jobs have never run. WINDOWS 48 records that no build-macos job exists, that the macOS Gecko build is blocked, and that the macOS jobs are only shape-checked. The universal output is not uploaded or consumed, so in this milestone no user artifact carries the defect.
Severity: v1.7 ships Linux only. No macOS bundle job exists. The failure is latent in a code path for the next version, so I adjusted it to low from the review's critical. The fix is still needed before the macOS half lands.
D-40 covers this defect. D-40 moves the macOS Orca jobs, the macOS artifact and WINDOWS 48 to the next version and keeps their code in the tree. That defers the defect without refuting it.

*Checked at:* release.yml:2182-2189 and 2297-2304 (loose upload, no tar); check-win-sidecar.sh:58 (hash-only admission); package-macos.sh:70,75,81,405-406 (modes copied, never set). Partial runtime guards: orca/src/main/daemon/pty-subprocess/spawn-preflight.ts:126,243 calls ensureNodePtySpawnHelperExecutable (local-pty-executable-spawn.ts:71-95), and agent-browser-dependency-binary.ts:4-12 chmods 0755. No guard for ripgrep (bundled-ripgrep-path.ts:25-47). WINDOWS.md row 48: macOS jobs never run, no build-macos job.

This pass verifies declared mitigations; it does not search for new threats (layer 1 does). `threats_open` in each SECURITY.md counts open threats at high or above, as the GSD template defines it.
